# Bug hunt 2026-08-21 (full-repo sweep + design review)

Fifth fleet hunt, the first run as a single orchestrated workflow: 14 module-scoped
hunters (every tracked source file in the repo, one territory each), each batch
adversarially verified by a second agent told to refute, plus a separate fleet of
6 macro reviewers looking at the approach and design rather than lines. Hunters
were briefed on CLAUDE.md, SPEC.md, the KNOWN_BUGS ledger (through CR-45) and the
08-11 / 08-14 hunt archives, so nothing below re-reports a resolved item. The
working tree at hunt time was clean at `f27c181` (companion 0.9.43, dashboard
0.7.4). 35 agents, ~6.2M tokens, 28 minutes. Nothing in this document has been
fixed yet: it is the input to a fix pass, not the record of one.

**Tally: 78 findings, 76 CONFIRMED (12 high / 35 medium / 29 low), 2 plausible,
0 refuted.** Design review: 53 issues (1 critical / 12 high / 27 medium / 13 low).
Every suite the hunters ran was green, which says more about what the suites do
not cover than about the code (see each area's coverage note).

## Orchestrator's synthesis: what to fix first

The individual findings cluster into a handful of themes. The themes, not the
count, are what matter.

1. **CR-45's [ RESUME ] is not safe yet, in two independent ways.** The breaker's
   `resume()` never resets the remote-listing baseline, so a shrink/empty trip
   re-trips on the identical listing forever (comp-lanes-ab-1, reproduced). And
   the dashboard's resume request is a STANDING order cleared only by a report
   that shows the breaker clear; the companion posts no report on resume, so a
   pass that re-trips inside the 60 s window gets resumed again on the next
   reply: 100 more proxies into trash per cycle from one admin click
   (comp-lanes-ab-2). Together these re-open exactly the unbounded-sequence
   failure the breaker exists to stop. Fix both before the next companion ship.
2. **Per-editor (`cce1`) tokens break three fleet surfaces** the moment the
   shared token is retired, which the dashboard's own boot warning tells the
   operator to do: music ingest 403s (music-1), ytdl requester-first claim
   401/403s (ytdl-web-1), and `/api/v1/verify` still hands out the retired
   shared token (dash-core-2); `/fleet/halt` has the inverse bug (dash-core-5).
   One verifier callback installed by the dashboard mounts (the way
   `ai_backend.set_provider_lookup` already works) fixes the first two.
3. **The b-roll drag-and-drop ingest cannot survive anything going wrong**: a
   cancel kills the upload queue for the rest of the session (comp-loopback-1),
   a tray restart strands the batch with no re-claim and no heartbeat
   (comp-loopback-2), a failed rclone upload parks the item forever
   (comp-loopback-3), and a refused `/result` goes live with no segments
   (comp-loopback-4). Each ends in 409 "already indexing another batch" for
   every later drop. This is a feature shipped three days ago; fix as a set.
4. **Per-machine plans have two data-model holes**: the first own-row for a
   machine that was inheriting the unassigned bucket silently drops every
   inherited project and enforce unshares them (dash-core-1, reproduced on a
   scratch DB), and a mixed wired+remote account re-creates CR-28 because the
   role gates are per PERSON while the rows are per MACHINE (dash-admin-8,
   data-model-1, data-model-2). "Remove from this machine" is machine-scoped
   in the tray and person-scoped on the wire (comp-lane-c-2, sync-safety-3).
5. **Two sources of truth for site settings.** The Settings page and wizard
   write the DB and publish it to companions, but the dashboard's own `/ytdl`
   mount, brand, create-project template and shared-asset list still read only
   the environment (dash-release-ai-1, product-surface-1, product-surface-2,
   dash-admin-3). On an appliance, where compose sets no `DASH_SITE_*`, the
   wizard's answers reach every companion and not the dashboard itself.
6. **The release pipeline has a critical clobber**: `publish_feed.py` rebuilds
   the channel from the gitignored local `feed/` dir and uploads with
   `--clobber`, so running it from any other machine replaces the live vendor
   channel with a one-record document (release-pipeline-1). Beside it: two
   unreconciled publish paths that already disagree (release-pipeline-2), ship
   unaware of image mode (release-pipeline-3), no installer in the feed
   (release-pipeline-4), no `current` pointer or retraction
   (release-pipeline-5), and a signed `min_version > version` record that
   would raise every machine's floor past the offer (comp-app-core-3,
   dash-release-ai-3: the same defect found from both ends).
7. **Stuck-forever states with no admin exit**: an interrupted dashboard OTA
   leaves `in_progress=true` and 409s every apply and rollback
   (dash-release-ai-2); `publish_db --rollback` cannot list the `.prev` it made
   (server-1); the Setup wizard's storage check writes inside the container and
   can never go green (dash-admin-1); the appliance compose mints three
   different internal tokens (dash-admin-2); local-auth mode lets the last
   admin disable themselves (dash-admin-5) and first-admin bootstrap is
   unreachable from a browser (dash-admin-4).
8. **Trust-model seams**: identity tokens are unrevocable 30-day bearers
   (trust-model-1), disabling an account revokes nothing (trust-model-2,
   dash-core-3), and the default trusted-proxy list puts every Tailscale Serve
   visitor into ONE login-throttle bucket (trust-model-3: five wrong passwords
   by anyone lock the fleet out of /login and /verify for up to an hour).
9. **Lane A resurrects NAS-side reorganisations** (sync-safety-1, high, large):
   `copy --ignore-existing` with no upload ledger re-sends anything moved on
   the NAS from every editor who still holds it at the old path. CR-44 taught
   lane B to tolerate the move; nothing stops lane A from undoing it. A design
   decision, not a patch.
10. **Steady-state cost** (ops-efficiency-1/-2): every machine re-sends its full
    manifest and media tree every 60 s and the server rewrites thousands of
    rows per machine per minute; each ticked project costs three rclone
    processes and two recursive SFTP listings per minute with nothing to move.
    Not urgent, but it is what a slow NAS or a 20-editor fleet will feel first.

Suggested order for a fix pass: themes 1-3 (companion ship-blockers), 4-5 and 7
(dashboard, OTA-deployable), 6 (tooling, before the next feed publish), 8, then
9-10 as designed work.

Companion document: `docs/HOW_IT_WORKS.md`, the customer-facing end-to-end
explainer written in the same run (describes the product as built, not as
planned).


## Index of high-severity findings

- **comp-lanes-ab-1** [CONFIRMED] Breaker resume() never resets the remote-listing baseline, so a shrink/empty trip re-trips on the very next pass (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/lane_guard.py:301`)
- **comp-lanes-ab-2** [CONFIRMED] A standing dashboard resume request auto-clears every subsequent trip until a report happens to land while the breaker is clear (`dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:4485`)
- **comp-loopback-1** [CONFIRMED] A cancelled or 410'd ingest batch leaves the UploadQueue permanently stopped, so every later batch in that companion session sits in 'uploading' forever (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/broll_ingest.py:1996`)
- **comp-loopback-2** [CONFIRMED] A resumed batch is never re-claimed and never heartbeated, and resumed 'uploading' items are never re-queued: a tray restart mid-batch strands the batch and 409s every new one (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/broll_ingest.py:703`)
- **comp-loopback-3** [CONFIRMED] An upload that rclone fails leaves the item in 'uploading' for ever: never retried, never failed, batch never finishes (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/broll_ingest.py:2088`)
- **dash-core-1** [CONFIRMED] A tick for a machine that was inheriting the unassigned bucket silently drops every inherited project from that machine's plan (and enforce unshares them) (`dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/db.py:1886`)
- **dash-release-ai-1** [CONFIRMED] The Settings page's 'YouTube downloader' tick publishes the feature to the fleet but never mounts /ytdl (mount reads env only) (`dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/ytdl.py:257`)
- **dash-release-ai-2** [CONFIRMED] A dashboard code update interrupted by a process kill leaves update_state.json in_progress=true forever; every later apply and rollback 409s with no way to clear it (`dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/dashboard_update.py:453`)
- **server-1** [CONFIRMED] publish_db --rollback cannot find the previous index: newest_prev lists a directory TRUENAS_USER cannot read (`server/publish_db.py:355`)
- **broll-2** [CONFIRMED] `--model fable` with the default `thinking: disabled` sends `{"type": "disabled"}`, which claude-fable-5 rejects with 400 -- and that 400 is classified per-video, so the run marks every clip 'error' (`broll/indexer/broll_index/claude_client.py:528`)
- **music-1** [CONFIRMED] Music fleet ingest 403s for every editor holding a per-editor (cce1) report token (`music/web/musicweb/fleet_auth.py:64`)
- **ytdl-web-1** [CONFIRMED] Fleet routes accept only the shared DASH_REPORT_TOKEN, so the documented move to per-editor tokens silently kills requester-first downloads (`ytdl/web/ytdlweb/routes_fleet.py:83`)

## Index of critical/high design issues

- **sync-safety-1** [high, gap, effort large] Lane A resurrects any folder reorganisation done on the NAS (the reorganisation path SPEC recommends)
- **trust-model-1** [high, gap, effort medium] Companion identity tokens are unrevocable 30-day bearers with no refresh: too long to revoke, too short to live with
- **trust-model-2** [high, gap, effort small] Disabling a local account revokes nothing: sessions, report tokens and identity keep working
- **trust-model-3** [high, fragility, effort small] Default trusted-proxy list does not match the shipped TrueNAS network shape, so the whole fleet shares one login-throttle IP bucket
- **data-model-1** [high, inconsistency, effort small] Wired/remote is per machine in the data but per person in every gate: a mixed-role account re-creates CR-28 and shares folders with a wired machine
- **data-model-2** [high, gap, effort large] The machine's role is still minted from the PERSON (DASH_ADMIN_USERS) and obeyed by the companion and the wizard
- **release-pipeline-1** [critical, fragility, effort medium] publish_feed.py rebuilds the channel from the local, gitignored feed/ dir and clobbers the live one
- **release-pipeline-2** [high, inconsistency, effort medium] Two publish paths for one fleet (ship.cmd PUT vs CI+feed) with no reconciliation; the studio and the feed already disagree
- **release-pipeline-3** [high, inconsistency, effort small] ship.cmd is unaware of image mode: it restarts the live dashboard for nothing and cannot pass its health gate once the dashboard VERSION is bumped
- **release-pipeline-4** [high, gap, effort medium] The installer (kind=onboard) channel is never published through the CI/feed path, so feed-only customers have no installer
- **release-pipeline-5** [high, fragility, effort medium] The feed has no 'current' pointer and no retraction; stage/current policies replay the whole history in append order
- **product-surface-1** [high, inconsistency, effort medium] Feature flag `youtube_download` has two sources of truth: Settings/DB publishes it to companions, but the /ytdl mount reads only the environment
- **product-surface-2** [high, inconsistency, effort medium] The wizard/Settings site manifest is stored and published but the dashboard's own brand, create-project template and asset-sharing still read the environment snapshot

# Part 1: verified findings, by area

Each entry carries the hunter's description and scenario, the suggested fix, and the verifier's reasoning. Severities are the VERIFIER's adjusted ones; where the hunter's differed it is noted.


## Companion: sync lanes A/B (2 high / 1 medium / 2 low)

> **Coverage.** Read end to end: lane_guard.py (breaker, trash prune, halt), rclone_lane.py (constants, filter builders, up/down command builders, RcloneRunTally/parse_json_log, list_remote_top/list_remote_files, RcloneLane.__init__, _build_command/_backup_dir, _run_once_locked, _account_pass/_count_relocations/_trashed_this_pass, resume_after_trip, prune), sequencer.py (_run, _run_pass, _process_project, _run_lanes_a_and_b, pause/resume/trigger), repath.py, canon.py, paths.py, selection.py, root_guard.py (probe_root, access_is_blocked, RootGuard). Followed the CR-45 path across app.py (_apply_resume_lane_b, resume_lane_b, halt_all_sync, _stop_lanes), reporter.py (_select_interval/_report_loop cadence) and dashboard api.py (resume request endpoints, the report-reply commands block, SyncGuardIn) + db.py. Ran the companion suite filtered to this area read-only (772 passed, 0 failed/skipped) and read test_lane_guard.py's CR-44 tests and test_rclone_lane.py's tally test. Verified two behaviours empirically against the bundled rclone 1.74.4 (companion/.tools/rclone.exe) in the scratchpad: (a) the JSON log shape of a --backup-dir move (two records per file: "Moved (server-side)" then "Moved into backup dir", msg never contains the path), and (b) `sync --min-age 120s` against a destination holding an OLD copy of a file the source has a YOUNG copy of: the local copy is moved into the backup dir and not replaced. Reproduced finding 1 with a scratch script against LaneBBreaker. Not covered: express lane A (rclone_express), syncthing_* modules, shared_folders.py, the dashboard UI partial for [ RESUME ], and live behaviour on a real NAS/macOS host.


### comp-lanes-ab-1: Breaker resume() never resets the remote-listing baseline, so a shrink/empty trip re-trips on the very next pass

**HIGH, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/lane_guard.py:301`. Related: CR-45

`LaneBBreaker.resume()` (lane_guard.py:296-313) clears the latch and the deletion counters but leaves `self._remote_counts` untouched, and `check_remote()` (lane_guard.py:355-367) only writes a new baseline on the NON-trip path. So after a trigger-1 trip (listing shrank past 50%, or listed EMPTY where it held N before) the stale `last` stays on disk and the identical listing trips again with the identical sentence, no matter how many times the operator resumes. The docstring on resume() says exactly why this matters ("resuming without clearing them re-trips on the next pass ... reads as 'the button doesn't work'") but only handled the deletion counters. Reproduced with a scratch script: check_remote(scope, 10 names) -> check_remote(scope, 2 names) trips -> resume() True -> check_remote(scope, 2 names) trips again. Combined with CR-45 (finding 2) the dashboard's standing request then re-resumes it on every report, so the fleet page shows a machine flapping tripped/resumed forever.

**Failure scenario.** An editor (or the base rig) deliberately consolidates a project on the NAS from 10 top-level folders to 3. Editor B's lane B pre-flight lists 3 vs a remembered 10 -> trips 'shrank from 10 to 3'. The admin checks the NAS, sees the reorganisation is intended, clicks [ RESUME ] (or the editor clicks the tray's Resume proxy download). resume() clears the latch, resume_lane_b() arms the lane and triggers a pass, _run_once_locked calls check_remote(scope, 3 names) again, `last` is still 10 -> trips again before a byte moves. Proxy download for that project never runs again on that machine; the only way out is hand-deleting ~/.ccsync/state/lane_b_breaker.json while the companion is stopped.

**Suggested fix.** In resume(), drop the remembered remote counts (at least the scope named in the trip, simplest: `self._remote_counts.clear()`) and persist, so the next listing re-seeds the baseline -- the operator has just asserted the server is in the state it should be synced from. Add a test: trip on shrink, resume, same listing must NOT trip and must become the new baseline.

**Verifier.** Reproduced with a scratch script against companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/lane_guard.py: check_remote('Projects/X', 10 names) -> check_remote('Projects/X', 2 names) trips 'shrank from 10 to 2' -> resume('tray') returns True -> check_remote with the same 2 names trips again with the identical sentence; the EMPTY variant (3 -> [] -> resume -> [] trips again) behaves the same. resume() (lines 296-313) resets _deletes/_bytes/_last_pass_deletes only; check_remote writes _remote_counts[key] only on the non-trip path (lines 364-366), and nothing else in lane_guard.py, RcloneLane.resume_after_trip (rclone_lane.py:3013-3024) or CompanionApp.resume_lane_b (app.py:4066-4090) touches the baseline. The call order in _run_once_locked (rclone_lane.py:2599-2606) means the re-trip happens before any byte moves, so after resume_lane_b arms and trigger_pass_now, the lane parks again immediately. test_resume_clears_the_latch_and_the_counters (test_lane_guard.py:166) only covers the deletion counters. No KNOWN_BUGS entry covers it (CR-44/45 describe triggers 2/3 and the delivery path). High: the tray/dashboard resume button is dead for any trigger-1 trip whose new listing is the intended state, with no recovery short of deleting the state file while the companion is stopped.


### comp-lanes-ab-2: A standing dashboard resume request auto-clears every subsequent trip until a report happens to land while the breaker is clear

**HIGH, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:4485`. Related: CR-45

The CR-45 design relies on the dashboard clearing `lane_b_resume_requested_at` when a report arrives with `sync_guard.lane_b_breaker.tripped == false` (api.py:4485-4504); until then every reply carries `commands.resume_lane_b.apply=true` and the companion applies it whenever its breaker is tripped (app.py:4320-4360, gated only on `self.lane_b_breaker.tripped`, no memory of which request it already honoured). But the companion sends no report on resume: `resume_lane_b()` (app.py:4066-4090) arms the lane and calls `sequencer.trigger_pass_now()`, while the reporter's next post is whatever `_select_interval()` chose BEFORE the reply (reporter.py:714-740): 60 s when nothing was SYNCING, which is the normal state with lane B parked. A lane B pass that re-trips inside that window (a deleting pass moves up to --max-delete 100 files by local rename in seconds; a check_remote re-trip per finding 1 is instantaneous) reports tripped=true, the request stays standing, the next reply resumes it again, and so on every report cycle: 100 more proxies into .ccsync-trash per cycle. That is precisely the 'the cap bounds ONE pass; nothing bounded the sequence' failure the breaker was built to stop (lane_guard.py module docstring), now reinstated by one admin click. KNOWN_BUGS CR-45's claim that 'a LATER, unrelated trip is never cleared by a stale request, because the dashboard has dropped it by then' only holds if a report lands between the resume and the re-trip. The endpoint also deliberately accepts a request for a machine that is NOT tripped (api.py:2715-2733) 'to get in front of one' -- for an offline machine that request waits until its first report, which if the persisted breaker is tripped delivers an auto-resume of a trip nobody reviewed.

**Failure scenario.** A project's Proxy/ subtrees are emptied on the NAS (a proxy dataset fails to mount under an otherwise-present tree, or a clean-out) while its top-level folders remain, so check_remote passes. Editor's pass 1 trashes 100 local proxies, trips. The admin, believing it transient, clicks [ RESUME ]. Next report reply (<=60 s) -> companion resumes and triggers a pass -> rclone moves 100 more proxies into the trash in a few seconds and trips again -> the report 60 s later says tripped -> request still standing -> reply resumes again ... Every ~60 s another 100 proxies leave the project directory until the whole local proxy set is in .ccsync-trash (and prune_trash resumes the moment the breaker reads clear between trips, so the 14-day window can start expiring those copies). The admin clicked once.

**Suggested fix.** Make the request one-shot. Companion side: persist the applied request's `requested_at` in the breaker state file and refuse to apply the same stamp twice (an unrelated later trip needs a fresh click). Dashboard side: clear the request when it is DELIVERED (the reply that carried it) rather than when the machine reports clear, and have the companion post an immediate light report after a resume so the fleet page reflects it. Also refuse `POST .../resume-lane-b` unless the machine's last report shows breaker_tripped (or at least expire a pre-armed request on the first report regardless of its content).

**Verifier.** Traced: dashboard api.py:4485-4504 keeps the request standing and re-sends commands.resume_lane_b on every report until a report arrives with sync_guard.lane_b_breaker.tripped == false; the companion's _apply_resume_lane_b (app.py:4320-4360) gates only on self.lane_b_breaker.tripped and holds no memory of requested_at. resume_lane_b (app.py:4066-4090) arms the lane and triggers a pass but posts no report (no post_once/report_now call anywhere in app.py outside the reporter loop); the reporter's cadence is chosen before the reply (reporter.py:731-744) and is dashboard_report_interval = 60 s (config.py:296) while nothing is SYNCING, which is the parked state. A re-trip within that window is not hypothetical: finding 1 makes a trigger-1 re-trip instantaneous, and a deleting pass is bounded only by --max-delete 100 (rclone_lane.py:118) of local renames, seconds of work, after which resume() has already zeroed every cumulative counter. So the cycle resume -> trash up to 100 -> trip -> report tripped -> reply resumes again repeats every report interval from one admin click, which is exactly the unbounded-sequence failure the breaker exists to stop. The endpoint's explicit acceptance of a request for an untripped machine (api.py:2726-2728) extends this to an offline machine's first report. The CR-45 ledger text's 'the dashboard has dropped it by then' assumes a clear report lands between resume and re-trip, which the code does not guarantee. Bytes land in .ccsync-trash rather than being lost (prune_trash skips while tripped, lane_guard.py:543-545), so not critical; high stands.


### comp-lanes-ab-3: Lane B's --min-age on `sync` trashes the editor's local copy of every proxy rewritten on the NAS in the last 120 s, and a bulk re-render trips the breaker on files that are all still on the server

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/rclone_lane.py:1562`. Related: CR-44

build_down_command adds `--min-age 120s` to `rclone sync` (rclone_lane.py:1562-1568). rclone applies age filters per side: a NAS file rewritten <120 s ago is excluded from the SOURCE listing, while the editor's older local copy is included on the DESTINATION side, so sync treats it as 'source no longer has it' and moves it into --backup-dir without replacing it. Verified against the bundled rclone 1.74.4: src/X/Proxy/a.mov (mtime now) vs dst/X/Proxy/a.mov (mtime 2 days ago) with --min-age 120s -> 'Moved into backup dir', dst left with no a.mov. Consequences: (1) the proxy disappears from the editor's project for at least one rotation (Resolve shows it offline), then is re-downloaded; (2) each one counts as a deletion in RcloneRunTally (`_BACKUP_MOVE`) and feeds `note_pass`; (3) when the base rig regenerates an existing proxy set at the SAME names (Resolve 'Generate Proxy Media' after a codec/resolution change, or the Blackmagic Proxy Generator re-encoding), >=50 files or >=25% of a small project inside the 120 s window trips the breaker; (4) the CR-44 relocation probe cannot rescue it: `_count_relocations` (rclone_lane.py:2979-2992) matches basename + EXACT size and a re-render changes the size, and KNOWN_BUGS CR-44 explicitly states a re-encode 'is still a deletion, on purpose' -- which is true for a re-encode to a new name, but here the file exists at the SAME PATH on the NAS and was merely filtered. The LANE_B_MIN_AGE_SECONDS comment block documents the truncated-download problem the gate fixes but not this side effect.

**Failure scenario.** The base rig regenerates proxies for a 60-clip timeline in project X (same filenames, new size, fresh mtimes). Editor's sequencer reaches X 40 s later: check_remote passes, rclone excludes the 60 young source files, finds 60 old local ones under **/Proxy/**, moves all 60 into .ccsync-trash/<ts>/Projects/X/... and deletes nothing on the NAS. deleted=60 > 50 -> probe lists the scope, every basename is there but at a different size -> relocated=0 -> breaker trips 'one proxy-download pass moved 60 file(s) ... into .ccsync-trash'. The editor's timeline goes offline for those 60 clips, lane B stops until a human resumes it, and the toast blames the server. Smaller case: any single re-rendered proxy vanishes from the editor's folder for one rotation.

**Suggested fix.** In `_count_relocations`, treat a remote file at the SAME relative path as the trashed one (any size) as 'not deleted' -- it was filtered, not removed. The relative path is recoverable by stripping `_last_backup_dir` from the walked path in `_trashed_this_pass`, and list_remote_files already sees full paths. Better still, stop the deletion itself: run the age gate without letting it delete, e.g. `--min-age` only on a preceding `copy` step, or add `--delete-excluded`-free semantics by excluding young files on BOTH sides via a filter that rclone applies to the destination too (a `--max-age`-style approach), or fall back to `*.tmp`/`*.partial` excludes plus rclone's default in-flight size/hash check.

**Verifier.** Reproduced with the bundled companion/.tools/rclone.exe v1.74.4: src/X/Proxy/a.mov (fresh mtime) vs dst/X/Proxy/a.mov (2 days old) under `sync --min-age 120s --backup-dir` emits 'Moved (server-side)' + 'Moved into backup dir' for a.mov and leaves dst without it, while an unchanged old file (b.mov) is untouched. build_down_command adds --min-age to the sync (rclone_lane.py:1559-1566, default 120 from config.py:153). The LANE_B_MIN_AGE_SECONDS comment block (rclone_lane.py:236-251) documents only the truncated-download motivation. Downstream: each such move counts in RcloneRunTally.deleted via _BACKUP_MOVE (rclone_lane.py:1679-1691) and feeds note_pass; _count_relocations (rclone_lane.py:2979-2992) needs basename + exact size, and a re-render at the same path almost always changes size, so the probe cannot excuse it. Medium is right: a single proxy rewritten on the NAS at the same path vanishes from the editor's project for one rotation and a sufficiently fast bulk regeneration (>= 50 files, or >= 25% of a project with >= 20 local proxies, inside 120 s) trips the breaker on files that are all on the server, but it is an edge case needing same-name rewrites landing within the window, and the files are recoverable.


### comp-lanes-ab-4: Every file lane B trashes is also counted as a completed download and double-counted in `transferred` (the 'Moved (server-side)' twin record)

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/rclone_lane.py:1679`. Related: SYNC-10

Verified against the bundled rclone 1.74.4 with --use-json-log: moving a destination file into --backup-dir emits TWO per-file records, `"msg":"Moved (server-side)"` then `"msg":"Moved into backup dir"`, both with the same `object`. RcloneRunTally.feed_record (rclone_lane.py:1679-1691) matches the first on `"Moved" in msg`, sees neither "Deleted" nor `_BACKUP_MOVE`, so it increments `transferred` AND appends the trashed path to `_completed`; the second record increments `transferred` again and `deleted`. Net per trashed proxy: transferred +2, deleted +1, and the file lands in the dashboard's transfer HISTORY as a lane B arrival -- the exact SYNC-10 symptom ('counting it as a completion put it in the dashboard's transfer HISTORY as if it had just arrived') through the record SYNC-10's fix did not know about. test_rclone_lane.py:942 pins 'Moved (server-side)' as a completion, which is never what it means on lane B (lane A is `copy` and emits no Moved lines at all). `result.deleted` is correct, so the breaker is unaffected; the lane detail 'transferred N file(s)' and the history are wrong.

**Failure scenario.** Lane B trashes 12 proxies of project X (a routine superseded-proxy cleanup). The fleet dashboard's transfer history shows those 12 paths as files that just arrived on the editor's machine, direction 'down', lane 'lane_b_proxy_down', and the lane detail says 'transferred 24 file(s)' while zero bytes were downloaded. An admin investigating a breaker trip reads the history and concludes the files were downloaded, not removed.

**Suggested fix.** In feed_record, treat `msg.startswith("Moved (server-side)")` as a no-op (or as a backup move only when a following `_BACKUP_MOVE` record for the same object is expected); simplest: `if "Moved (server-side)" in msg: return` before the per-file matcher, and fix test_tally_captures_completed_file_names to reflect that lane B never completes a 'Moved' file.

**Verifier.** My own rclone 1.74.4 run (above) shows both records for one --backup-dir move: {"msg":"Moved (server-side)","object":"X/Proxy/a.mov"} followed by {"msg":"Moved into backup dir","object":"X/Proxy/a.mov"}. In RcloneRunTally.feed_record (rclone_lane.py:1679-1691) the first matches 'Moved' in msg, contains neither 'Deleted' nor _BACKUP_MOVE ('into backup dir', line 1593), so transferred += 1 and the path is appended to _completed; the second adds transferred += 1 and deleted += 1. _record_completions is called unconditionally for both directions (rclone_lane.py:2702, 2822-2841) and feeds pop_completions -> the dashboard transfer history with direction 'down'. test_tally_captures_completed_file_names (test_rclone_lane.py:937-946) pins 'Moved (server-side)' as a completion. result.deleted is correct so the breaker is unaffected; the 'transferred N file(s)' detail (rclone_lane.py:2756/2775/2811) and the history are wrong. KNOWN_BUGS SYNC-10 covers only the stats-tick double count, not this record. Low: reporting only.


### comp-lanes-ab-5: Relocations on passes that did not trip are never discounted, so the cumulative 'slow leak' rule later trips on a handful of real deletions and blames a reorganisation that was already judged benign

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/lane_guard.py:407`. Related: CR-44

note_pass() only runs the relocation probe when the pass is ABOUT to trip (lane_guard.py:407-413, by design for cost). A pass whose trashed files were all moves but stayed under the per-pass limit therefore adds them to `_deletes` permanently; nothing ever takes them off again, because `_discount_relocations` only ever subtracts the CURRENT pass's probe result. The cumulative counter thus fills with moves that the breaker would have excused had they arrived in one large pass, and the first later pass with a few genuine deletions trips trigger 3 with a 'slow leak' sentence that names a number mostly made of moves. Counters only reset on resume().

**Failure scenario.** An editor reorganises a project over a week in Explorer drags of ~40 proxies each: five passes add 200 to the cumulative counter, each under the 50 per-pass limit so the probe never runs. Pass six supersedes 8 proxies (a real re-render to new names): 208 > 200, the probe checks only this pass's 8 (not moves) -> trips 'proxy download has moved 208 file(s) into .ccsync-trash since it was last checked ... a slow leak'. Every byte of the 200 is on the NAS; the breaker stops lane B for a routine 8-file cleanup and the reason misleads the admin.

**Suggested fix.** When the cumulative rule is what would trip, the probe already runs but only covers this pass; either also probe when a pass's deletions exceed a cheaper threshold (e.g. > limit/2) so moves are discounted as they happen, or keep the per-scope trashed (basename,size) list of un-probed passes in the state file and re-check them once when the cumulative rule is reached.

**Verifier.** Reproduced with a scratch script: five note_pass calls of 40 deletions each (defaults: max_deletes_per_pass 50, max_deletes_cumulative 200, min_local_sample 20) return None without ever invoking the probe (lane_guard.py:407-408 returns before _count_relocations), leaving _deletes = 200; a sixth pass of 8 real deletions with a probe answering 0 trips 'proxy download has moved 208 file(s) ... a slow leak'. _discount_relocations (lines 458-475) only ever subtracts the current pass's probe result and _deletes resets only in resume(). The probe's laziness is a documented design choice (CR-44 'the probe is LAZY'), and the trip is recoverable by one resume, so the cost is a spurious stop plus a misleading sentence after an unusual week-long drip of ~40-file moves. Low is correct.


## Companion: lane C (Syncthing) (1 medium / 4 low)

> **Coverage.** Read end to end: companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/{syncthing_lane,syncthing_supervisor,syncthing_admin,shared_folders}.py, machine.py, identity.py, the sequencer's accept/ignore/unpause paths (sequencer.py 140-300, 740-830, 1241-1530), app.py supervisor wiring (820-840, 1295-1320, 1725-1760, 3021-3035, 3380-3450, 4500-4520), reporter.py device-id cache, selection.py untick, dashboard collector._run_enforce (732-960), db machines/selection helpers (1640-1760, 1870-1895, 1936-2075, 2535-2555), api._register_machine / api_untick / _machine_arg, provision.py, installer shim/plist code (windows_bootstrap.ps1 660-720 and 1755-1810, windows_upgrade.ps1 425-470, macos_bootstrap.sh 1700-1830), KNOWN_BUGS SYNC-17, docs/MULTI_MACHINE_PLAN.md, docs/GOTCHAS.md token section, dashboard/tests/test_enforce.py. Ran the companion area suites read-only (test_syncthing_lane, test_syncthing_supervisor, test_syncthing_admin, test_shared_folders, test_machine, test_identity, test_sequencer, test_sync_sequencer_policy): 284 passed in 21 s. Syncthing's silent removal of unknown folder devices (finding 1) was confirmed against upstream lib/config/config.go ensureExistingDevices and its call in folderconfiguration.go prepare(). Not covered: no local Syncthing instance is running on this machine, so REST status codes for unknown folders (404 vs 500 on /rest/db/ignores) could not be re-verified; the dashboard fake (fake_syncthing.py) accepts any device id on put_folder, which is why finding 1 is invisible to the suite. Did not audit the tray rendering of lane C errors, repath.py, or lane_guard.py (out of area). Checked items that look wrong but are not defects: the macOS supervisor launcher on a pre-2026-08-17 Mac (legacy label only) reports "launcher missing" after three strikes, but that Mac's legacy LaunchAgent has KeepAlive so Syncthing is restarted by launchd anyway; shared_folders._accept's "paired device" check is implicit (Syncthing only lists pending offers from configured devices); sign_out deleting identity.json's editor_report_token is moot because the documented placement is config.toml report_token.


### comp-lane-c-2: "Remove <project> from this machine" unticks the project on every one of the editor's machines

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/selection.py:341`

SelectionClient.untick deliberately sends DELETE /api/v1/selection/<editor>/<slug> with no ?machine=, and api_untick (dashboard api.py:1618-1623) turns a missing machine into db.remove_selection(machine=None), which deletes EVERY selections row for that editor+slug, including the other computer's own per-machine row. The justification in both places is the unassigned bucket ('if it is in the unassigned bucket the folder stays shared to this one') and old companions that cannot name a machine -- but the companion that calls this does know its machine (selection.get sends ?machine= a few lines up), and since v24 a second computer has rows of its own. The tray menu item literally says 'from this machine' (tray.py:2853) and the success message says 'removed from this machine. The server copy is untouched.'

**Failure scenario.** Editor with a desktop and a laptop, both with explicit per-machine plans that include project X (MULTI_MACHINE_PLAN's headline use case). On the laptop she picks Advanced -> Remove 'X' from this machine to free disk. remove_project unticks (all rows gone), removes the local folder and deletes the laptop copy. Within 60 s the enforce cycle sees no selection for X on any of her machines and unshares the folder from the desktop's device too; the desktop's sequencer drops X from its plan on the next selection poll, so lanes A/B stop syncing it and lane C's folder is left configured but unshared. Nothing on the desktop or the dashboard says why X stopped syncing there -- the plan simply lost a row she never unticked. Under-sharing is called 'the safe direction' but it is exactly the 'editor who quietly cannot open a project' outcome the plan doc (§5) says must not happen.

**Suggested fix.** Have untick send ?machine=<this machine>. On the dashboard, make the per-machine DELETE materialise the bucket when needed: if the editor's only rows for the slug are bucket rows (machine=''), copy them onto each OTHER registered machine and then delete the bucket row, so this machine loses the tick and the others keep it. Keep machine=None (remove everywhere) for requests that really carry no machine (old companions, the person-level UI).

**Verifier.** Traced end to end. companion/src/ccsync_companion/selection.py:341-347 deliberately omits ?machine= on the DELETE; dashboard api.py api_untick passes machine=None to db.remove_selection (db.py:1943-1947), which is 'DELETE FROM selections WHERE editor_username=? AND project_slug=?' -- every row, including another machine's explicit per-machine row. The tray item is 'Remove <name> from this machine...' (tray.py:2853) and the success copy is 'removed from this machine. The server copy is untouched.' (app.py:3447). The in-code justification (unassigned bucket / old companions) only covers the bucket case; when both computers have explicit v24 plans (the headline MULTI_MACHINE_PLAN use case) the other computer's own row is deleted too, the enforce cycle then unshares its device within 60 s, and its sequencer drops the project on the next selection poll with nothing on that machine or the dashboard explaining it. MULTI_MACHINE_PLAN.md:47 documents 'unticking removes it everywhere' only for the dashboard SIDEBAR (a person-scoped control); it says nothing that sanctions the tray's per-machine action fanning out. Not in KNOWN_BUGS. Medium stands: an editor's other computer silently loses a project they never unticked.


### comp-lane-c-1: Enforce cycle adds a machine's reported Syncthing device id even when that device is not in the server's device list, so the PUT never converges

**LOW (hunter said medium), CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/collector.py:879`

The per-machine branch of _run_enforce does `device_id = machine_devices.get((editor, machine)); if device_id: desired.add(device_id)` with no check that the id is a device the server's Syncthing actually has configured (`id_to_editor` / the `devices` list). machine_devices comes straight from machines.syncthing_device_id, which the companion reports from its own /rest/system/status on its first report (api._register_machine -> db.upsert_machine, COALESCE keeps any non-null value). Syncthing's config prepare() runs ensureExistingDevices(), which silently strips folder-device entries whose device id is not in the device list (confirmed in upstream lib/config/config.go), so the PUT returns 200, the folder comes back without the device, and the next cycle computes the same desired != actual diff. The old person-level path could not do this because editor_devices was built only from the config's device list. dashboard/tests/test_enforce.py::test_once_a_machine_owns_the_device_the_share_follows_its_own_plan registers 'LAPTOPX-LAPTOPX', a device the fake does not have, and passes only because fake_syncthing.put_folder stores whatever it is given.

**Failure scenario.** An editor's second computer (or a machine whose Syncthing home was regenerated, the known 'stuck lane C = regenerated device ID' case) starts its companion and reports machine_id + syncthing_device_id before the admin approves the pending device on the dashboard. Its plan (own rows, or the unassigned bucket via fetch_machine_selections) names N projects. Every 60 s enforce cycle: for each of those N folders, desired gains the unknown id, put_folder is issued, Syncthing strips it, config.xml on the NAS is rewritten, and the log prints 'enforced shares on <slug>: +[<id>] -[]' -- forever, until the device is approved, and permanently for a decommissioned machine whose Syncthing device an admin removed while its machines row and selections remain (nothing but the 20-machine cap or a rename adoption ever deletes a machines row). The 'enforced shares' log becomes noise that hides real share changes, and poll_runs never shows the cycle as wrong.

**Suggested fix.** Only add a machine's device id when it is present in the pass's device list (e.g. `if device_id and device_id in id_to_editor:`), and log once per device that the machine's Syncthing device is awaiting approval (it is exactly what the pending-devices screen should say). Add a test where the registered machine's device id is absent from the fake's device list and assert no put_folder is issued.

**Verifier.** Traced dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/collector.py:878-881: the per-machine branch adds machine_devices[(editor, machine)] to `desired` with no membership check against id_to_editor/devices, whereas the unmapped-device fallback (lines 887-891) and the shared-asset branch only ever draw from editor_devices (built from the config's device list). machine_devices comes from machines.syncthing_device_id, populated by api.py:3517/3545 from the companion's self-reported myID, which exists before the server ever approves the device. put_folder is PUT /rest/config/folders/{id} (syncthing_client.py:239); upstream Syncthing's folder prepare() (ensureExistingDevices) drops folder-device entries whose id is not a configured device and still returns 200, so the next cycle computes the same diff and issues the same PUT. The test at dashboard/tests/test_enforce.py:116 registers 'LAPTOPX-LAPTOPX', absent from the fake's device list, and passes only because the fake stores whatever it is given. Severity lowered from medium: the device does not get shared wrongly (it cannot, it is not approved), nothing is lost, and it self-corrects the moment the admin approves the pending device. The lasting cost is an 'enforced shares on <slug>: +[id]' log line per folder per 60 s plus a config.xml rewrite, permanent only for a decommissioned machine whose row and selections outlive its device. Noise/churn, not a broken editor path.


### comp-lane-c-3: Syncthing device id is cached for the process lifetime, so a regenerated Syncthing identity is reported as the old one until the companion restarts

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/reporter.py:353`

Reporter._syncthing_device_id stores the first non-empty myID in _syncthing_device_id_cache and never re-reads it; SyncthingLane._get_my_device_id (syncthing_lane.py:365-375) does the same for the lane's outgoing-need calculation. The supervisor (SYNC-17) is now the thing that restarts `syncthing serve --home=<managed home>` on an editor machine, and `serve` on a home whose key/cert files are gone generates a fresh identity -- i.e. the one event that changes myID mid-run is one the companion itself causes, without restarting.

**Failure scenario.** An editor (or a support session following the 'stuck lane C' recipe) deletes %LOCALAPPDATA%\ccsync\syncthing-config to reset Syncthing while the tray keeps running. The supervisor relaunches it after 30 s; the new instance has device id D2. The reporter keeps sending D1 on every report, so machines.syncthing_device_id stays D1 (COALESCE only replaces with a non-null value, and D1 is non-null), the enforce cycle keeps sharing the machine's folders with dead D1, the pending-devices screen cannot say 'this is <machine>', and the lane's outgoing-need loop compares remote devices against D1. It corrects itself only at the next companion restart/self-upgrade, which may be days away.

**Suggested fix.** Invalidate both caches whenever the supervisor closes an incident (SyncthingSupervisor._note_reachable can call a hook the app wires to reporter/lane), or simply re-read myID on a slow cadence (e.g. every full report) -- it is one loopback GET.

**Verifier.** Code confirmed: reporter.py:249/343-353 caches the first non-empty myID in _syncthing_device_id_cache and nothing in companion/src ever resets it (grep shows only the init, the read and the one write); syncthing_lane.py:230/365-375 does the same for _my_device_id. No hook from SyncthingSupervisor._note_reachable invalidates either. The mid-run regeneration scenario requires Syncthing to exit AND its key/cert to be gone before the supervisor relaunches it -- a support-session wipe of the managed home -- which is plausible but uncommon, and it self-heals on the next tray restart/upgrade. Meanwhile the dashboard keeps the stale id (upsert_machine COALESCE keeps a non-null value), so per-machine shares target the dead device and the pending-device screen cannot name the machine. Low as reported.


### comp-lane-c-4: Lane C's 'no API key' early return never informs the supervisor, so a Syncthing whose config.xml is missing is never restarted and an open incident never closes

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/syncthing_lane.py:438`

check_once returns STATE_ERROR 'no Syncthing API key (checked ...)' before touching the network and without calling _note_supervisor at all. _has_any_api_key is false exactly when neither the managed nor the stock config.xml exists -- the state a wiped or never-generated Syncthing home is in, where `syncthing serve --home=...` (what the shim runs) would regenerate the config and bring the API back. The supervisor only acts on tick(False), so the one thing that could repair this state is never asked to; and if an incident was already open (ticks of False before the file vanished), no tick(True) ever arrives to close it, so sync_guard.syncthing_supervisor keeps reporting an incident and the state file is never cleared.

**Failure scenario.** The managed Syncthing home on an editor's machine is deleted (disk cleanup tool, a support session's 'delete the config dir and let it regenerate' advice, or a failed Syncthing upgrade that leaves no config.xml). Syncthing is dead and config.xml is gone. Lane C shows 'no Syncthing API key (checked ...)' forever; the supervisor, whose whole purpose is 'nothing else restarts Syncthing', stands idle because it was never told the API is unreachable, even though running the shim would regenerate the home and restore the API.

**Suggested fix.** In the no-key branch, still call self._note_supervisor(False) (the supervisor's launch is key-independent and api_reachable already treats 401/403 as alive), and keep the distinct 'no API key' sentence as the lane detail; alternatively have the supervisor's probe/launch path not depend on the lane's key check at all.

**Verifier.** syncthing_lane.py:438-445 returns STATE_ERROR without calling _note_supervisor, so the supervisor gets neither a False (to launch) nor a True (to close an incident). The launcher is key-independent (syncthing_supervisor.py:209-260 runs the shim / launchctl kickstart), so asking it would regenerate the home. However the scenario is narrower than described: _has_any_api_key (lane:284-289) also returns true when _active_api_key is held in memory, i.e. a lane that has talked to Syncthing once in this process keeps going down the normal ping path after the home is wiped, gets connection-refused, and the supervisor IS told and does relaunch. The no-key branch only bites when the companion process starts with no config.xml anywhere and no configured key (tray restarted after a wipe, or a home never generated) while Syncthing is not running; at a real logon the Run-key shim starts Syncthing independently. Real but narrow; low.


### comp-lane-c-5: Supervisor's 20 s API wait runs on whatever thread calls check_once, including the tray's diagnostics callback, with no lock around the launch decision

**LOW, PLAUSIBLE.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/syncthing_supervisor.py:643`

_note_unreachable decides to launch and then _attempt_start -> _wait_for_api sleeps up to api_wait_seconds (20 s) on the caller's thread. check_once is called not only from the lane's poll thread but synchronously from CompanionApp's diagnostics builder (app.py:4514, 'Copy diagnostics' in the tray). The decision to launch is made outside the lock (the lock is released after reading since/attempts/last_attempt and before _attempt_start), so two concurrent check_once callers past the grace window can both pass the backoff test and both spawn the shim.

**Failure scenario.** Syncthing is down and the grace window has elapsed. The editor, seeing the red lane C line, clicks Copy diagnostics at the same moment the poll thread ticks: the tray callback blocks for up to 20 s in _wait_for_api (the menu is unresponsive), and both threads run the launcher, starting two `syncthing serve --home=<same home>` processes; the second fails on the port/db lock and exits, leaving a confusing pair of 'start attempt 1' / 'start attempt 2' log lines and an attempts counter advanced by two for one outage.

**Suggested fix.** Guard _attempt_start with a non-blocking 'launch in progress' flag set under the lock (return early if set), and have the diagnostics path read lane_c.status()/api_reachable() instead of calling check_once(), so tray callbacks never drive the supervisor.

**Verifier.** Half refuted. The tray does not run diagnostics on its UI thread: tray.py:2474 uses _spawn, which starts a daemon worker thread (tray.py:1696-1700), so the menu is never blocked; the 20 s _wait_for_api only parks that worker (Copy diagnostics takes up to 20 s to reach the clipboard, a minor annoyance). The double-launch race is technically present -- _note_unreachable (supervisor.py:526-592) reads since/attempts/last_attempt under the lock, releases it, and _attempt_start re-takes it to bump attempts/last_attempt -- but the unguarded window is the few instructions between releasing the lock and re-acquiring it, so a poll-thread tick and a diagnostics click would have to land within microseconds of each other; and even then the second `syncthing serve` on the same home exits on the DB/port lock, costing one spurious 'start attempt N' log line and an attempts counter off by one (which the next _note_reachable resets). Not practically reachable by an editor; kept PLAUSIBLE rather than REFUTED only because the lock-gap genuinely exists.


## Companion: Resolve integration (3 medium / 2 low)

> **Coverage.** Read end to end: resolve_bridge.py (lock/wedge guard, enumerators + poll cache, save point/journal hook, replace_clip, link_proxy_media, unlink, undo_last_relink, perform_insert, import_files_to_bin_path/_canonicalize_imported), resolve_journal.py, watcher.py, fixer.py, proxy_relink.py, proxy_history.py, luts.py, resolve_prefs.py, project_setup.py, drive_swap.py, ui_dispatch.py, ui_state.py, and popup.py's fix-all paths (dedupe/rows, perform_fix_all, PopupDialog threading/close/deliver). Followed the callers in app.py: _handle_non_canonical/_canon_relink_loop/_relink_non_canonical, undo_last_relink, _handle_out_of_tree/_show_out_of_tree_popup/_run_popup_batches/_drain_stranded_popup_batches, scan_whole_project, _refresh_media_tree_once/_classify_pool_once/_relink_proxies_once, _handle_mapping_warning, and the tray wiring for Scan whole project / Undo. Checked KNOWN_BUGS CR-12 (save point + journal) against the code: the hook is real and every path-rewriting write does go through replace_clip/link_proxy_media (the one direct LinkProxyMedia in _attach_adjacent_proxy is journalled and documented). Ran the companion suite read-only: 4256 passed, 46 skipped (291 s); test_resolve_edit_safety covers save point, journal, and undo but has no cross-project undo case and nothing pins the "Scan whole project drains held non-canonical clips" claim. Not covered: stills.py/resolve_mapping_helper beyond the ensure_media_storage call, consolidate.py, music_worker.py, the live behaviour of ExportProject/ReplaceClip path spelling on macOS (CR-12 itself notes the export is unverified on a live Resolve; the macOS clip-path flavour is an open hardware question in _log_darwin_clip_path_flavor). Did not modify the repo.


### comp-resolve-1: Rate-limited non-canonical relinks are held in a queue nothing drains; the log/doc say Scan whole project runs it, but it does not

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/app.py:1849`. Related: CR-12

_handle_non_canonical checks resolve_journal.allow_automatic(project, 'canon-relink') at the start of a burst. When refused (a burst ran <15 min ago) it leaves the clips in self._canon_relink_pending, sets _canon_relink_busy=False and logs 'Tray → Advanced → Scan whole project runs it now' (docs/RESOLVE_EDIT_SAFETY.md makes the same promise). But scan_whole_project (app.py:2151-2204) only collects OUT_OF_TREE clips for the popup; it never calls _handle_non_canonical, never drains _canon_relink_pending, and never re-offers NON_CANONICAL paths. Both producers latch once per process (watcher._offered_non_canonical at watcher.py:255-257, app._pool_offered_non_canonical at app.py:2873-2875), so the held paths can only be relinked if some OTHER, never-seen non-canonical path appears after the 15-minute window and re-enters _handle_non_canonical. There is no tray action and no timer that retries the held queue.

**Failure scenario.** Editor imports 10 clips through F:\Creators_Club (stored locally spelled) -> auto-relinked to P:\ at T=0. At T=5 min they import 5 more the same way -> refused by the limiter, held, log says use Scan whole project. Editor runs Scan whole project: 'all media is in the tree'. The 5 clips stay F:\-spelled for the rest of the companion process (Media Offline for every other editor who opens the project) even though the code believes it merely deferred them.

**Suggested fix.** Either have scan_whole_project (and/or _classify_pool_once on its next 120 s tick) call _handle_non_canonical with the pending batch once allow_automatic permits (drop the once-per-process latch for keys still in _canon_relink_pending), or start a one-shot timer for seconds_until_automatic() when a burst is refused. Add a test: refused burst + scan_whole_project -> replace_clip called.

**Verifier.** Traced end to end. app.py:1849-1860: when allow_automatic() refuses, _canon_relink_busy is reset, the items stay in _canon_relink_pending and the log promises 'Tray → Advanced → Scan whole project runs it now'. grep shows _canon_relink_pending is only touched at 897, 1834, 1852 and 1879 (the loop, which is only started from _handle_non_canonical). scan_whole_project (app.py:2151-2204) classifies OUT_OF_TREE only and calls _show_out_of_tree_popup; it never touches NON_CANONICAL, _handle_non_canonical or the pending list. Both producers latch per process (watcher.py:255-257 _offered_non_canonical; app.py:2873-2875 _pool_offered_non_canonical), so the held paths are never re-offered; only a brand-new non-canonical path arriving after the 15-minute window would re-enter _handle_non_canonical and incidentally drain the queue. docs/RESOLVE_EDIT_SAFETY.md:41 makes the same false promise. CR-12 is marked fixed but this deferral path in that fix does not do what it says. Medium is right: clips stay machine-private-spelled for the process lifetime, but nothing is lost and a companion restart re-offers them.


### comp-resolve-2: Undo replays the newest journal of ANY project against whatever project is open, matched by file path only

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/resolve_bridge.py:1306`. Related: CR-12

undo_last_relink() takes resolve_journal.latest_session() with no project filter (app.undo_last_relink at app.py:1944 passes nothing), reads data['entries'], walks the CURRENT project's media pool and relinks any clip whose File Path equals an entry's 'new' path back to 'old'. It never compares data['project'] with the open project (current_project_name / the walk's project_name). The docstring claims a different open project is 'skipped, never guessed at', but that only holds when no clip in the open project shares a path with the journal, which is exactly false for shared assets (P:\Assets\Music\..., B-roll archive clips, anything under Assets/ that every project references) and for any second project built from the same footage.

**Failure scenario.** Project A: auto-canonical pass rewrites F:\Creators_Club\Assets\Music\bed.wav -> P:\Assets\Music\bed.wav (journalled under A). Editor opens project B, which also uses P:\Assets\Music\bed.wav, and presses Tray → Advanced → Undo meaning to revert something in B. The newest journal is A's: B's clip is ReplaceClip'd to F:\Creators_Club\Assets\Music\bed.wav, a machine-private spelling that is offline for every other editor, the toast says 'Put 1 clip path(s) back', and the undo is not journalled so it cannot itself be undone. The watcher's once-per-process latch may already hold that key, so the auto-relink does not repair it.

**Suggested fix.** Resolve the open project first (current_project_name(max_age=0) or the pool walk's project_name) and call latest_session(project_name); refuse with a clear message when the newest journal belongs to another project (or when data['project'] != open project). Add a test with a same-path clip in a different project.

**Verifier.** resolve_bridge.py:1306-1320: undo_last_relink() calls resolve_journal.latest_session() with no project filter; sessions(None) (resolve_journal.py:320-336) walks every project slug dir and returns the lexically newest file across all projects. app.undo_last_relink (app.py:1944) passes nothing. data['project'] is read only by describe_latest for the toast text, never compared with the open project. The replay matches the current pool's clips by normalised File Path == entry['new'] and ReplaceClips them to entry['old'] with journal=False. For any path shared by two projects (Assets/Music, B-roll archive, shared footage) the cross-project rewrite is real and the 'skipped, never guessed at' docstring claim is false. The tests in test_resolve_edit_safety.py all use a single project 'FF5', so nothing covers this. Medium: user-initiated, single-machine, and the resulting F:-spelled clips are NON_CANONICAL so the next process restart (or an unlatched watcher key) re-canonicalises them; but the undo itself is unjournalled and so not reversible from the tray.


### comp-resolve-3: Journal bookkeeping makes fusionscript calls (GetName, GetClipProperty) outside _API_LOCK after a successful ReplaceClip/LinkProxyMedia

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/resolve_bridge.py:1161`. Related: CR-12 / AUDIT_2 CORE-H4

The module's invariant (lines 37-47) is that EVERY call into the Resolve C extension is serialised by _API_LOCK, because fusionscript.dll is not thread-safe and a concurrent call has faulted 0xc0000005 and taken the windowed companion down with no log. replace_clip's success branch calls resolve_journal.record(..., clip_name=_safe_clip_name(media_pool_item)) at line 1161 AFTER the `with _bridge_call('replace_clip')` block closed at 1156; _safe_clip_name calls media_pool_item.GetName(). link_proxy_media does the same at 1247-1248 (_safe_clip_name and _safe_clip_property -> GetClipProperty()) after its lock block ended at 1238. Both are native calls made with no lock held, on the FIX ALL worker / canon-relink thread / media-tree thread, while the watcher polls get_timeline_items every 3 s under the lock.

**Failure scenario.** FIX ALL over 50 clips: after each ReplaceClip takes, the worker thread calls GetName() unlocked while the watcher thread is mid-sweep inside GetClipProperty() under the lock. Two concurrent calls into fusionscript.dll, which is the documented access-violation path: the tray process dies silently mid-batch with files copied but some clips unrelinked and no journal line for the last one.

**Suggested fix.** Read clip_name (and for link_proxy_media the File Path) inside the locked block alongside `after`/`old_proxy`, and pass the strings to resolve_journal.record. Same for _attach_adjacent_proxy's record call (already inside the lock, fine) - make the rule explicit with a test double that asserts the lock is held on every call.

**Verifier.** resolve_bridge.py:37-47 states the invariant (every fusionscript call under _API_LOCK, 0xc0000005 otherwise). replace_clip: the `with _bridge_call('replace_clip')` block ends at line 1156 (after `after = _file_path_locked()`); the `resolve_journal.record(... clip_name=_safe_clip_name(media_pool_item) ...)` at 1159-1163 is outside it, and _safe_clip_name (line 520-525) calls media_pool_item.GetName() with no lock of its own. link_proxy_media: the locked block ends at 1238; lines 1246-1249 call _safe_clip_name and _safe_clip_property (GetClipProperty()) unlocked. _bridge_call acquires _API_LOCK (line 129/132) and nothing else in those helpers re-acquires it. The watcher's poll_timeline_items and the 120 s media-tree walk run concurrently under the lock, so the concurrent-native-call window exists on every successful journalled relink. Could not reproduce a fault without live Resolve, but the code path is exactly the one the module's own comments say crashes; medium stands (silent tray death mid-batch, not data loss since replace_clip already took).


### comp-resolve-4: Exported .drp rollback copies are never swept; only the .json journals are, contrary to the doc's 60-day claim

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/resolve_journal.py:367`. Related: CR-12

_sweep() globs `*.json` under ~/.ccsync/resolve_edits/<slug>/ and unlinks those older than RETENTION_DAYS. save_project() writes `<stamp>.drp` into the same directory (resolve_bridge.py:1051), up to once per project per 15 minutes of editing, and nothing ever removes them. docs/RESOLVE_EDIT_SAFETY.md 'Housekeeping' states 'Journals and exports older than 60 days are swept on the next write'.

**Failure scenario.** An editor whose companion auto-relinks or FIX ALLs regularly on a large project accrues a .drp of the whole project database (tens of MB each) per save point indefinitely in their home directory; after months the folder is GBs, and the doc tells the admin it self-cleans.

**Suggested fix.** Sweep `*.drp` with the same cutoff in _sweep (or glob('*') filtered to .json/.drp), and add a test that an old .drp is removed.

**Verifier.** resolve_journal._sweep (lines 361-372) globs only '*.json'. save_project (resolve_bridge.py:1048-1051) writes `<stamp>.drp` into the same directory (journal_root()/slug). No other code path references .drp deletion (grep of tests shows .drp only as a string in note_save_point tests). docs/RESOLVE_EDIT_SAFETY.md:77 claims exports are swept too. Low is right: disk growth in the editor's home, no functional breakage; export is rate-limited to once per project per 15 minutes so growth is bounded per session.


### comp-resolve-5: BAD_PREFIX mapping warning is a toast per clip path, not per incident; on macOS each is a blocking osascript spawn on the watcher thread

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/watcher.py:266`. Related: COMP-GUARD-7 (different root cause, same symptom)

poll_once calls self._on_mapping_warning(item) once per newly seen BAD_PREFIX key, synchronously. app._handle_mapping_warning (app.py:1775-1804) turns every call into _notify_tray(...). _warned_mapping only dedupes per path, so a timeline with N canonical clips on an unmapped prefix produces N toasts in the first poll (and again after every recovery-reset at watcher.py:300-311). On Windows Shell_NotifyIcon just replaces the balloon, but on macOS tray_native.notify (tray_native.py:1243-1264) runs `osascript` synchronously with a 10 s timeout per call, so the watcher thread is parked for N spawns and Notification Center fills with N identical alerts. The root_present gate only covers an unplugged local_root, not a wrong/missing canonical mapping.

**Failure scenario.** Mac editor opens a 300-clip project before the Mapped Mount is configured (or Windows editor before P: is mapped): 300 identical 'Resolve is looking for media on P:\ ...' notifications; on the Mac the watcher loop stalls for a minute-plus while 300 osascript processes run serially, and the tray's project name/dashboard report go stale meanwhile.

**Suggested fix.** Coalesce in _handle_mapping_warning: one toast per prefix-broken episode (edge-triggered on prefix_broken, reset when prefix_healthy), keeping the per-path log line; or batch the new BAD_PREFIX items per poll like _handle_foreign_batch does.

**Verifier.** watcher.py:261-269: one synchronous _on_mapping_warning(item) per newly seen BAD_PREFIX key, deduped only by path (_warned_mapping), and re-armed wholesale on recovery at 300-311. app._handle_mapping_warning (1775-1804) has no coalescing and calls _notify_tray per call; _notify_tray (1980-1985) has no dedupe. On macOS tray_native.notify (1243-1264) is a synchronous subprocess.run(['osascript',...], timeout=10) on the calling (watcher) thread. The root_present gate (watcher.py:106-113, 370-380) is only consulted for an absent local_root, so a wrong/missing Mapped Mount or unmapped P: with the root present still produces N toasts on the first full poll. The failure scenario is real but cosmetic-to-annoying: an osascript 'display notification' normally returns in well under a second, so a 300-clip timeline parks the watcher for tens of seconds rather than minutes, and the Windows case is one balloon replacing another. Low stands.


## Companion: app core / tray / upgrade (4 medium / 1 low)

> **Coverage.** Read end to end: upgrade.py, release_pubkey.py, ed25519.py (verify path), eula.py, site.py, reporter.py, manifest.py, crash_report.py, idle.py, secretfile.py, __main__.py, tray_native.py (all three backends). Read targeted: app.py (single-instance guard, report-response fan-out, _apply_fleet_halt/_apply_pushed_update/_apply_resume_lane_b, _on_upgrade_available, apply_upgrade + stand-down predicate, EULA gate/_licence_watch/_start_lanes, halt/resume, shutdown(), run()), tray.py (start_tray loops, update dialog, quit/resume/halt handlers, upgrade/licence items), config.py (VERSION, load_config/_apply_site_manifest, resolved_log_path), shutdown_guard.py (Windows guard + Darwin terminate/SIGTERM policy; keep-awake guards not read), theme.py (claim_app_identity/_register_app_display only). Followed boundaries: dashboard api.py _upgrade_info + the commands block (halt/upgrade/resume_lane_b) and publish-time min_version validation (release_trust.valid_min_version), installer/build_editor_package.ps1 min_version source, sync/lane_guard.HaltState, sync/sequencer.stop and rclone_lane.stop timings. Checked KNOWN_BUGS CR-22/27/40/41/44/45 and R11 before reporting. Ran companion suite read-only: 4300 passed, 2 skipped (289 s). Verified version ordering uses int tuples everywhere that matters (upgrade.parse_version/compare_to_running, eula.version_tuple; dashboard uses "different", never string order) so 0.9.43 vs 0.10.0 is correct. Not covered: the keep-awake half of shutdown_guard.py, theme.py asset/logo resolution, tray.py's snapshot/fingerprint/menu body beyond the sections named, and nothing was exercised against the deployed build or a live dashboard.


### comp-app-core-1: Self-upgrade hand-off: the child gives up after 20 s but the parent's shutdown can legitimately take longer, leaving the machine with no companion (R11 shape, different timer)

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/app.py:198`. Related: R11

upgrade._apply_inner spawns the new build, watches it for only CHILD_TAKEOVER_GRACE_SECONDS = 2 s, then calls request_shutdown() (app.shutdown). The child reaches the single-instance guard ~1 s in and waits at most PREDECESSOR_WAIT_SECONDS = 20 s (app.py:198, used by _acquire_mutex_win32 and _wait_for_predecessor) for the parent's mutex/pid slot to clear, then logs 'treating it as a live second instance' and EXITS. But shutdown() (app.py:5753) is a chain of bounded joins whose sum exceeds 20 s on an ordinary busy machine: _stop_lanes -> sequencer.stop() = _wait_for_lane_c_turn_idle(5) + thread.join(timeout=10) (sync/sequencer.py:365; the sequencer's _interrupt event is not consulted by rclone_lane's run_once, so a worker parked on an rclone child sits out the full 10 s), then lane A/B stop() each up to periodic join 5 + terminate/wait 5 + observer join 5, then watcher/media-tree joins 5+5 (a fusionscript call that does not return), proxy_generator 5, broll_ingestor 5, youtube_importer 5. The parent only stands down after it has already spawned the child and there is nothing that retries the launch (Run-key / LaunchAgent autostart is logon-only). R11 fixed the 'refuse immediately' version of this; the bounded wait it introduced is simply shorter than the teardown it is waiting for.

**Failure scenario.** Editor is mid lane-B proxy download (rclone running under the sequencer) with Resolve open and the watcher inside a media-pool call. Admin pushes an update (or auto_update is on). t=0 new exe spawned; t=2 parent calls shutdown(); sequencer join times out at ~t=12; lane B terminate + observer join ~t=17; watcher join times out ~t=22; the child's 20 s deadline expired at ~t=21 with the mutex still held -> child exits with the 'already running' path; parent finishes exiting at ~t=25. Machine has no companion until next logon; dashboard shows the machine going dark right after 'applying pushed update'.

**Suggested fix.** Make PREDECESSOR_WAIT_SECONDS comfortably larger than shutdown()'s worst-case budget (e.g. 90 s), or have the parent pass its remaining shutdown budget in CCSYNC_REPLACES_PID's sibling env var and have the child wait that long; alternatively have the parent re-spawn the child from the tail of shutdown() if the earlier child has exited. Add a test that sums the join timeouts in shutdown()/_stop_lanes and asserts PREDECESSOR_WAIT_SECONDS exceeds it.

**Verifier.** Traced end to end. upgrade._apply_inner (upgrade.py:899-944) spawns the child, watches it for CHILD_TAKEOVER_GRACE_SECONDS=2 s, then calls request_shutdown(). apply_upgrade (app.py:3919-3964) pauses nothing beforehand, so the sequencer can be mid lane-B rclone run. The child's guard waits PREDECESSOR_WAIT_SECONDS=20 s (app.py:198, used by _acquire_mutex_win32 and _wait_for_predecessor) and then refuses. shutdown() (app.py:5753+) serialises: sequencer.stop() = _lane_c_idle.wait(5) + thread.join(10) (sequencer.py:363-365) -- and the sequencer's _interrupt is only consulted by sequencer's own waits (sequencer.py:1606-1639), rclone_lane.run_once never sees it, so a worker parked on an rclone child sits out the whole 10 s before lane_b.stop() finally terminates the child; then lane C join 5, lane A stop, lane B terminate/wait(5) + observer join 5, watcher + media-tree join 5+5, proxy_generator 5, broll_ingestor 5, youtube_importer 5, then tray stop and interpreter exit before the mutex clears. 10 s is near-certain during any lane-B download; exceeding 20 s needs ~10 s more of stalls (e.g. watcher inside a fusionscript call + media-tree thread, or a generator kill on a disconnected share), which is plausible but not the common case. Nothing ties the two constants together and no test sums them (app.py:194's 'only for as long as a normal shutdown takes' is unenforced). Outcome when it trips is R11's: no companion until next logon. Medium stands.


### comp-app-core-2: macOS tray backend mutates AppKit (NSStatusItem button image/tooltip, NSMenu rebuild) from the tray refresh and pulse worker threads, not the main thread

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/tray_native.py:1284`

_DarwinIcon's `icon` setter (tray_native.py:1196) calls _apply_image() (line 1284) which does button.setImage_(...) directly; the `title` setter calls button.setToolTip_ directly; and __setattr__ (line 1342) rebuilds and installs a whole NSMenu via setMenu_ whenever `menu` is rebound. tray.py's _refresh_loop (tray.py:2948-2956) and _pulse_loop (tray.py:3007) run on daemon worker threads and assign icon.icon up to 8x every 3 s while pulsing, icon.title every 2 s, and icon.menu on every fingerprint change. AppKit UI objects are main-thread-only; the pystray backend this replaced marshalled these through performSelectorOnMainThread. tray.py already knows the rule (the MAC-7 placement check goes through ui_dispatch.dispatch, tray.py:746), but the three setters in the backend do not, so the rewrite silently dropped the marshalling. No test can catch this on Windows, and the companion suite stubs the backend.

**Failure scenario.** Mac editor with a syncing lane: the pulse thread calls NSStatusBarButton.setImage_ off the main thread 8x per 3 s while the main thread is inside Tk-Aqua's runloop drawing the same status item. Main Thread Checker flags it; in practice this is intermittent corruption of the menu-bar item or a crash inside AppKit with no Python traceback (the shape of the 'companion vanished at startup with nothing in the log' incidents already recorded for macOS). Rebinding `menu` off-thread while the user has the menu open is the worst case: the NSMenu the user is tracking is replaced under them.

**Suggested fix.** Route _apply_image, the tooltip set and _apply_menu through ui_dispatch.dispatch (or NSObject performSelectorOnMainThread_withObject_waitUntilDone_) when not already on the main thread; keep the worker threads' rate limiting. Add a darwin-guarded test with a fake AppKit that asserts the setters are invoked via the dispatcher.

**Verifier.** Code-level defect traced; runtime crash not observable from Windows. tray_native.py has no performSelectorOnMainThread / ui_dispatch call anywhere (only comments at 737 and 1168 acknowledge the main-thread rule). _DarwinIcon.icon setter -> _apply_image -> button.setImage_ (1196-1198, 1284-1291); title setter -> button.setToolTip_ (1204-1212); __setattr__('menu') -> _apply_menu -> _status_item.setMenu_(_build_nsmenu(...)) (1342-1348, 1293-1298). tray.py starts _refresh_loop and _pulse_loop as plain daemon threads unconditionally (tray.py ~3015-3020) before the darwin branch at 3034 calls run_detached() on the main thread, and those loops assign icon.icon / icon.title / icon.menu (2948-2956, 3007). pystray's darwin backend wrapped _update_icon/_update_title/_update_menu in a mainthread decorator; this rewrite dropped it. Off-main-thread AppKit mutation is a documented correctness violation (Main Thread Checker flags it); the failure is intermittent corruption/crash rather than deterministic, so medium, not high.


### comp-app-core-3: A signed record whose min_version is above its own version raises every machine's downgrade floor past the build being offered, refusing it and every later build below that floor; nothing validates min_version <= version on either side

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/upgrade.py:651`. Related: CR-6

UpgradeManager._accept_offer (upgrade.py:651) calls note_floor(self._floor_file, info['min_version']) BEFORE below_floor(info['version'], floor), and note_floor is monotonic and persisted. So an offer {version: 0.9.44, min_version: 0.9.50} raises the floor to 0.9.50 and is then refused as 'below the downgrade floor'. The dashboard's publish handler only checks release_trust.valid_min_version (dotted-numeric; api.py:3100), tools/sign_release.py only checks the same shape (line 180), and build_editor_package.ps1 takes CCSYNC_MIN_VERSION verbatim from the environment (line 629) -- so a typo'd or stale env var is signed, published, and remembered by every machine that merely RECEIVES the offer (no install needed). The only recovery is hands-on deletion of ~/.ccsync/upgrade_floor.json on each machine (docs/RELEASE.md), which is exactly the cost the floor is designed to impose on attackers, now imposed by one operator slip. Note also that the floor is raised on OFFER, not on install, so any machine that merely saw a high-min_version build can no longer take a rollback of `current` to an older version even though it never ran the new one.

**Failure scenario.** Operator ships 0.9.44 with CCSYNC_MIN_VERSION=0.9.45 left over from a planning note (or a fat-fingered 0.9.54). Every reporting companion verifies the signature, writes upgrade_floor.json = 0.9.45, logs 'REFUSING the offered build v0.9.44', and from then on also refuses the corrected 0.9.44 republish and any hotfix 0.9.44x. The fleet is frozen on its current versions until someone touches every machine.

**Suggested fix.** In _accept_offer, reject (and do NOT note the floor for) any record where parse_version(min_version) > parse_version(version): a record that says 'you may not install below X' while offering X-1 is self-contradictory and a signing-tool bug. Mirror the check in tools/sign_release.py and the dashboard publish handler (422). Consider raising the floor only when the build is actually applied (inside _apply_inner after the swap succeeds) rather than on receipt.

**Verifier.** Reproduced with a scratch probe against the real module: note_floor(f, '0.9.50') then below_floor('0.9.44', read_floor(f)) -> True, and a corrected 0.9.44 republish stays refused. _accept_offer (upgrade.py:651-658) calls note_floor BEFORE below_floor, note_floor is monotonic and persisted (191-225), and nothing checks min_version <= version: companion (no test in test_upgrade.py covers it), dashboard publish handler only calls release_trust.valid_min_version (api.py ~3100; release_trust.py:130-141 checks shape only), tools/sign_release.py:180 checks dotted-numeric only, build_editor_package.ps1:629 takes $env:CCSYNC_MIN_VERSION verbatim. The floor is raised on receipt of the offer, not on install, so every reporting machine is affected with no click. Impact is a fleet frozen on its current builds (they keep working) plus hands-on recovery per machine (docs/RELEASE.md:693); medium is right.


### comp-app-core-4: auto_update is one-shot per offer: a stand-down ('popup'/'consolidate') or a failed download is never retried, so the CR-27 machine class never gets an unattended update

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/app.py:4217`. Related: CR-41

_on_upgrade_available (app.py:4178-4220) is the only trigger for unattended updates and it is invoked by UpgradeManager.note_report_response solely when `newly` is set, i.e. when the offered version differs from the one already held (upgrade.py:700-702). It starts one apply_upgrade thread and discards the result. apply_upgrade stands down with 'popup' whenever _popup_active_lock is held and with 'consolidate' during a copy, and returns 'failed' on a transient download error -- none of which re-arms anything. Subsequent reports carry the same offer, `newly` stays None, and the flag is effectively dead for that version until the companion restarts. CR-41 fixed precisely this latch-once shape for the admin PUSH path (_run_pushed_update with 90 s / 600 s retries) but the auto_update path was left as the single fire.
