On the machines MULTI_MACHINE_PLAN §9 names as the reason for auto_update (ruskin's PC, whose out-of-tree popup takes the lock ~3 s after every launch, CR-27/CR-27a), the restart does not help either: the popup wins the race on every start.

**Failure scenario.** Site has auto_update=true. 0.9.44 is published. Editor's companion has the NEW PROJECT / out-of-tree popup open (or a consolidate running) when the offer first arrives: apply_upgrade returns 'popup', the editor gets one 'Can't update while a CCSync window is open' toast, closes the window, and the update never happens. The dashboard shows the machine outdated indefinitely while the site believes updates are unattended; the admin has to fall back to UPDATE NOW, which does retry.

**Suggested fix.** Reuse the pushed-update retry machinery: have _on_upgrade_available record the auto-applicable version and let _on_report_response (or a small timer) re-attempt apply_upgrade(quiet_refusals=True) with the same PUSHED_UPDATE_RETRY_SECONDS / _FAILED_RETRY_SECONDS back-off while the offer still stands and is newer; stop when VERSION catches up. Add a test mirroring test_app's CR-41 case for the auto path.

**Verifier.** Traced. note_report_response (upgrade.py:700-712) only sets `newly` when _available is None or the version differs, and _on_upgrade_available (app.py:4178-4220) is the sole auto-update trigger; it starts a daemon thread running self.apply_upgrade and ignores the return. apply_upgrade (3936-3947, 3963) returns 'popup' / 'consolidate' / 'failed' without re-arming anything; grep for auto-update/ccsync-auto-upgrade in app.py finds no other re-entry. The CR-41 retry machinery (_run_pushed_update, app.py:4294-4318, PUSHED_UPDATE_RETRY_SECONDS 90/600) is wired only to the push path. Existing tests (test_app.py:1835-1860) only assert the first call applies or is skipped for a rollback. Mitigations: a companion restart clears _available so the next report fires once more, and the admin's UPDATE NOW push does retry -- so the flag is degraded rather than dead, and the machines CR-27 describes lose the restart race too. Medium.


### comp-app-core-5: The downgrade floor file follows log_path, so the documented 'delete ~/.ccsync/upgrade_floor.json' recovery does nothing on a machine whose log was redirected

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/upgrade.py:175`

floor_path() returns config_mod.resolved_log_path(cfg).parent / 'upgrade_floor.json' (upgrade.py:173-175). The module comment and docs/RELEASE.md describe the file as living under the config dir (~/.ccsync) and name deleting it as the one manual way past a floor. resolved_log_path honours config.toml's `log_path`, so an install that set e.g. log_path = 'D:/logs/ccsync.log' keeps its floor at D:/logs/upgrade_floor.json. The refusal log line does print self._floor_file, which softens this, but the runbook and the tray/dashboard guidance point at the wrong path, and a machine whose log_path is later changed silently forgets its floor (a new, empty location) -- which is also a quiet way to lose the monotonic guarantee.

**Failure scenario.** Admin configured log_path to a second drive on an editor's PC. A deliberate rollback below the floor is needed; following docs/RELEASE.md they delete ~/.ccsync/upgrade_floor.json (which does not exist), restart, and the rollback is still refused. Conversely, editing log_path to a new folder resets the floor to 'none' without anyone deleting anything.

**Suggested fix.** Anchor the floor at config_mod.CONFIG_DIR / FLOOR_FILENAME (the directory identity.json and eula_accepted.json already use), independent of log_path; keep resolved_log_path for the log only. Update the comment and docs/RELEASE.md if the path choice is intentional.

**Verifier.** floor_path (upgrade.py:173-175) returns resolved_log_path(cfg).parent / 'upgrade_floor.json'; resolved_log_path (config.py:1827-1839) honours cfg['log_path'], default '~/.ccsync/companion.log' (config.py:260). identity.py:76 and eula.py:139 anchor their files at config_mod.CONFIG_DIR instead. The module comment (upgrade.py:166-170, 200) and docs/RELEASE.md:677,693 both name ~/.ccsync/upgrade_floor.json. So with a custom log_path the documented deletion does nothing and a later log_path change silently resets the floor. Only bites on machines with a redirected log_path (none known in the field) and the refusal message prints the real path, so low is correct.


## Companion: loopback servers, b-roll/music ingest (3 high / 1 medium / 2 low)

> **Coverage.** Read end to end: docs/LOOPBACK_API.md, loopback_guard.py, broll_server.py (all 2124 lines: vetting, CORS, body caps, PUT upload path, ingest dispatch, /insert, mounts, containment), music_server.py, broll_fetch.py, broll_upload.py, broll_ingest.py (all 2531 lines), music_ingest.py, broll_ingest_media.py, ingest_kinds.py, sidecar_tools.py, broll_vlm_sidecar.py, music_clap_sidecar.py, music_worker.py, stills.py, bpg.py (watch-folder + launcher halves), broll_vlm/local_vlm.py server lifecycle; skimmed broll_vlm/local_runtime.py (download_verified) by structure only. Followed the ingest path across the boundary into broll/web/app/routes_fleet.py and ingest_batches.py (claim idempotency, lease = 300 s, _leaseholder_or_410, write_item_result, mark_uploaded) and into app.py's _ingest_deps/effective_mode wiring. Ran the companion suite filtered to the area (-k broll/music/loopback/ingest/sidecar/bpg/clap/vlm): 878 passed, 1 skipped. The origin allow-list, Host/DNS-rebinding check, token path, content-type rules, PUT cap/partial/rename and share/rel_path traversal rules all held up against the scenarios I tried; I found no bypass there. Not covered: ytdl_server.py / ytdl_executor.py (listed under other areas), broll_vlm/compact_format.py and contract.py, music_clap/mel_numpy.py numerics, the vendored local_runtime download internals, bpg's PowerShell/CIM probes on a live machine, and any live test against Resolve or the NAS (read-only audit). The ingest defects below are all in the unattended-batch state machine, which the suite exercises only through a FakeQueue that lands every upload instantly, so none of them is visible to the existing tests.


### comp-loopback-1: A cancelled or 410'd ingest batch leaves the UploadQueue permanently stopped, so every later batch in that companion session sits in 'uploading' forever

**HIGH, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/broll_ingest.py:1996`

BrollIngestor.cancel() (line 1096) and _lease_lost() (line 2212) both call _stop_uploads(), which calls broll_upload.UploadQueue.stop_all(). stop_all() sets self._stopped = True (broll_upload.py:323) and the worker _loop returns; _ensure_thread() (broll_upload.py:365) refuses to start a new thread while _stopped is set, and nothing ever clears it. But the orchestrator keeps the same queue object: _queue() (broll_ingest.py:1996) only constructs a queue when self._uploader is None, and neither cancel() nor _lease_lost() resets self._uploader. music_ingest.MusicIngestor._queue() (music_ingest.py:436) has the identical shape. So after the first cancel (tray, page, dashboard command, admin cancel arriving as 410, or an expired lease), every subsequent batch's enqueue() appends jobs to a list no thread will ever drain. Reproduced with the real UploadQueue and a landing runner: run -> cancel -> run second batch -> 5 ticks: item stage 'uploading', 4 jobs queued, queue thread None.

**Failure scenario.** Editor drops 10 clips, clicks cancel after the first one (or an admin cancels from the dashboard). Editor drops a new batch on the same day without restarting the tray. The new clips are proxied, described and POSTed to the server (stage 'uploading', 90%), then never go live: no rclone is ever spawned, _pump_uploads never sees them landed, _maybe_finish never releases the batch, and the heartbeat keeps the lease alive indefinitely. Any further drop is refused with 409 'this machine is already indexing another batch' (line 1363) until the editor cancels again and restarts the companion.

**Suggested fix.** In _stop_uploads() (or in cancel()/_lease_lost() after it), drop the dead queue: set self._uploader = None so _queue() builds a fresh one for the next batch; or give UploadQueue a way to be restarted (clear _stopped and _thread in a reset()). Also have stop_all() append the queued jobs it fails to self._failed so failures() reports them. Add a test that cancels a batch and runs a second one against the real UploadQueue with a runner seam.

**Verifier.** Traced: broll_upload.UploadQueue.stop_all() (broll_upload.py:318-331) sets _stopped=True and nothing in the class ever clears it; _ensure_thread (365-370) returns early while _stopped, so enqueue() appends to _queue with no worker. Reproduced with the real queue: after stop_all()+enqueue(), _thread is None and queued=1. self._uploader is assigned only in __init__ (broll_ingest.py:598) and _queue() (1998); cancel() (1096-1103), _lease_lost() (2212-2222) and stop() (1510) all call _stop_uploads() and never reset it, and the BrollIngestor lives for the whole tray process (start() at 1493). music_ingest._queue (427-442) has the same shape. Every post-cancel batch in that session reaches ITEM_UPLOADING and parks there; _next_item skips uploading items and _maybe_finish never releases. High stands: cancel from the tray/page/dashboard is a first-class action and the only recovery is restarting the companion.


### comp-loopback-2: A resumed batch is never re-claimed and never heartbeated, and resumed 'uploading' items are never re-queued: a tray restart mid-batch strands the batch and 409s every new one

**HIGH, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/broll_ingest.py:703`

_resume() sets batch['needs_claim'] = True (line 703) and its docstring and the module header promise 're-claim on the next tick', but nothing in the file ever reads needs_claim (grep: one write, zero reads). _start_heartbeat() is called only from run() (line 1438), so a resumed batch has no heartbeat thread, and the server's 300 s lease (broll/web/app/config.py LEASE_SECONDS) expires; the server's claim() is idempotent for the same machine precisely to support this restart, and it is never called. Separately, items restored at stage 'uploading' carry their `uploads` rels but the UploadQueue is a fresh in-memory object, so _pump_uploads (line 2060) sees every rel as 'missing' with nothing 'broken' and `continue`s forever; _next_item skips ITEM_UPLOADING, and nothing re-enqueues. Reproduced: run -> tick (item at 'uploading') -> new orchestrator over the same state dir -> 3 ticks: 0 claims, 0 heartbeats, heartbeat thread None, item still 'uploading', real queue empty.

**Failure scenario.** Case A: companion is restarted (upgrade, crash, reboot) while a clip's 40 GB original is uploading. After restart the item stays 'uploading' for ever; the server's lease expires after 5 minutes but no request is ever made that would surface the 410, so the companion believes it still holds the batch. The page shows the batch stuck, and a new drop is refused with 409 'already indexing another batch' (line 1363) until the editor finds the cancel action; restarting again just resumes the same stuck state file. Case B: restart while a clip is at 'proxying' and the tray is down for more than 5 minutes: the next checkpoint POST gets 410 lease_expired and _lease_lost drops the whole batch, so the 'resumes at the checkpoint' promise in the test name is only true for restarts under 5 minutes.

**Suggested fix.** In tick() (or at the top of _drain), when batch.get('needs_claim') is set: re-issue FleetClient.claim() with the stored tier/capabilities, merge the manifest (video_id/archive names are idempotent), clear the flag, call _start_heartbeat(), and on 403/409/410 call _lease_lost. After a successful re-claim, re-run _enqueue_uploads(item) for every item at ITEM_UPLOADING whose outputs still exist (the queue's enqueue is idempotent per rel). Extend test_a_companion_killed_mid_batch_resumes_from_the_checkpoint to assert a claim and a heartbeat happen and that the upload is re-queued.

**Verifier.** grep over broll_ingest.py: needs_claim is written once (703) and never read; _start_heartbeat() is called only from run() (1438), not from _resume/start/tick, so a resumed batch has no heartbeat and never re-claims (the docstring at 671-673 and the comment at 701-702 promise otherwise). Restored items at ITEM_UPLOADING keep their `uploads` rels but the fresh queue has no jobs, so _pump_uploads (2060-2090) sees every rel as missing-not-broken and continues each tick; _next_item (1685-1697) skips uploading. The existing test (test_broll_ingest.py:785-801) only asserts the stage survives the restart, not that anything progresses. Without a heartbeat the server's lease lapses and admin cancel_requested can never arrive either. High is fair: a tray upgrade/restart mid-batch is a routine path and it leaves a permanent 409 on the machine.


### comp-loopback-3: An upload that rclone fails leaves the item in 'uploading' for ever: never retried, never failed, batch never finishes

**HIGH, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/broll_ingest.py:2088`

_pump_uploads distinguishes 'missing' rels from 'broken' ones (rels present in queue.failures()), but for broken it only copies the error text onto the item and `continue`s (lines 2088-2090). It never calls _fail_item, never counts an attempt and never re-enqueues (UploadQueue.enqueue is idempotent only against queued/active jobs, so a re-enqueue would have worked). _next_item() skips ITEM_UPLOADING, _has_work() counts it as outstanding, so the gate stays RUNNING/NOTHING_TO_DO with the batch held and the heartbeat keeping the lease alive. Reproduced with FakeQueue.failed populated after the first tick: after 5 more ticks the item is still 'uploading' with error 'rclone exited with code 1', the batch is still held, nothing released, and run() of a new batch answers 409.

**Failure scenario.** Editor's NAS connection drops mid-upload (or the far side is out of space, or the staged original was deleted after describe). rclone exits non-zero, run_upload returns (False, err), the job lands in _failed. The item shows its error text on the page but stays at 'uploading' indefinitely; the batch never completes, and every new drop on that machine is refused with 409 until the editor cancels (which, per comp-loopback-1, then also kills uploads for the rest of the session).

**Suggested fix.** On `broken`: increment item['upload_attempts'] and, below MAX_UPLOAD_ATTEMPTS, re-enqueue the failed rels (remove them from the queue's _failed list or give UploadQueue a retry(rel) that re-queues a failed job); at the cap call _fail_item(item, error) so the batch can finish. Add a test where the queue reports a failure and assert the item is retried then failed rather than parked.

**Verifier.** broll_ingest.py:2085-2090: when a rel is in queue.failures() the pump copies the error onto the item and `continue`s; no _fail_item, no upload_attempts increment, no re-enqueue (those exist only on the 409 branch at 2110-2127). UploadQueue._loop (400-431) has no internal retry: run_upload failure goes straight to _finish -> _failed, and the only re-queue path is the NAS-unreachable prereq refusal, not an rclone non-zero exit. No retry route exists in broll_ingest (grep 'def retry' empty). _maybe_finish (2184-2195) counts the item as outstanding for ever while the heartbeat keeps the lease, so the batch is held and new drops 409. A dropped connection mid-upload is a plausible editor event; high stands.


### comp-loopback-4: b-roll _post_result ignores a refused /result: the clip is uploaded and goes live with no segments, and is never re-described

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/broll_ingest.py:1989`

BrollIngestor._post_result logs a warning when item_result() returns non-200 but still sets item['stage'] = ITEM_INDEXED (line 1993) and returns normally; _crunch_item then sets item['described'] = True and hands the clip to the upload queue. The server's write_item_result can refuse with 400 (_check_transition on an unexpected server-side state, a validation failure in the body such as a sprite field or segment shape, or a 5xx during the segment write); mark_uploaded (ingest_batches.py:953) does not require segments to exist, so it flips videos.status to 'indexed' anyway. The music subclass (music_ingest.py _post_result) does the right thing: clears `embedded` and fails the item. The b-roll path was written before that and never got the same treatment.

**Failure scenario.** Server answers 400/500 to /items/{uid}/result for one clip (e.g. a transient DB lock during the segments replace, or a transition refusal because the server's item state lagged). The companion uploads poster/sprite/proxy/original, mark_uploaded succeeds, the clip is searchable with no description, themes or category, and because item['described'] is True a retry of the item never re-runs the model; the editor sees a 'live' clip that no search can find and nothing in the UI says why.

**Suggested fix.** Mirror music_ingest._post_result: return a bool; on non-200 leave `described` unset, call _fail_item(item, detail) and return None from _describe/_crunch_item so the item is retried (describe again) up to MAX_ITEM_ATTEMPTS instead of being uploaded. Treat a 409 'unclaimed' as a reason to re-claim.

**Verifier.** broll_ingest.py:1989-1993: on status != 200 _post_result only logs, then unconditionally sets ITEM_INDEXED and returns None; the caller proceeds to mark described and enqueue uploads. music_ingest._post_result (369-415) by contrast clears `embedded`, calls _fail_item and returns False, and its caller checks the bool (334). broll/web/app/ingest_batches.mark_uploaded (953+) flips status off `ingesting` without checking segments exist. So a refused result yields a live, undescribed, unsearchable clip that is never re-described. Medium is right: it needs a server-side refusal, and the clip still lands in the archive.


### comp-loopback-5: A blank mount entry ('') is treated as the filesystem root and skips the containment check, so /insert, /music/send and /music/reveal can be pointed at any file

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/broll_server.py:568`

translate_path_with_root only probes/refuses when mounts.get(share) is None; an empty string passes `isinstance(root, str)` (line 523), becomes root_norm '/' on posix (line 537) or '' + '\\' on Windows, and the translated path is '/<rel>' or '\\<rel>'. contained_local_path then does `if root:` (line 568), which is False for '', so the realpath containment check is skipped entirely and the path is returned. Verified: contained_local_path('broll', 'etc/passwd', {'broll': ''}) returns '\\etc\\passwd' on Windows and translate_path_with_root(..., platform='darwin') returns ('', '//etc/passwd'). resolve_mounts only overwrites a blank 'broll' entry when local_root is set; resolve_music_mounts likewise; any other share name with a blank entry is served as-is. The README written into ~/.broll-companion.json and the MED-11 comment both say a blank entry 'counts as absent', which is not what the translator does.

**Failure scenario.** An editor's old ~/.broll-companion.json from the standalone-companion days carries the template `"mounts": {"broll": ""}` and the machine has no local_root yet (first run, or a de-tenanted build before onboarding). A page on the allowed dashboard origin (or anyone with the loopback token) POSTs /insert with share 'broll' and rel_path 'Users/<name>/Desktop/anything.mov': the companion imports that file into Resolve, or for /music/reveal opens Explorer/Finder on it. Normal deployments are unaffected because the derived default replaces the blank entry.

**Suggested fix.** In translate_path_with_root, treat a blank/whitespace mount (`not root.strip()`) exactly like None: probe on darwin, else raise MountNotConfiguredError. In contained_local_path, always run the containment check (the root is never legitimately empty once the above holds). Add a test for mounts={'broll': ''} on both platforms.

**Verifier.** Reproduced on this machine: contained_local_path('broll','Users/x/a.mov',{'broll':''}) returns '\\Users\\x\\a.mov' and translate_path_with_root(..., platform='darwin') returns ('', '//etc/passwd'). translate_path_with_root (broll_server.py:518-537) only refuses a None root; '' passes isinstance(str) and becomes the filesystem root; contained_local_path's `if root:` (568) skips containment for ''. resolve_mounts (353-366) and resolve_music_mounts (music_server.py:85-100) replace a blank entry only when local_root derives a default, and only for their own share name; no test covers a blank mount. Reachability needs the allowed dashboard origin or the loopback token plus a blank entry with no local_root (pre-onboarding or a hand-edited file), so low is the honest severity; the docstring's 'a blank one counts as absent' is false at the translator.


### comp-loopback-6: POST /broll/ingest/prepare accepts any local path for source='path' items; the 'allowed roots' refusal the plan and docstring describe does not exist

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/broll_ingest.py:2359`

prepare()'s docstring says refusals are per item for 'a path outside the allowed roots', and docs/BROLL_INGEST_PLAN.md line 177 lists 'path outside allowed roots' as a prepare refusal, but _path_refusal (line 2359) checks only os.path.isfile and the extension. The plan also says /pick is 'THE ONLY route that learns a local path', yet prepare takes `path` straight from the JSON body with no check that it came from a pick. So the dashboard-origin page (or any token holder) can name any readable video on the machine and have the companion hash it, make a proxy, run the model on it, POST the description to the fleet and rclone the original into the shared NAS archive. The origin allow-list confines this to the dashboard's own pages, so it is defence in depth rather than an open hole, but the written contract is not what the code enforces.

**Failure scenario.** A bug or XSS in the b-roll page (or a leaked loopback token used by another local process) posts prepare with items [{source:'path', path:'D:/Private/family.mp4', name:'family.mp4'}] followed by run: the editor's machine indexes and uploads a file they never picked into the customer's shared archive, and the page's preview grid fetches its thumbnail via /broll/ingest/thumb.

**Suggested fix.** Have pick_ingest_sources record the paths (or their top-level folders) it returned in the orchestrator, and have _path_refusal accept a source='path' item only when it realpath-contains inside one of those picked roots (or, failing that, inside local_root / a configured allow-list). Update the docstring/plan if the decision is to keep it open.

**Verifier.** prepare() (broll_ingest.py:1162+) takes source='path' items straight from the JSON body (lines ~1222-1231) and the only check is _path_refusal (2359-2371): os.path.isfile plus the kind's extension list. No picked-roots record, no local_root containment; docs/BROLL_INGEST_PLAN.md:177 and the prepare docstring both list 'path outside the allowed roots' as a refusal that does not exist. Defence-in-depth only (origin allow-list / token gate the route), so low.


## Companion: YouTube stack and media tooling (3 medium / 2 low)

> **Coverage.** Read end to end: companion/src/ccsync_companion/{ytdl_server,ytdl_executor,ytdl_common,ytdl_cookies,ytdl_browser_login,ytdl_attestation,ytdlp_manager,sidecar_tools,youtube_import,ffmpeg_tools,proxy_gen,proxy_scan,consolidate}.py, the /ytdl dispatch in broll_server.py, app._ytdl_deps, site.feature_enabled/FEATURE_KEYS, config.load's merge (unknown keys such as ytdl_player_client survive, so the CR-39 lever works), broll_fetch's fetch_refusal/poll_fetch/build_fetch_command, rclone_lane filter/command signatures used by consolidate, and on the dashboard side ytdl/web/ytdlweb/routes_fleet.py in full, db.py lease/CAS/mark_pending/requeue functions, worker._reclaim_local_job/_await_local_claim/_phase_download, and app.js's probe/dispatch/local-switch code. Checked CR-29..CR-39 against code: probe retry (PROBE_RETRY_MS), grace period (_await_local_claim), clear_mode_lock clearing all four columns, runUrls dispatching, DOWNLOAD accepting done, CR-31 transport retry, CR-39 player_client argv and _fail_clip logging, /ytdl/fetch -- all present as the ledger says. KNOWN_BUGS CR-29..CR-45 consulted. Ran the companion suite read-only for the area (-k "ytdl or proxy or consolidate or ffmpeg or youtube or sidecar"): 1020 passed, 0 skipped/failed. Not covered: yt-dlp's actual behaviour with web_safari + cookies (needs a live run), worker.duplicate_location / ytsearch internals, the loopback_guard origin checks (other area), macOS-only root_guard probing on a real Mac.


### comp-ytdl-1: Local YouTube executor ignores the root guard: on a Mac with the SSD unplugged it creates the destination on the boot volume and downloads there

**MEDIUM (hunter said high), CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/ytdl_executor.py:1420`. Related: COMMERCIAL_READINESS item 5 (on-demand fetch bypasses root guard) -- the same gap left open in the executor

DownloadJob._download_all resolves the destination with destination_for() and then does Path(outdir).mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True) followed by yt-dlp writes, with no check that the editor's tree is actually mounted. Deps (lines 404-422) has no root-presence seam and app._ytdl_deps (app.py:4954-4967) passes none, even though app.root_is_present is wired into the youtube importer, proxy generator and every lane (app.py:873-1170), and the sibling /ytdl/fetch route gets the same protection via broll_fetch.fetch_refusal (broll_fetch.py:181-222, added 2026-08-17 for exactly this failure). Nothing upstream stops it either: capabilities() does not look at the disk; its free-space number comes from free_bytes_at(projects_root) (line 667), which walks up to the first EXISTING ancestor, so for /Volumes/T7/Creators_Club/Projects with T7 unplugged it reports the boot volume's free space and passes; _label_is_ours for an editor consults the cached selection labels, not the disk. root_guard.py's header documents the consequence: macOS happily creates /Volumes/<Name>/... on the internal disk, fills it, and the next replug mounts the real drive at '/Volumes/T7 1' (ROOT_MISPLACED), taking every sync lane down. macOS builds now ship from CI and sidecar_tools carries darwin pins, so Mac editors are on this path.

**Failure scenario.** Mac editor with local_root=/Volumes/T7/Creators_Club ejects the SSD but leaves the tray running, then opens the downloader page and presses DOWNLOAD (or pastes a link). Probe -> capabilities ok (free_bytes is the boot disk's), claim succeeds, manifest arrives, mkdir creates /Volumes/T7/Creators_Club/Projects/<label>/Youtube/<term> on the internal disk, and 20 clips (GBs) land there. Lane A never uploads them (the lane is parked by the root guard), the ledger says the editor's machine has them, and when the SSD is replugged macOS mounts it at /Volumes/T7 1 because the directory is occupied -- the misplaced-root outage root_guard.py exists to prevent.

**Suggested fix.** Give Deps a root_present_fn (app passes self.root_is_present, as every other feature does) and refuse in capabilities() with a new REASON_TREE_ABSENT when it is False; re-check it in _download_all before mkdir, and route the macOS case through root_guard.probe_root exactly as broll_fetch.fetch_refusal does (ROOT_ABSENT/ROOT_MISPLACED refuse, ROOT_UNKNOWN passes). Add a test that a missing/misplaced root yields ok:false and no mkdir.

**Verifier.** Traced end to end. ytdl_executor.Deps (lines 404-422) has no root-presence seam and app._ytdl_deps (app.py:4954-4967) passes none; capabilities() (637-698) checks feature flag, dashboard, editor, identity, attestation, yt-dlp status, ffmpeg and a disk_usage via free_bytes_at, which walks UP to the first existing ancestor (539-556) and so answers for the boot volume when /Volumes/T7 is absent. DownloadJob._download_all (1410-1425) goes straight from destination_for to Path(outdir).mkdir(parents=True), and on macOS /Volumes is writable so the mkdir succeeds on the internal disk. Nothing in ytdl_server.py's claim path consults root_guard either (only /ytdl/fetch does, via broll_fetch.fetch_refusal at ytdl_server.py:408, added 2026-08-17 for exactly this). root_guard.py's header describes the resulting ROOT_MISPLACED outage. Severity lowered to medium rather than high: it needs a Mac editor on a youtube_download+attested site, with the SSD ejected while the tray is up, pressing DOWNLOAD from the page - the owner rated the identical gap in on-demand fetch (COMMERCIAL_READINESS item 5) M-tier. The consequence (GBs on the boot disk plus a misplaced-root outage on replug) is real, so it is not low.


### comp-ytdl-2: ensure_ffmpeg_pair has no caller outside sidecar_tools.ensure(), so a site with youtube_download off never gets the managed ffmpeg that proxy generation and b-roll ingest depend on

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/sidecar_tools.py:440`. Related: docs/BROLL_INGEST_PLAN.md §3.3 item (claims fixed)

ensure() returns at line 440-453 the moment ytdlp_manager.youtube_enabled(cfg) is False, BEFORE it reaches ensure_ffmpeg_pair (line 457). The 2026-08-18 refactor (docstring lines 352-362 and docs/BROLL_INGEST_PLAN.md lines 57/158) says the ffmpeg pair was 'factored out with no youtube gate' because 'b-roll ingest needs ffmpeg regardless' -- but grep shows the only call into this module from the rest of the companion is ytdlp_manager.py:925 (sidecar_tools.ensure, on the YtDlpManager thread), and that thread does not even start when the feature is off (ytdlp_manager.start, lines 880-884). Nothing in broll_ingest.py or proxy_gen.py calls ensure_ffmpeg_pair. ffmpeg_tools._managed_binary (ffmpeg_tools.py:99-123) falls back to the tools dir, so both features would work if the binary were there; it never is on the vendor build.

**Failure scenario.** A customer deploys the vendor build (site.toml [features] youtube_download absent/false, the documented default). Editors enable proxy generation or use the b-roll drag-and-drop ingest: broll_ingest._check_ffmpeg (broll_ingest.py:761-770) and proxy_gen._check_ffmpeg (proxy_gen.py:948-969) both resolve 'ffmpeg' via PATH then the tools dir, find nothing, and the machine sits in STATE_NO_FFMPEG / 'no-ffmpeg' forever; the log says 'proxy generation is on but ffmpeg not found on PATH' and nothing on the machine will ever install one, contrary to the docs and the ingest plan.

**Suggested fix.** Call sidecar_tools.ensure_ffmpeg_pair(cfg) from a thread that runs regardless of the YouTube flag -- e.g. have YtDlpManager.start()/_loop always run and gate only the yt-dlp/deno steps on youtube_enabled, or call ensure_ffmpeg_pair from the proxy generator's/ingestor's first no-ffmpeg tick. Add a test asserting ensure_ffmpeg_pair is reached with youtube_download off.

**Verifier.** grep across companion/src confirms the only caller of ensure_ffmpeg_pair is sidecar_tools.ensure() (line 457), which returns at 440-453 when ytdlp_manager.youtube_enabled(cfg) is False; the only caller of ensure() is ytdlp_manager._loop (925), whose thread start() refuses to create when enabled is False (880-884). broll_ingest.py, proxy_gen.py and broll_vlm_sidecar.py reference sidecar_tools only in comments. So on a site with youtube_download off (the vendor default) the pinned ffmpeg is never fetched, and ffmpeg_tools' tools-dir fallback finds nothing. The comment at sidecar_tools.py:445-449 ('which b-roll ingest and the proxy generator call whether or not YouTube is on'), docs/BROLL_INGEST_PLAN.md lines 57/158, and the KNOWN_BUGS.md 'posture note' at line 484-489 ('every editor machine now fetches the pinned ffmpeg-static build') all describe a wiring that does not exist. Tests in test_sidecar_tools.py exercise ensure_ffmpeg_pair directly and never assert a caller. Medium: b-roll ingest and proxy generation sit in no-ffmpeg on a vendor-build editor machine unless the editor installs their own ffmpeg.


### comp-ytdl-3: YtDlpManager.start() is a permanent no-op when the site flag is off or the manifest cache is absent at tray launch; turning youtube_download on later needs a tray restart on every machine

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/ytdlp_manager.py:882`

start() checks self.enabled (youtube_enabled -> site.feature_enabled('youtube_download'), which reads the cached manifest and FAILS CLOSED on a missing cache) and returns without creating the thread. app.py calls ytdlp.start() exactly once (app.py:4903); the site-refresh thread (app.py:4835) updates the cache but nothing re-invokes start(), and the daily re-evaluation of `enabled` lives inside _loop, which never began. The capability probe then reads status() == {'ok': False, 'message': 'not checked yet'} (lines 531-536), so capabilities() answers REASON 'not checked yet' and no yt-dlp/ffmpeg/deno is ever installed, until the companion process restarts.

**Failure scenario.** Operator enables [features] youtube_download (and youtube_unblock) in site.toml and redeploys the dashboard. Editors' trays pick up the manifest on the next refresh and show the YouTube tray items, but every DOWNLOAD falls back to the server with the toast 'Downloading on the server: not checked yet', and the log carries only the startup line 'local YouTube downloads are disabled by config'. Same on a fresh install whose first tray start ran before the site cache was written. Resolves only on the next companion upgrade/restart.

**Suggested fix.** Always start the manager thread and let _loop/ensure() apply the gate per pass (ensure already returns ACTION_DISABLED when off), or have the site refresh callback call ytdlp.start() when the flag flips to on. Make the status message distinguish 'feature off for this site' from 'not checked yet'.

**Verifier.** YtDlpManager.start() (ytdlp_manager.py:880-884) returns without creating the thread when self.enabled (youtube_enabled -> site.feature_enabled, fails closed on a missing cache) is False. app.py invokes start() once via _start_ytdlp_manager (4903) during App.start(); the site refresh is a fire-and-forget thread (app.py:4834-4837, site_mod.refresh_site) with no callback, and grep finds no other ytdlp.start() call. The daily re-evaluation lives in _loop, which never began. Status stays at the constructor's {'ok': False, 'message': 'not checked yet'} (531-536); after the flag flips, capabilities() passes the youtube_enabled check at line 674 and then refuses at 689-691 with that message, so every DOWNLOAD falls to the server until a tray restart. Also affects a first start that ran before the site cache was written. Medium as reported: a real operator path (turning the feature on for a fleet) silently does nothing until every machine restarts its tray.


### comp-ytdl-4: CR-31 transport retry re-sends non-idempotent clip status POSTs, so a processed-but-unanswered request double-counts dl_done / dl_failed

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/ytdl_executor.py:771`. Related: CR-31

FleetClient._call retries on any raised transport failure, including a read timeout (HTTP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=15) that fires AFTER the server has processed the request. routes_fleet.clip_status is not idempotent: a 'done' runs _record_done -> db.bump(c, job_id, 'dl_done') (routes_fleet.py:596) and a 'failed' runs db.bump(c, job_id, 'dl_failed') (routes_fleet.py:557) on every call, and db.set_video does not guard on the previous state. For a non-last clip the retried POST passes _leaseholder_or_410 and bumps again; _hand_back_to_the_server later subtracts only the number of rows it requeues (line 630), so the drift persists into the job's final counters.

**Failure scenario.** Dashboard is slow (e.g. the NAS mount stalls a concurrent rglob in download_manifest on the single uvicorn worker) so the companion's 'done' POST for clip 5 of 22 is processed but the response arrives after 15 s; urllib raises timeout, _call retries, the server bumps dl_done again. The SPA's progress bar (app.js:909-910 uses dl_done + dl_failed over dl_total) shows 23/22 and the history panel's counts are off by one for that job.

**Suggested fix.** Make clip_status idempotent server-side (bump only when the row's dl_state actually transitions, e.g. UPDATE ... WHERE dl_state<>'done' and bump on rowcount), or have the executor send a per-attempt idempotency key the route dedupes on.

**Verifier.** FleetClient._call (ytdl_executor.py:771-800) retries any exception from deps.request; default_request (290-330) lets urllib's timeout propagate as an exception, so a request the server processed but answered late is re-sent within CALL_RETRY_BUDGET_SECONDS. routes_fleet.clip_status: a 'done' calls _record_done -> db.bump(c, job_id, 'dl_done') (routes_fleet.py:596), a 'failed' calls db.bump(..., 'dl_failed') (557); db.bump is an unconditional += (db.py:522-528) and db.set_video's _update has no state predicate, so a retried POST on a non-last clip passes _leaseholder_or_410 and double-counts. ledger_add is an upsert (db.py:1248-1264), so the ledger itself is not duplicated - only the progress counters drift. _hand_back_to_the_server subtracts only requeued rows (630). Low is right: cosmetic counter drift in an outage-only edge case.


### comp-ytdl-5: Base-rig project-label check stats a casefolded path, so on a case-sensitive filesystem every label with an upper-case letter is refused

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/ytdl_executor.py:1471`

_label_is_ours on a mode='base' machine builds `Path(root) / Path(*wanted.split('/'))` where `wanted` is normalize_label(label), which .casefold()s the string (line 536). The folded spelling is then used for here.is_dir(). That is fine on NTFS/default APFS but wrong on a case-sensitive volume (Linux, case-sensitive APFS): '2026/FF5/Energy Transition' becomes '2026/ff5/energy transition', is_dir() is False, and the job is refused with 'names project ... which is not in this base rig's tree'. Since 2026-08-19 more than one wired machine can be mode=base (commit 13503d5), so this is no longer only the Windows base rig.

**Failure scenario.** A wired Mac (mode='base', local_root on a case-sensitive APFS volume or an SMB mount exposing the NAS's case-sensitive ZFS dataset) is dispatched a job for project '2026/FF5/Energy Transition'. capabilities says ok, the claim succeeds, the manifest arrives, _label_is_ours stats '.../Projects/2026/ff5/energy transition', gets False, downloads nothing and lets the lease expire; the server reclaims after 180 s and the editor's machine never downloads anything on that rig.

**Suggested fix.** Use the manifest's original label spelling (split on both separators, not casefolded) for the filesystem probe, keeping the casefolded form only for the set comparison against selection labels; or compare against a casefolded listing of the projects dir.

**Verifier.** normalize_label (ytdl_executor.py:523-536) casefolds, and _label_is_ours on mode='base' (1469-1477) builds Path(root)/Path(*wanted.split('/')) from that casefolded string and calls is_dir(); destination_for uses project_rel_path separately, so the probe path and the write path differ in case. On NTFS and default APFS is_dir() is case-insensitive so the field's Windows base rig is unaffected; on a case-sensitive volume (case-sensitive APFS, or a macOS SMB mount of a ZFS dataset with casesensitivity=sensitive) any label with an upper-case letter is refused and the lease expires. Since 13503d5 more than one machine can be mode=base, but no such machine exists in the field today. Low is correct.


## Dashboard: core (1 high / 2 medium / 4 low)

> **Coverage.** Read end to end: auth.py, sessions.py, app.py, oidc.py, secrets_boot.py, runtime_id.py, health.py, schema.sql, db.py (migrations v2-v26, selections/machines/plan helpers, machine_state upsert, prune/evict, fleet halt, backlog query), collector.py (loop, config, enforce, completion, remoteneed, prune), api.py (credential resolution, login/verify/me, selection routes, report ingestion + commands block, admin users/sessions/report-tokens, machine push-update / resume-lane-b / copy-plan, packages publish/download/current, health/site, queue + editors + transfers view builders), ui.py login/logout/toggle/admin partials. Followed the companion side for /verify report_token adoption (identity.preferred_report_token) and commands.upgrade handling (companion app.py). Reproduced dash-core-1 empirically against a scratch DB with the real db helpers (scratchpad/bucket_probe.py). Not covered in depth: site_store/setup_engine/setup_api/release_feed/dashboard_update/package_store/nas backends (outside the area), the api.py project-setup/adopt_folder tree routes, and the remaining 1800 lines of ui.py templates rendering. The dashboard pytest suite (62 files) was started read-only with -x; at the time of writing it was 29% through with zero failures and still running (it is slow on this machine), so I cannot report its final tally. KNOWN_BUGS.md was checked through CR-45; none of the findings below is listed there.


### dash-core-1: A tick for a machine that was inheriting the unassigned bucket silently drops every inherited project from that machine's plan (and enforce unshares them)

**HIGH, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/db.py:1886`. Related: MULTI_MACHINE_PLAN.md / CR-42 area (per-machine plans), not previously listed

`selections_for_machine` (db.py:1886-1900) and `fetch_machine_selections` (db.py:1964-1990) give a machine the `machine=''` bucket ONLY while it has zero rows of its own. `add_selection_for_person` (db.py:1841) and `api_tick` (api.py:1561-1608) write a new row straight onto the machine without first materialising the bucket it was inheriting. The first own-row therefore eclipses the whole inherited plan. The reverse is also broken: `remove_selection(machine=M)` on an inherited project deletes 0 rows (api_untick answers changed=false, project stays ticked), and unticking the one own-row makes the bucket reappear. The bucket is reached in the normal onboarding flow: an admin ticks projects for an editor before that editor's companion has ever reported (api_tick comment: 'gets the unassigned bucket, which their first report inherits'), and v24's migration also leaves bucket rows for any editor with no registry entry. Reproduced with the real helpers: bucket {A,B} -> first report registers LAPTOP -> selections_for_machine == [A,B] -> add_selection_for_person('C') -> selections_for_machine == [C], fetch_machine_selections == {'C': [(editor, LAPTOP)]}.

**Failure scenario.** Admin assigns projects A and B to new editor 'newbie' on the assignments page before newbie's companion first reports (rows land in the '' bucket). Companion reports; LAPTOP is registered and its GET /selection?machine=LAPTOP returns A,B; lane C is shared and lane B downloads proxies. A day later the admin ticks project C for newbie via the sidebar checkbox (person-level) or ?machine=LAPTOP. LAPTOP's plan becomes exactly [C]; the next enforce cycle (collector._run_enforce, selections from fetch_machine_selections) removes LAPTOP's device from folders A and B (2 removals, under the brake), the companion's sequencer stops lane A/B for A and B, and the editor can no longer open the projects they were working on. Nothing is logged as an error. Conversely DELETE /selection/newbie/A?machine=LAPTOP before any own-row returns changed=false and A keeps syncing.

**Suggested fix.** When the first own-row is written for (editor, machine), copy the bucket's rows onto that machine first (in add_selection/add_selection_for_person, or in _register_machine on the machine's first report: materialise db.selections_for_machine(editor, machine) into own rows and stop inheriting). Likewise make remove_selection(machine=M) materialise-then-delete when M is inheriting. Add a test: bucket -> register -> tick C -> plan must be {A,B,C}; and bucket -> untick A?machine=M -> plan {B}.

**Verifier.** Traced and reproduced against the real helpers (db.connect + db.migrate on a scratch DB): bucket {a,b} via add_selection_for_person with no machine on record -> upsert_machine('LAPTOP') -> selections_for_machine == ['a','b'] and fetch_machine_selections maps a,b to ('newbie','LAPTOP') -> add_selection_for_person('c') -> selections_for_machine == ['c'] and fetch_machine_selections == {'c': [...]} only; remove_selection('a', machine='LAPTOP') returns False. db.py:1957-1973 (selections_for_machine) and db.py:2031-2068 (fetch_machine_selections, `if (editor, machine) in own: continue`) implement inherit-only-while-empty; add_selection (db.py:1896) / add_selection_for_person (db.py:1916) / api_tick (api.py:1595-1603) write the first own row without materialising the bucket; _register_machine (api.py:3506) only calls upsert_machine and never copies the bucket onto the new machine. The bucket is reachable in normal use: api_tick with target None for an editor with no registered machine (the comment at api.py:1599 says the first report 'inherits' it) and the collector's seed (collector.py:789) writes ANY_MACHINE for unknown devices. collector._run_enforce uses fetch_machine_selections (collector.py:851), so the next cycle unshares A and B from LAPTOP (2 removals, well under the brake). Test test_the_unassigned_bucket_applies_only_where_there_is_no_plan pins the eclipsing rule but never the tick-after-inherit transition. Not in KNOWN_BUGS. High stands: an editor silently loses the projects they were syncing.


### dash-core-2: /api/v1/verify hands out the shared DASH_REPORT_TOKEN even when DASH_SHARED_REPORT_TOKEN_ENABLED=0, so a freshly signed-in companion adopts a credential every report will refuse

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:1373`. Related: CR-18 follow-up (per-editor tokens migration)

`api_verify` returns `"report_token": settings.report_token` unconditionally (api.py:1373). `resolve_companion_credential` (api.py:146-149) refuses that same token whenever `shared_report_token_enabled` is False, and app.py's pre-body gate (`_report_auth_denial`, app.py:399-422) 401s it before the body is read. The companion stores what /verify returns in identity.json (`identity.py` write_identity/_adopt_report_token) and `preferred_report_token` falls back to it whenever no cce1 token has been placed, which is the state of every machine that has not been hand-issued one (docs/GOTCHAS 'per-editor report tokens': delivery is manual).

**Failure scenario.** Operator follows the documented migration: mints cce1 tokens for existing machines, sees the boot log say every machine is on per-editor tokens, sets DASH_SHARED_REPORT_TOKEN_ENABLED=0. A new editor then runs the wizard / tray Sign in: /verify succeeds, returns role + the shared token, the companion adopts it, and every POST /api/v1/report from that machine is answered 401 'bad or missing X-CCSync-Token'. The machine never appears on the fleet grid, GET /selection fails the same way so nothing syncs, and the tray shows a generic report failure; nothing on the dashboard points at the retired token because the machine never got far enough to write a report_auth row.

**Suggested fix.** In api_verify, return report_token only when settings.shared_report_token_enabled (else "" or omit), and add a `report_token_kind`/hint so the tray can say 'ask your admin for a per-editor token'. Longer term, let /verify mint and return a cce1 token bound to the just-verified editor (the seam the report-token comment at api.py:2525-2533 already names).

**Verifier.** api.py:1373 returns `settings.report_token` unconditionally; resolve_companion_credential (api.py:146-149) refuses it when settings.shared_report_token_enabled is False (settings.py:532 maps DASH_SHARED_REPORT_TOKEN_ENABLED=0). The companion writes it to identity.json (identity.py:338) and preferred_report_token (identity.py:160-162) falls back to 'identity.json (shared)' whenever no cce1 token has been placed, then _adopt_report_token publishes it as cfg['dashboard_token']. KNOWN_BUGS CR-18 states /verify must never mint a cce1 token (by design) but says nothing about it continuing to hand out the retired shared token, and docs/GOTCHAS.md:767 instructs operators to set =0 once the boot list is empty, so a new editor onboarded after that point is a real path. Medium is right: the new machine never reports and the failure is opaque, but it needs an operator who has completed the migration.


### dash-core-3: Disabling a local account leaves its live sessions and per-editor report tokens working until they expire

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:2374`

`api_admin_disable_user` (api.py:2374-2391) and ui `partial_admin_disable_user` (ui.py:1258-1283) only flip `users.disabled`. `auth._resolve_session` (auth.py:447-474) validates a cookie purely against the `auth_sessions` row; `local_users.verify_password` checks `disabled` only at LOGIN time, and `verify_editor_report_token` (db.py:1049-1066) never consults `users` at all. Only the DELETE route calls `_purge_user_credentials`. Compare: an admin's `is_local_admin` does check disabled, so the inconsistency is clearly unintended.

**Failure scenario.** DASH_AUTH_METHOD=local appliance. A contractor leaves; the admin clicks DISABLE on Admin > Users (the obvious non-destructive control) rather than DELETE. The contractor's open dashboard tab keeps working for up to 12 h idle / 7 d absolute (sessions.DEFAULT_IDLE/ABSOLUTE), can still tick/untick their projects, read their queue and download packages; their companion keeps reporting and pulling selections with its cce1 token indefinitely, so lane C shares stay in place. The admin has no indication the disable did not take effect.

**Suggested fix.** On disable=true, call the same `_purge_user_credentials` path delete uses (revoke sessions via session_store.revoke_user after commit, revoke the editor's report tokens), or make `_resolve_session` / `verify_editor_report_token` consult `users.disabled` in local mode. Add a test alongside test_local_auth_mode.test_disabled_local_account_cannot_log_in asserting an existing session 401s after disable.

**Verifier.** api_admin_disable_user (api.py:2375-2391) only calls local_users.disable_user (UPDATE users SET disabled) and commits; _purge_user_credentials (api.py:2394) is called only from the delete route (api.py:2445). auth._resolve_session (auth.py:447-474) validates the cookie purely via session_store.validate(sid) and never consults users; local_users.verify_password checks disabled only at login (local_users.py:236); db.verify_editor_report_token (db.py:1036-1056) checks only editor_report_tokens.revoked_at. One mitigation the report omits: is_local_admin (local_users.py:178) does check disabled, so a disabled ADMIN loses admin powers immediately; but editor-level session use and companion reports/selection reads continue until session expiry / indefinitely for the cce1 token. Medium is fair for a local-mode appliance.


### dash-core-4: A successful login clears the per-IP login budget, so one valid account resets the spray protection for every other username

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/auth.py:221`

`clear_login_failures` (auth.py:221-224 -> sessions.SessionStore.clear_failures, sessions.py:360-372) deletes BOTH the (user) row and the (ip) row. The IP budget exists specifically so 'spraying one password across a hundred usernames from one host' is bounded (sessions.py docstring, COMMERCIAL_READINESS item 15). Any caller who owns one valid account (an editor, or an attacker who has one set of credentials) can interleave a successful login of their own after every 4 failures and keep the IP counter at zero forever; only the per-username budget (5 failures then backoff) remains.

**Failure scenario.** Attacker on the tailnet with one editor account tries password P against usernames u1..u100: 4 failures, log in as self (IP row deleted), 4 more failures, log in as self, ... The IP budget never blocks. Each victim still only takes 4-5 guesses per hour window, so this is a weakening rather than a bypass, but it silently removes the second of the two budgets the design relies on.

**Suggested fix.** On success clear only the (SCOPE_USER, username) row; let the IP row age out through LOGIN_FAILURE_WINDOW_SECONDS (or decrement it by one instead of deleting).

**Verifier.** auth.clear_login_failures (auth.py:221-224) passes client_ip and sessions.SessionStore.clear_failures (sessions.py:390-401) DELETEs both the (SCOPE_USER, username) and (SCOPE_IP, ip) rows. Called after every successful login/verify (api.py:1271, api.py:1362, ui.py:323). So a caller with one valid account resets the per-IP spray budget at will; the per-username budget still applies. Weakening, not a bypass; low is right.


### dash-core-5: GET /api/v1/fleet/halt still accepts the retired shared token and rejects per-editor tokens

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:2618`

`api_fleet_halt` authenticates companions with `token_ok(settings.report_token, ...)` directly (api.py:2618) instead of `companion_token_ok`/`resolve_companion_credential`. So (a) when DASH_SHARED_REPORT_TOKEN_ENABLED=0 the shared token, which every other route now refuses, still unlocks this read, and (b) a companion holding only a cce1 token gets 401. Every other companion-facing route was moved behind resolve_companion_credential on 2026-08-17; this one was missed.

**Failure scenario.** Fleet fully migrated to cce1 tokens and the shared token disabled. A companion (or an operator scripting against the API with a per-editor token) calls GET /api/v1/fleet/halt to confirm the tray's 'your admin stopped syncing' banner and is told 'log in first'; meanwhile the leaked/retired shared token can still read the halt state. Read-only and non-secret, hence low.

**Suggested fix.** Replace the token_ok call with companion_token_ok(settings, conn, token).

**Verifier.** api_fleet_halt (api.py:2609-2622) uses token_ok(settings.report_token, ...) directly, bypassing resolve_companion_credential's shared_report_token_enabled check and not accepting cce1 tokens. Impact is even smaller than reported: the companion never calls GET /fleet/halt (it reads the halt from the report reply's commands block, companion app.py:1202 _apply_fleet_halt), so only hand scripting hits it; and the read is non-secret. Low.


### dash-core-6: A pushed update whose target version the machine never reports (it moved past it) is re-sent on every report forever and never clears

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:4458`. Related: MULTI_MACHINE_PLAN §9 / CR-40..41 area

The v25 request is retired only when `running == update_request['version']` (api.py:4458-4464). The companion side ignores the command whenever the offered build differs from `wanted` (companion app.py:4252-4263, logs once). If the machine ends up on any OTHER version - the editor clicked Update to a newer build published in between, or a newer build was made current and the admin's earlier push named the old one - the row in `machines.update_requested_*` stays set, `commands.upgrade` rides every 30 s report, and the packages page keeps showing the request as pending until an admin notices and cancels it by hand.

**Failure scenario.** Admin pushes 0.9.43 to ruskin/PC on Monday; ruskin is away, the machine is off. Tuesday ship publishes 0.9.44 and makes it current. Wednesday ruskin boots, the tray offers 0.9.44 and he clicks it; he is now on 0.9.44. Every report from then on carries commands.upgrade {version: 0.9.43}, the companion logs 'pushed update to v0.9.43 IGNORED' once, and the dashboard shows an outstanding push that can never complete.

**Suggested fix.** Clear the request when the reported version is >= the requested one (packaging.version / the companion's own version compare), or when the requested version is no longer the current package for that platform; and have api_push_machine_update overwrite/clear any stale request when a newer build becomes current.

**Verifier.** api.py:4457-4474 clears the request only on `running == update_request['version']` (exact string match); otherwise commands.upgrade is re-sent on every report. Companion app.py:4252-4263 ignores the command when the current offer's version differs from `wanted`, logging once. So a machine that lands on a newer build than the one pushed keeps a pending request forever until the admin cancels via DELETE /admin/machines/{editor}/{machine}/update (api.py:2698-2707). No re-apply or data risk; cosmetic/stale state. Low.


### dash-core-7: The `ccsync-dashboard` console entry point skips secrets_boot.ensure_secrets, unlike the run.sh --factory path

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/app.py:817`

`run()` (app.py:817-821) builds `Settings.from_env()` itself and passes it to `create_app(settings)`, and `create_app` only calls `secrets_boot.ensure_secrets()` when `settings is None` (app.py:191-202). The module docstring and secrets_boot.py both claim the bootstrap runs 'on the run() path a real container takes'; it does not - only deploy/run.sh's `uvicorn --factory ccsync_dashboard.app:create_app` gets it. pyproject.toml registers `ccsync-dashboard = ccsync_dashboard.app:run` as the installed script.

**Failure scenario.** An appliance-style start (`ccsync-dashboard`, or `python -m ccsync_dashboard.app`) with no DASH_SESSION_SECRET in the environment: instead of loading/generating `<data>/secrets/dash_session_secret`, Settings sees an empty secret, login is silently switched off (check_boot_secrets only validates a PRESENT secret), /api/v1/report accepts identity-less reports, and the sidecar env files are never refreshed. Today's shipped container uses run.sh so this is latent, which is why it is low.

**Suggested fix.** Have run() call secrets_boot.ensure_secrets() before Settings.from_env() (or call create_app() with no argument), and fix the two docstrings.

**Verifier.** app.py:817-821 run() builds Settings.from_env() itself and passes it in; create_app only calls secrets_boot.ensure_secrets() when settings is None (app.py:190-202). secrets_boot.py's docstring and the create_app comment both claim the bootstrap runs on the run() path, which is false. pyproject.toml:147 registers `ccsync-dashboard = ccsync_dashboard.app:run`. The shipped container uses deploy/run.sh's `uvicorn --factory ccsync_dashboard.app:create_app` (run.sh:265, 279), which does hit the no-arg path, so this is latent today. Low is correct.


## Dashboard: admin / provisioning / setup (6 medium / 2 low)

> **Coverage.** Read end to end: assignments.py, provision.py, setup_api.py, setup_routes.py, setup_engine.py (all 1117 lines), site_store.py, settings.py, local_users.py, internal_sftp.py, truenas_client.py (shim), syncthing_client.py, tailscale_local.py, nas/__init__.py, nas/base.py, nas/factory.py, nas/truenas.py, nas/synology.py; followed the feature paths into collector._run_enforce (732-935), db.selections_for_machine / fetch_machine_selections / add_selection_for_person / base_only_editors / upsert_machine, api.py (api_site, the selection tick/untick routes, admin users routes, _register_machine, the queue pending block), app.py (_OPEN_EXACT, login_gate, lifespan seed), ui.page_setup, static/setup.js, static/site_settings.js, templates/setup.html and admin_settings.html, secrets_boot.py, deploy/compose.appliance.yaml / compose.yaml / compose.image.yaml mounts and env_files, companion site.py (FEATURE_KEYS / normalise), site.example.toml, KNOWN_BUGS CR-1..CR-45 headings and CR-28 body, docs/MULTI_BASE_RIG_PLAN.md (grep), docs/APPLIANCE_INSTALL.md (the "what is true today" section). Ran the dashboard suite read-only: 1456 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (477 s). Its storage-task tests lay out tmp_path/<root>/Projects, which is why the /projects-mount bug below never shows up there; its setup_routes tests assert the first-run window is closed by design. Not covered: server/ scripts themselves (snapshot_before call sites were only grep-verified: install_dashboard_app.py:3810 and setup_tree.py:266 both call it before their recursive/privileged steps), live TrueNAS/DSM behaviour, and the companion's half of the sync plan beyond site.py. FEATURE_KEYS: verified the dashboard publishes exactly youtube_download/youtube_unblock/auto_update and the whitelist names all three; no defect there. site.toml import/export round-trip was traced key by key and is consistent with site.example.toml's section names; no defect there beyond the unvalidated nas_kind noted below.


### dash-admin-1: Setup 'Storage check' task creates the shared asset folders inside the container's root filesystem, never on the NAS tree, so the required task can never go green on any real deployment

**MEDIUM (hunter said high), CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/setup_engine.py:529`

_run_storage derives the tree root as `root.parent` where `root = Path(settings.projects_dir)`, and _check_storage (line 484) does the same with `Path(projects_dir).parent`. Every compose file in the repo mounts ONLY the Projects directory, at `/projects` (deploy/compose.yaml:403, compose.image.yaml:221, compose.appliance.yaml:167, with DASH_PROJECTS_DIR=/projects). `Path('/projects').parent` is `/`, so the task mkdirs `/Assets/Luts` and `/Assets/Stills` in the container rootfs as APP_UID. The suite's tests build `tmp/<root>/Projects` so the parent really is the tree root there, which is why this passes 1456/1456.

**Failure scenario.** Admin opens /setup on the deployed TrueNAS dashboard and clicks [ DO IT ] on Storage check. The probe file in /projects succeeds, then `(/ / 'Assets/Luts').mkdir(parents=True)` either raises PermissionError (rootfs not writable by uid 3000) -> logged warning, `created` stays empty, yet the task still returns status ok with detail 'created 0 shared asset folder(s)'. The very next CHECK re-runs _check_storage, finds /Assets/Luts missing and flips the chip back to TODO 'not yet probed -- click Do it'. `storage` is not optional, so outstanding_required() never empties, `done` never reports ok and the Setup nav badge never clears. If the rootfs happens to be writable, the task instead reports ok while the real NAS tree still has no Assets/Luts or Assets/Stills, and the collector's shared-folder provisioning (which uses DASH_SYNCTHING_ASSETS_PREFIX, a different mount) is unaffected either way.

**Suggested fix.** Do not derive the asset root from projects_dir's parent. Either give the container an explicit assets mount/setting (the Syncthing side already has syncthing_assets_prefix; add a dashboard-side DASH_ASSETS_DIR mounted at <tree>/Assets) and create `<assets_dir>/<leaf>` there, or drop folder creation from the storage task and make the check purely 'projects_dir is writable'. Also make _run_storage return warn/fail when any mkdir failed instead of ok with 'created 0'. Add a test whose projects_dir is a directory whose parent is not the tree root.

**Verifier.** Traced. setup_engine.py:484 and :529 both take Path(projects_dir).parent as the tree root; every compose file mounts only <tree>/Projects at /projects (compose.yaml:403, compose.image.yaml:221, compose.appliance.yaml:167) with DASH_PROJECTS_DIR=/projects, so the target is /Assets/Luts in the container rootfs. _run_storage swallows the mkdir OSError and still returns status ok with 'created 0' (:533-539), and _check_storage's evidence is those very folders, so the chip flips back to todo on the next check; 'storage' is registered non-optional (:542). The only test (test_setup_engine.py:247) builds tmp/<root>/Projects, where the parent IS the tree root. Downgraded to medium: the /setup page is reachable by a logged-in admin on the live TrueNAS dashboard, but the wizard is an admin-only, not-yet-customer-facing surface (APPLIANCE_INSTALL.md marks the Storage task NOT YET TRUE) and no editor sync path depends on it; the real harm is a permanently unclearable setup badge, not data loss.


### dash-admin-2: Appliance compose: secrets-init, the dashboard's secrets bootstrap and the sftp sidecar each hold a different CCSYNC_INTERNAL_TOKEN (and the Syncthing API key flips on first boot), so the sidecar's AuthorizedKeysCommand is refused with 401 forever

**MEDIUM (hunter said high), CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/deploy/compose.appliance.yaml:112`

Three writers disagree on file names. secrets-init (compose.appliance.yaml:105-117) writes `internal.env` (CCSYNC_INTERNAL_TOKEN=B) and `syncthing.env` (STGUIAPIKEY=B1); the sftp service loads `internal.env` via env_file (line 263), syncthing loads `syncthing.env` (line 234). The dashboard service gets neither variable (lines 155-158 say so explicitly), so secrets_boot.ensure_secrets (secrets_boot.py:136) looks for `<data>/secrets/ccsync_internal_token` and `<data>/secrets/syncthing_api_key`, finds nothing, GENERATES new values A/A1, persists them under those names, and _write_sidecar_env_files (secrets_boot.py:188-196) writes `sftp.env` (which nothing reads) and OVERWRITES `syncthing.env` with A1. internal_sftp._configured_token's own file fallback (internal_sftp.py:409) reads yet a fourth name, `internal_token`, which no writer ever produces.

**Failure scenario.** First `docker compose up` on an appliance: syncthing starts with key B1 from secrets-init's file, the dashboard boots with A1 -> every /rest call 403s until someone restarts the syncthing container (it then re-reads the overwritten file and matches). The sftp sidecar starts with token B from internal.env; the dashboard's Settings.internal_token is A (from env, set by ensure_secrets); every `GET /internal/sftp/keys/<user>` from ccsync-keys.sh carries B, hmac.compare_digest(B, A) fails, the route logs 'refused ... bad or missing bearer token' and returns 401 -> sshd gets no authorized_keys -> no editor can ever authenticate to lanes A/B on the appliance. Subsequent boots do not heal it: the dashboard re-reads its own `ccsync_internal_token` file (A), secrets-init skips because `internal.env` exists (B).

**Suggested fix.** Pick one file per secret and make every party use it. Simplest: have secrets-init write `ccsync_internal_token` and `syncthing_api_key` (the names ensure_secrets reads) AND have the dashboard service env_file the same `internal.env`/`syncthing.env` that the sidecars read; or have ensure_secrets read/write `internal.env`/`syncthing.env` in KEY=VALUE form and make internal_sftp's fallback path `ccsync_internal_token`. Add an integration-style test that runs ensure_secrets against a secrets dir pre-populated the way secrets-init populates it and asserts the resulting sftp-side and dashboard-side tokens are equal.

**Verifier.** Traced end to end. secrets-init (compose.appliance.yaml:105-117) writes syncthing.env / internal.env; the sftp service's only env_file is internal.env (:262-263) and the syncthing service's is syncthing.env (:233-234); the dashboard service has NO env_file and no CCSYNC_INTERNAL_TOKEN / SYNCTHING_API_KEY in its environment (:137-150). app.py:201 calls ensure_secrets() which reads <data>/secrets/ccsync_internal_token and syncthing_api_key (secrets_boot.py:135-138), generates new values when absent, then _write_sidecar_env_files overwrites syncthing.env and writes sftp.env, which nothing reads (:188-196). internal_sftp._require_internal_token compares against settings.internal_token (env, generated A) with a file fallback named 'internal_token' (internal_sftp.py:56) that no writer produces. So the sidecar presents B, the dashboard expects A: every ccsync-keys.sh call 401s. Downgraded to medium only because compose.appliance.yaml is not deployed anywhere yet (APPLIANCE_INSTALL.md: the whole appliance stack is NOT YET TRUE); on the TrueNAS/image deploys in the field the token comes from compose environment and this does not bite. It will be high the day the appliance ships.


### dash-admin-3: template_folders / shared_asset_folders saved on Settings or in the wizard are published in the manifest but ignored by every dashboard code path that actually creates folders (the api_site comment claims the opposite)

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:1932`

site_store.resolved_manifest serves the DB row for template_folders/shared_asset_folders when one exists and api_site (api.py:1020-1028) comments that 'either way the tree an installer expects and the tree /project-setup creates cannot drift apart'. But create_tree_project (api.py:1932) iterates `provision.TEMPLATE_FOLDERS` (the import-time DASH_SITE_TEMPLATE_FOLDERS env value or the documentary defaults), ui.py:600 renders the same env list on the /project-setup page, collector.py:407 provisions `provision.SHARED_ASSET_FOLDERS` (env), and setup_engine._run_storage/_check_storage use provision.SHARED_ASSET_FOLDERS too. The wizard's required `studio` task (_STUDIO_REQUIRED_KEYS) makes the admin fill template_folders in, and the Settings form re-submits both fields on every save.

**Failure scenario.** Admin on an appliance (no DASH_SITE_* env) sets TEMPLATE FOLDERS to 'Footage, Audio, Graphics' in the wizard and saves; GET /api/v1/site now publishes that list and the task goes ok. An editor then uses /project-setup -> CREATE NEW PROJECT: the dashboard creates AE/, Audio/Music/, Audio/Voiceover/, B-roll/, Interviewees/, Render in Place/, Subs/, Youtube/ (the documentary defaults) and the page's preview showed those defaults too. Same for SHARED ASSET FOLDERS: add 'Assets/SFX', the manifest advertises folder id `assets-sfx`, the collector never creates or shares it and the storage task never lays it down.

**Suggested fix.** Resolve both lists through site_store.resolved_manifest at call time: create_tree_project and ui's project-setup page should read `manifest['template_folders']`, and the collector's shared-folder provisioning / enforce (SHARED_ASSET_FOLDER_IDS) and setup_engine's storage task should read `manifest['shared_asset_folders']` (the collector already has a DB connection per cycle). Alternatively make both fields read-only on the Settings page and drop template_folders from _STUDIO_REQUIRED_KEYS until the consumers are wired; either way fix the api.py:1020 comment.

**Verifier.** Traced. site_store.KEYS accepts template_folders/shared_asset_folders (:76-77), resolved_manifest prefers the DB row (:323-330), admin_settings.html:53-57 and setup.js:128 submit them, and _STUDIO_REQUIRED_KEYS (:436) requires template_folders. But create_tree_project iterates provision.TEMPLATE_FOLDERS (api.py:1933, import-time env/default), ui.py:600 renders the same, collector.py:407 provisions provision.SHARED_ASSET_FOLDERS, and the enforce cycle keys on provision.SHARED_ASSET_FOLDER_IDS; none consult resolved_manifest. The api.py:1020-1025 comment claiming the two cannot drift is wrong for the DB-override case. Medium is right: editors on an env-configured site see no change, but any site that edits the field via Settings (available today on the live dashboard) gets a manifest that lies to installers.


### dash-admin-4: In DASH_AUTH_METHOD=local with zero accounts, /setup redirects to /login and every /api/v1/setup/* route 401s, so the first-admin bootstrap that setup_api exposes is unreachable from a browser

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/setup_routes.py:161`

first_run_open calls setup_engine.probe_admin_status, which returns None unless a `ccsync_dashboard.identity` module exists (setup_engine.py:329) - it does not in this tree - and None is read as CLOSED. ui.page_setup (ui.py:1782) therefore 303s an anonymous visitor to /login, and setup.js's first calls (GET /api/v1/setup/eula, GET /api/v1/setup/tasks) 401. Meanwhile setup_api.setup_status already answers `users_exist` from local_users.any_users_exist and POST /api/v1/setup/admin is open and functional. The docstrings acknowledge 'in THIS build', and the code deliberately avoids teaching the probe about local_users 'so the window does not swing open' - but in local mode with no rows, nobody can log in at all (verify_credentials needs a password hash; DASH_ADMIN_USERS alone cannot authenticate), so the window being shut is a dead end rather than a safety. APPLIANCE_INSTALL.md lists 'no admin account' among things NOT YET TRUE, so this is partly known, but the contradiction between setup_api (open, works) and the page that calls it (closed) is not recorded anywhere.

**Failure scenario.** Fresh appliance, DASH_AUTH_METHOD=local, no users row. Operator browses to https://<node>/ -> /login; any credentials fail. Browses to /setup -> 303 /login?next=/setup. The only way to create the first admin is `curl -X POST /api/v1/setup/admin` by hand; the wizard UI (and the EULA step it gates) cannot be reached.

**Suggested fix.** Make first_run_open honour the same answer setup_api.setup_status gives: when auth_method == 'local' and local_users.any_users_exist(conn) is False, the window is open (keep it shut for smb/oidc). The risk the comment worries about does not apply - under local mode with no account there is no session to protect - and the window closes the instant the first account exists (already enforced by setup_admin's BEGIN IMMEDIATE check). Add a test: local mode, empty users table, GET /setup returns 200 and GET /api/v1/setup/tasks returns 200; after POST /api/v1/setup/admin, an anonymous GET /api/v1/setup/tasks returns 401.

**Verifier.** Traced. No identity module exists in dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard (ls confirms), so probe_admin_status returns None (setup_engine.py:329-331) and first_run_open returns False (setup_routes.py:57-58); ui.page_setup 303s to /login (ui.py:1782-1783) and require_setup_access 401s. Meanwhile setup_api.setup_status/setup_admin use local_users.any_users_exist directly and auth.check_local_settings (auth.py:659-663) explicitly says setup_admin is how the first account gets in. With no row, local login cannot succeed (verify_password needs a hash). Partially acknowledged in APPLIANCE_INSTALL.md ('no admin account' NOT YET TRUE) and the setup_routes docstring, and nothing in production runs DASH_AUTH_METHOD=local yet, so medium: a real dead end in an unshipped mode, recoverable by a curl.


### dash-admin-5: Local mode: an admin can disable themselves / the last enabled admin with no guard, locking the appliance out of admin entirely

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:2374`

api_admin_delete_user goes through local_users.delete_user, which refuses deleting yourself and deleting the last enabled admin (local_users.py:289-299, with a docstring explaining that DASH_ADMIN_USERS needs a redeploy on the appliance). api_admin_disable_user (api.py:2374) calls local_users.disable_user (local_users.py:241), which has neither refusal. auth.is_admin (auth.py:733-743) consults local_users.is_local_admin on every request in local mode, and is_local_admin returns False for a disabled row, so the effect is immediate and the session that did it cannot undo it.

**Failure scenario.** Single-admin appliance (the normal post-wizard state). Admin opens the Users page and clicks disable on their own row by mistake (or on the only other admin while their own row is already disabled). The POST succeeds; the next request from the same browser is a non-admin: /admin/* 403s, /api/v1/admin/users/<self>/disable with disabled=false 403s. No local account can administer the dashboard; recovery needs a redeploy with DASH_ADMIN_USERS or sqlite surgery on /data/dashboard.db.

**Suggested fix.** Give disable_user the same two guards delete_user has when `disabled=True` (refuse the requesting user's own account; refuse the last enabled admin), surface them as 409 in api_admin_disable_user, and cover both with tests beside the delete ones.

**Verifier.** Traced. local_users.disable_user (local_users.py:241-246) has no self/last-admin guard, unlike delete_user (:289-299). api_admin_disable_user (api.py:2374-2391) passes straight through. auth.is_admin checks DASH_ADMIN_USERS first then is_local_admin (auth.py:731-734; local_users.py:173-178 returns False for a disabled row), and verify_password refuses a disabled account, so the disabling session loses admin on the next request and cannot log back in. Only DASH_ADMIN_USERS (a redeploy on the appliance shape) or DB surgery recovers. Medium: lock-out of an admin surface, but local mode is not in the field yet and no data is lost.


### dash-admin-8: A person-level tick for an editor who owns BOTH a wired (base) machine and a remote one writes a selection row onto the wired machine, reviving the CR-28 permanent [ GETTING READY ] on that machine

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/db.py:1916`. Related: CR-28

CR-28's guard is per PERSON: api_tick (api.py:1573) and the sidebar toggle (ui.py:769) refuse only when `editor in db.base_only_editors(conn)`, and the queue filters (api.py:423, lane-C/lane-A-B pending blocks) skip only those editors. add_selection_for_person targets `machines_of(conn, editor)` without consulting machine_modes, so for an editor whose machines are {desktop: base, laptop: editor} (the exact shape commit f27c181 'wired machines: a site can have more than one' introduces) the tick lands on the base-mode desktop too, and `?machine=<desktop>` is accepted outright. docs/MULTI_BASE_RIG_PLAN.md item 7 / the 'person-level tick must skip his wired desktop' bullet describe this as a pending work package, so it is planned but not in KNOWN_BUGS and the code today has the defect.

**Failure scenario.** Owner's account has the wired base rig (mode base) and a laptop (mode editor). They tick 2026/FF5/Animals from the sidebar: rows (alex, BASE-RIG, slug) and (alex, laptop, slug) are written; base_only_editors does not contain alex, so build_transfers_view's pending block shows 'alex · BASE-RIG · 2026/FF5/Animals [ GETTING READY ]' and it never clears, because the base rig syncs nothing and never gets a completion row - the CR-28 symptom, one machine over.

**Suggested fix.** Make the base-rig predicate per machine: a `db.base_machines(conn) -> set[(editor, machine)]` used by add_selection_for_person (skip base machines), by api_tick/ui toggle when `?machine=` names one (409), by fetch_machine_selections' bucket fan-out, and by the three queue sources in build_transfers_view; keep base_only_editors as the rollup for accounts with no non-base machine. This is what MULTI_BASE_RIG_PLAN.md already proposes; until it lands, at least skip base-mode machines in add_selection_for_person.

**Verifier.** Traced. add_selection_for_person fans out to machines_of(conn, editor) (db.py:1935) with no machine_modes filter; api_tick (api.py:1573) and the sidebar toggle (ui.py:769) refuse only when editor in base_only_editors, which requires ALL of the person's machines to be base (db.py:1184-1186), and ?machine=<base machine> is accepted. build_transfers_view's pending block filters on the same per-person set (api.py:442, :518), so a (alex, BASE-RIG, slug) row renders [ GETTING READY ] forever once alex also owns an editor-mode laptop. KNOWN_BUGS CR-28 is marked fixed for the single-base-rig shape; MULTI_BASE_RIG_PLAN.md §5 ('the person-level tick must skip his wired desktop') lists this as unbuilt work, so it is planned but not in the ledger and present in code after f27c181. Medium: only hits an account with mixed wired/remote machines, which no one has onboarded yet, and the symptom is a stuck chip, not lost data.
