### dash-admin-6: Enforce cycle adds a registry device id that Syncthing has not approved yet, so every cycle re-PUTs the folder and logs a '+device' that Syncthing silently discards

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/collector.py:879`

machine_devices is built from machines.syncthing_device_id, which the companion reports (api._register_machine -> db.upsert_machine) as soon as it has a local Syncthing, typically before the admin approves that device on the NAS. _run_enforce adds `machine_devices[(editor, machine)]` to `desired` whenever the machine has a tick, without checking that the id is in the config's device list (`id_to_editor`). Syncthing's config validation drops folder device entries that name an unknown device (ensureExistingDevices), so the PUT is accepted but has no effect, `actual` never gains the id, and the plan is recomputed and re-PUT on every 60 s cycle.

**Failure scenario.** New laptop: companion reports machine + device id; admin ticks a project for that machine on /admin/assignments before approving the pending device. Every enforce cycle: get_folder + put_folder on each ticked folder, log 'enforced shares on <slug>: +[<id>] -[]', no share actually made; the admin reading the log believes the share exists while the editor's tray shows the folder never arriving. Continues until the device is approved.

**Suggested fix.** In the per-machine branch only add `device_id` when `device_id in id_to_editor` (i.e. it is a configured, non-server device); otherwise log once per device that the machine's Syncthing device is not approved yet (the pending-devices list is already surfaced on the admin page).

**Verifier.** Traced. machine_devices is filled from machines.syncthing_device_id for every registered machine (collector.py:854-858) and the per-machine branch adds it to desired without checking membership in id_to_editor, the config's device list (:879-882). Syncthing's config preparation drops folder device entries naming a device that is not in the top-level devices list, so the PUT is a no-op, actual never gains the id, and the plan is recomputed every cycle with a misleading '+[id]' log. Low is right: no unshare, no data risk, resolves itself once the device is approved; cost is log noise and an admin misreading the log.


### dash-admin-7: nas_kind is an editable, unvalidated free-text field on Settings and in site.toml import, is published to installers, and is never what nas.factory actually uses

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/site_store.py:78`

KEYS maps 'nas_kind' to the generic 'str' validator, so PUT /api/v1/admin/site and POST /api/v1/admin/site/import accept any string ('Synology', 'qnap', 'truenas ' with a typo). admin_settings.html renders it as a normal text input that site_settings.js re-submits on every save. resolved_manifest publishes the DB value in GET /api/v1/site and the export, but nas.factory.make_nas_client (factory.py:291) and setup_engine._nas_kind read settings.nas_kind (the DASH_NAS_KIND env var) only, and the companion's site.py normalise keeps nas_kind as a plain string.

**Failure scenario.** Admin on a Synology site whose container has DASH_NAS_KIND=synology edits NAS KIND to 'TrueNAS' (or it arrives capitalised via an imported site.toml) and saves: the manifest now tells every installer/wizard `nas_kind: TrueNAS` (which is not equal to 'truenas' for any client comparing exactly), while the dashboard's own NAS client and the setup checklist keep using synology. Nothing warns; the two halves of the product disagree about which NAS this is.

**Suggested fix.** Validate nas_kind in site_store.validate against nas.factory.NAS_KINDS (lower-cased), the way canonical_prefix and indexer_model_tier get special cases; and either make the field read-only on Settings when DASH_NAS_KIND is set (it is deploy-time data like the auto-derived keys) or have factory.make_nas_client read the resolved manifest so the DB value is actually authoritative.

**Verifier.** Traced. site_store.KEYS maps nas_kind to the generic 'str' validator (:78) and validate() special-cases only canonical_prefix and indexer_model_tier; admin_settings.html:42 renders it as a plain text input; resolved_manifest publishes pick('nas_kind') or 'truenas' (:371). nas/factory.py:36,51 and setup_engine._nas_kind (:803-804) read settings.nas_kind (env) only, lower-cased. So an edited/imported value reaches installers but never the dashboard's own NAS client. Low: cosmetic/consistency today since the companion and installers only echo nas_kind and nothing branches on it at fleet scale.


## Dashboard: release channel, AI providers, mounts (2 high / 1 medium)

> **Coverage.** Read end to end: release_trust.py, package_store.py, release_feed.py, dashboard_update.py, ai_providers.py, cli_tools.py, ytdl.py, broll.py, music.py (mount half), crash_report.py, the publish/download/offer routes in api.py (lines ~2900-3330), api_site + site_store.resolved_manifest, settings.from_env's DASH_RELEASE_PUBKEYS / feed / feature parsing, app.py create_app + lifespan (mount order, poller, watchdog, finish_restart), the companion's upgrade.py floor/offer acceptance (_accept_offer, note_floor, below_floor, parse_version), tools/sign_release.py's min_version handling, tools/publish_feed.py's record ordering, ytdlweb/ai_backend._cli_env, deploy/select_code_root.py's state-file surface. Ran read-only: dashboard tests test_packages, test_release_feed, test_dashboard_update, test_ai_providers, test_cli_tools, test_crash_report, test_broll_mount, test_music_mount, test_ytdl_mount, test_select_code_root: 457 passed. Verified as sound and NOT reported: signature verification over the server-assembled record on both publish doors (PUT and feed) with the same package_store writer; DASH_RELEASE_PUBKEYS placeholder filtering (503, not "rejected"); the redirect follow (https-only per hop, 5-hop cap, no credential header, body cap applied to the final hop, sha256/signature after the fact; cli_tools reuses the same opener); tar extraction refusals in both dashboard_update and cli_tools; stage-verify in a subprocess on DB copies; the pty sign-in's redaction of tokens/codes in status/tail/logs and the token file under secrets/ai 0600; $HOME overlay consistency between probe, Test and the ytdl worker (ai_backend strips the same API-key vars and applies the same overlay); /api/v1/site serves only site_store keys (AI settings rows are outside KEYS); all three mounts tri-state and wrapped in except so boot cannot be blocked; BrollGate/YtdlGate/MusicGate strip-then-append identity headers. Not covered: the pty flow could only be read, not exercised (Windows host, no pty); the live NAS/feed was not touched; the companion test suite was not run.


### dash-release-ai-1: The Settings page's 'YouTube downloader' tick publishes the feature to the fleet but never mounts /ytdl (mount reads env only)

**HIGH, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/ytdl.py:257`. Related: CR-2 (the flag was introduced there as env + site.toml; the Settings-page DB row came later in WP D and the mount was not updated)

mount_ytdl() gates on `settings.site_feature_youtube_download`, which Settings.from_env fills ONLY from the container env var DASH_SITE_YOUTUBE_DOWNLOAD (settings.py:587). But the feature flag is also a site_settings DB row: dashboard/templates/admin_settings.html:59 renders a checkbox named `features.youtube_download`, the Settings save writes that row, and `GET /api/v1/site` resolves the flag DB-row-first (site_store.resolved_manifest, line ~373 `as_bool("features.youtube_download")`). So the two consumers disagree: companions read /api/v1/site (site.feature_enabled) and see the feature ON; the dashboard itself decides at create_app from env only, and never re-reads the row, not even on restart. Contrast `ai_providers.cli_enabled()` (line ~384) which correctly reads the DB row first then the env; mount_ytdl does not. The vendor build ships with the env var unset, so this is exactly the path a customer admin will take.

**Failure scenario.** Vendor-build dashboard (no DASH_SITE_YOUTUBE_DOWNLOAD in compose). Admin opens Settings, ticks 'YouTube downloader', saves. /api/v1/site now returns features.youtube_download=true, every companion unhides its YouTube tray items, installs the ytdl tooling and starts calling the dashboard's /ytdl fleet routes (claim/manifest/status). The dashboard answered DISABLED at boot and /ytdl is not mounted, so every one of those calls and the /ytdl page 404 ("ytdl UI not mounted: this site has not enabled..." in the log while the site says it did). Restarting the container does not help because the mount still reads only the env. The reverse is also wrong: env=1 and the admin unticks the box -> companions hide the feature while the dashboard keeps /ytdl mounted and serving.

**Suggested fix.** Make the mount decision consult the same source /api/v1/site does: at create_app open a short-lived connection and compute `row_value(conn, 'features.youtube_download')` (DB row wins, env fallback, exactly like ai_providers.cli_enabled), or mount whenever either says on and have YtdlGate refuse per request when the live flag is off (so a tick takes effect without a restart, matching the WP D 'no --recreate' promise). At minimum the Settings page must tell the admin a restart is needed and the restart must honour the row. Add a test in test_ytdl_mount.py that seeds the site_settings row with env unset and asserts MOUNTED.

**Verifier.** Traced end to end. dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/ytdl.py:257 short-circuits on `settings.site_feature_youtube_download`, and that field is populated ONLY from `DASH_SITE_YOUTUBE_DOWNLOAD` in Settings.from_env (settings.py:587); app.py:800 calls mount_ytdl(app, settings) once at create_app with that env-derived object. Meanwhile admin_settings.html:59-60 renders a `features.youtube_download` checkbox, site_store.set_many stores the row, and site_store.resolved_manifest (site_store.py:373) resolves the flag DB-row-first for `GET /api/v1/site` (api.py:~1059), which the companion's `site.feature_enabled("youtube_download")` consumes (ytdl_server.py:267/384, ytdlp_manager.py:263, sidecar_tools). ai_providers.cli_enabled (ai_providers.py:385-395) is the contrasting correct pattern: row first, env fallback. Nothing in ui.py or api.py tells the admin a restart is needed, and a restart would not help because mount still reads env only. seed_from_env_once only copies env -> DB, never DB -> mount. test_ytdl_mount.py only exercises the settings kwarg (line 189), so no test covers the row path. In the vendor build (env unset) the Settings tick turns the feature on for every companion while the dashboard's /ytdl fleet routes stay unmounted and 404; the reverse (env=1, box unticked) leaves the dashboard serving while the companions hide the feature. High: a customer-admin path in the shipped vendor build that breaks the whole feature.


### dash-release-ai-2: A dashboard code update interrupted by a process kill leaves update_state.json in_progress=true forever; every later apply and rollback 409s with no way to clear it

**HIGH, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/dashboard_update.py:453`

start_apply() writes `in_progress: true` to /data/code/update_state.json (line 1033) and apply() keeps it true through download/extract/verify/backup (line 899). The only writers that set it back to false are _fail_state (line 354, reached only when the worker thread catches an exception) and consume_restart_request (line 849, reached only on a graceful shutdown that an update itself requested). Nothing resets it at boot: app.py's lifespan calls start_boot_watchdog and finish_restart but never inspects a stale in-progress flag, and there is no admin route to clear it. preflight (line 453) and rollback (line 986) both refuse on `state.get('in_progress')`. The module docstring's own rule ('every decision lands in a state file') means this latch survives the process, which is correct for the restart flag but wrong for a progress flag whose worker thread is gone.

**Failure scenario.** Admin clicks APPLY for dashboard 0.7.5 on a Synology with a slow uplink; the 256 MiB bundle download (BUNDLE_FETCH_TIMEOUT is 600 s) is mid-flight when the NAS reboots for a DSM update, docker stops the container, or the process is OOM-killed. The worker thread dies without running _fail_state. On the next boot update_state.json still says step=downloading, in_progress=true. Every subsequent POST /api/v1/admin/dashboard-update/apply answers 409 'an update to 0.7.5 is already in progress (step: downloading). Wait for it to finish or fail.' forever, and POST /rollback answers 409 too. On an appliance with no shell the admin cannot edit /data/code/update_state.json, so OTA is dead for that customer until someone with NAS shell access deletes the file. The same stuck state arises if threading.Thread.start() raises inside start_apply after the flag is written (line 1033-1043).

**Suggested fix.** At lifespan start (or in read_state), treat a stale flag as failed: if in_progress is true and restart_requested is false, the worker that owned it cannot exist in a fresh process, so write step='failed', in_progress=false, error='interrupted by a restart at step <step>'. Also guard the flag with a process-local owner (pid + started_at) and let preflight ignore an in_progress whose pid is not os.getpid(). Optionally add a [ CLEAR ] admin action on the panel. Add a test: write in_progress=true, build the app, assert preflight succeeds.

**Verifier.** Traced. start_apply (dashboard_update.py:1022-1050) writes in_progress=True under _APPLY_LOCK and then starts a daemon thread; apply() rewrites in_progress=True at :899. The only transitions back to False are _fail_state (:352, reached only from the worker's except clauses at :1040-1045) and consume_restart_request (:842-851, which returns early unless restart_requested is set, i.e. only after an update reached request_restart/rollback). read_state (:334) only sets defaults; app.py's lifespan (:323, :339) calls start_boot_watchdog and finish_restart and never inspects or resets a stale in_progress. No pid/owner is recorded, no admin route clears it, and status() (:1054-1120) merely echoes the flag. preflight (:453) and rollback (:986) both 409 on it. Tests at test_dashboard_update.py:270 and :514 seed in_progress=True and assert the 409, confirming the latch is honoured across a fresh app build, but no test covers recovery. So a container stop / OOM kill / NAS reboot during the download or extract step (BUNDLE_FETCH_TIMEOUT 600 s window) leaves update_state.json with in_progress=true permanently; every later apply and rollback is refused until someone with shell access edits /data/code/update_state.json. Kept high: on the zero-touch/appliance deployment shape the admin has no shell, so OTA is dead for that customer, and a power loss mid-download is an ordinary event.


### dash-release-ai-3: A signed offer whose min_version exceeds its own version raises every companion's downgrade floor before the offer is checked against it, bricking the upgrade channel fleet-wide until a higher build ships

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `companion/src/ccsync_companion/upgrade.py:651`

Upgrader._accept_offer verifies the signature, then calls note_floor(self._floor_file, info['min_version']) UNCONDITIONALLY (line 651), and only then asks below_floor(info['version'], floor). note_floor is monotonic by design ('the floor only ever goes up'). Nothing on the signing/publishing side checks that min_version <= version: tools/sign_release.py:180 only checks dotted-numeric, api.api_publish_package (line 3100) only calls release_trust.valid_min_version (format only), and release_feed.publish_from_feed passes min_version straight through. So a single typo in --min-version is accepted by every verifier and then, on every companion that merely SEES the offer (it is fed on every heavy report tick, not on a click), permanently raises the floor above the build on offer.

**Failure scenario.** Release rig runs `sign_release --version 0.9.44 --min-version 0.9.54` (finger slip on 0.9.34) and ships. The dashboard accepts the record (signature valid, min_version dotted-numeric). Every companion's next report carries the offer; _accept_offer verifies it, note_floor writes ~/.ccsync/upgrade_floor.json = 0.9.54, below_floor(0.9.44, 0.9.54) is True and the offer is refused with 'below the downgrade floor'. The vendor notices, rolls current back to 0.9.43 or republishes a corrected 0.9.44 with min_version 0.9.34: both are still below 0.9.54 and are refused by every machine that ticked in between, on both platforms. Recovery is shipping a build numbered >= 0.9.54 (burning ten version numbers) or deleting upgrade_floor.json by hand on every editor machine. The auto_update feature makes the exposure window a single report tick.

**Suggested fix.** Three independent guards, any one of which closes it: (1) in the companion, compute below_floor against the floor already on disk BEFORE note_floor, and only note_floor for an offer that is accepted (an offer refused for any reason must not move the latch); additionally refuse to record a floor higher than the offered version. (2) In release_trust.valid_min_version / api_publish_package and in release_feed._valid_records, refuse a record whose parsed min_version > parsed version. (3) In tools/sign_release.py, exit non-zero when min_version > version. Add a companion test: offer {version 0.9.44, min_version 0.9.54} must be refused AND leave the floor file untouched.

**Verifier.** Traced. Upgrader._accept_offer (companion upgrade.py:651) calls note_floor(self._floor_file, info.get('min_version')) unconditionally after signature verification and before below_floor; note_floor (:191-226) is monotonic and persists immediately. note_report_response feeds every heavy report tick through _accept_offer (the docstring says so explicitly), so merely seeing the offer is enough. No layer checks min_version <= version: tools/sign_release.py:180 is dotted-numeric only; api.api_publish_package (api.py:3100) only calls release_trust.valid_min_version, which is a character/format check (release_trust.py:130-141); release_feed passes min_version through at :561 and publish_latest.py:182 / publish_feed.py:157 forward the operator's argument verbatim. The companion test suite (test_upgrade.py:1175-1230) covers monotonicity and the floor refusing older builds but never an offer whose min_version exceeds its own version. One typo in --min-version therefore raises every reporting companion's floor above the offered build, and a corrected republish or rollback to the previous current is then refused on every machine that ticked; recovery is a build numbered >= the typo or deleting ~/.ccsync/upgrade_floor.json by hand per machine. Medium is right: operator error is required (the signing key is offline and in the vendor's hands), but the blast radius is fleet-wide and a careful release rig is the only guard today.


## Server scripts (1 high / 5 medium / 1 low)

> **Coverage.** Read end to end: server/common.py (site loader, host-key pinning, run_ssh/SUDO_PW preamble, snapshot_before, backend selection), server/backends/truenas.py (whole file), server/backends/synology.py (root_cmd/PATH_EXPORT, sftp_path, deploy_stack, snapshot/list/schedule), server/install_dashboard_app.py (all 4333 lines: compose_config, site_env, swap/prune/staging scripts, ffmpeg/deno provisioning, main() step order), server/publish_db.py, server/setup_snapshots.py, server/create_api_key.py, server/setup_tree.py, server/setup_editor_account.py, server/setup_syncthing_folder.py, server/write_marker.py, server/accept_device.py, server/check_health.py; skimmed secure_syncthing_gui.py and install_syncthing_app.py. Cross-checked docs/SERVER.md, docs/BACKUP_RESTORE.md (sections 1, 3, 4b, 4d), docs/SERVER-SYNOLOGY.md (grep), dashboard nas/truenas.py call list vs create_api_key.ALLOWLIST (they agree), and dashboard collector._ensure_shared_folders for the Assets path. Ran the server suite read-only from Git Bash with the dashboard venv: 528 passed, 2 skipped (yaml missing for one compose test; POSIX-mode test on NTFS). The suite covers the generated scripts under a stub sudo, the swap/rollback scripts, resolve_dataset's df parsing and the DSM fake, but it does NOT exercise newest_prev, the privilege level of the df probe, publish_db's SFTP staging on DSM, or a fresh-tree install where Assets/ does not exist yet. git ls-files --eol shows no CRLF files under server/. Not verified against the live NAS (read-only mandate): the findings that depend on TRUENAS_USER lacking traverse rights on 770 directories rest on the repo's own repeated statements of that fact (common.py:668, setup_tree.py:34-36, install_dashboard_app.py:1842-1851 / OPS-5, check_health.py:389). Checked KNOWN_BUGS.md through CR-45; none of the items below is already listed.


### server-1: publish_db --rollback cannot find the previous index: newest_prev lists a directory TRUENAS_USER cannot read

**HIGH, CONFIRMED.** `server/publish_db.py:355`. Related: OPS-5 / SERVER-9 (same unprivileged-probe class, fixed elsewhere)

Every other filesystem probe in this package runs under `sudo -S` because TRUENAS_USER has no traverse rights on the 770 directories (SERVER-9, OPS-5, check_tree). `newest_prev()` is the exception: it runs `ls -1d '<dir>/<file>.prev-*' 2>/dev/null | sort | tail -n 1` unprivileged through `ida.run_ssh_guarded`. The broll target dir is `<tree>/Assets/B-roll Archive`, which install_dashboard_app.py:3992 makes `3000:3001` mode 2770; the music target dir is `<apps>/music-data`, `3000:3000` mode 770 (the exact directory OPS-5 at install_dashboard_app.py:1845 says TRUENAS_USER cannot traverse). `ls` gets EACCES, its stderr is discarded, the pipeline's rc is tail's 0, stdout is empty, and newest_prev returns "". do_rollback (line 369-374) then prints "FAILED: no broll.db.prev-* found ... nothing to roll back to" and exits 1, even though the publish that just ran printed "previous index kept at <target>.prev-<ts>". docs/BACKUP_RESTORE.md §4d and §6 give `python publish_db.py --which broll --rollback --apply` as the first-line recovery for a bad index publish. The same function also returns "" in dry-run, so `--rollback` without `--apply` (the default mode) always reports the same false "nothing to roll back to".

**Failure scenario.** Operator publishes a broken broll.db with `publish_db.py --which broll --apply` (succeeds, keeps broll.db.prev-20260821T101500 beside it). Search is now wrong, so they run the documented `publish_db.py --which broll --rollback --apply`. newest_prev's unprivileged ls fails on the 2770 archive dir, returns "", and the script exits 1 with "no broll.db.prev-* found in ... there is nothing to roll back to". The operator is told the .prev does not exist while it does; the only way through is to guess the exact timestamped path and pass --from-prev by hand.

**Suggested fix.** Run the listing as root like every sibling probe: `echo "$SUDO_PW" | sudo -S -p "" sh -c 'ls -1d <pattern> 2>/dev/null | sort | tail -n 1'` (or `find <dir> -maxdepth 1 -name '<file>.prev-*'` under sudo), treat a non-zero rc as an explicit "could not list" error distinct from "nothing there", and make the dry-run path say it would list rather than reporting FAILED. Add a test that asserts the generated command carries sudo.

**Verifier.** server/publish_db.py:352-359 runs `ls -1d <dir>/<file>.prev-* 2>/dev/null | sort | tail -n 1` through ida.run_ssh_guarded with no sudo, and `tail` makes the pipeline rc 0 on EACCES, so an unreadable directory is indistinguishable from an empty one. The music target dir is the same `<apps>/music-data` 3000:3000 770 that install_dashboard_app.py:1845 (OPS-5) explicitly says TRUENAS_USER cannot traverse; the broll dir is chowned 3000:3001 2770 at :3992 and setup_tree.py:34 / common.py:668 both state TRUENAS_USER has no traverse on the 770 tree. Independent of permissions, the dry-run branch (:357 `if dry_run ... return ""`, and main sets args.dry_run = not args.apply at :423) means `--rollback` without `--apply` ALWAYS prints the FAILED 'nothing to roll back to' message, contradicting the script's own docstring (line 7) and BACKUP_RESTORE.md. No test covers newest_prev (grep of server/tests finds none). The escape hatch --from-prev exists, so not critical, but the documented first-line recovery is broken.


### server-2: setup_tree's pre-chown snapshot is never taken on TrueNAS: resolve_dataset's `df` runs unprivileged on a path the admin cannot stat

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `server/backends/truenas.py:875`. Related: SERVER-9 (unprivileged probe on the 770 dataset)

snapshot_before("setup_tree", args.projects_root) (setup_tree.py:266) is the floor under the `chown -R` / recursive chmod that follows. TrueNASBackend.resolve_dataset asks `df --output=source <path>` over plain `self.calls.ssh` (no sudo). For the Projects root, `<pool_root>/<tree>/Projects`, stat needs search permission on `<pool_root>` and `<tree>`, both 2770 owner:editors by this package's own design, which TRUENAS_USER lacks (common.py:668, setup_tree.py:34). df exits 1, the code falls back to the naive string `tank/TheCreatorsPool/Creators_Club/Projects` (line 888-890), `zfs snapshot` of that fails with "dataset does not exist", and snapshot_before (common.py:1773) prints a WARNING and continues. The deploy path works only because `/mnt/<pool>/apps` happens to be root:root 755. The test `test_truenas_falls_back_to_the_path_when_df_cannot_answer` pins the fallback but nothing exercises the privilege level of the probe, and CLAUDE.md/BACKUP_RESTORE.md §3 state that setup_tree snapshots the tree dataset before the chown.

**Failure scenario.** Admin runs `python server/setup_tree.py --year 2026 --series "Creator Profiles" --project "Season 1"` on this fleet. The df probe is refused, the naive dataset name does not exist, stderr shows "FAILED to snapshot tank/TheCreatorsPool/Creators_Club/Projects@ccsync-pre-setup_tree-..." followed by "WARNING: no pre-setup_tree snapshot ... Continuing", and the chown -R runs with nothing behind it. With a mistyped --projects-root the recursive chown has no point-in-time to roll back to, which is exactly the case COMMERCIAL_READINESS item 8 added snapshot_before for. The 2026-08-18 note in this same function shows the WARNING was already missed once for the apps path.

**Suggested fix.** Run the df probe under sudo like every other filesystem probe (`echo "$SUDO_PW" | sudo -S -p "" df --output=source ...`), or have setup_tree pass DEFAULT_DATASET_ROOT (the pool root, which df can read) instead of the Projects root. Consider making a snapshot failure on setup_tree at least as loud as the refusal (or default --require-snapshot when [tree] pool_root is configured), and add a test asserting the probe carries sudo.

**Verifier.** truenas.py:875-878 issues `df --output=source <path>` via plain self.calls.ssh (no sudo); on rc!=0 it returns the naive string (:888-890), and snapshot_now then runs `zfs snapshot tank/TheCreatorsPool/Creators_Club/Projects@...` which cannot exist, so common.snapshot_before only WARNs and setup_tree.py:266 proceeds to the chown -R. statfs needs search permission on every path component, and this repo's own comments (setup_tree.py:34, common.py:668, install_dashboard_app.py:1845) state TRUENAS_USER cannot traverse the 770 tree dataset, while docs/SERVER.md:389 confirms the tree is broll:editors 2770. I could not verify the live group membership of the admin account from the repo, but the docstring's claim that df 'needs no privilege' is wrong for a path under a 770 directory, and the 2026-08-18 note in the same function shows this WARNING was already silently missed once. Medium is right: the safety floor is absent, nothing is destroyed by itself.


### server-3: Fresh TrueNAS install leaves <tree>/Assets root:root 0755, so Syncthing (uid 3000) cannot create the LUT/Stills folders and editors cannot write under Assets

**MEDIUM, PLAUSIBLE.** `server/install_dashboard_app.py:3992`

The b-roll archive block runs `mkdir -p '<tree>/Assets/B-roll Archive' ... && chown 3000:3001 <those leaves> && chmod 2770 <those leaves>` as root; the music block at line 4028 does the same for `<tree>/Assets/Music`. `mkdir -p` creates the intermediate `Assets/` directory too, as root with sudo's default umask, i.e. root:root 0755, and nothing in this repo ever chowns it: setup_tree.py only touches Projects/, server/README.md, docs/INSTALL.md and docs/SERVER.md never mention creating Assets/, and the dashboard collector (_ensure_shared_folders, collector.py:405-418) creates `Assets/Luts` and `Assets/Stills` by adding Syncthing folders at `<prefix>/Luts`, relying on the Syncthing container (3000:3001) to mkdir the leaf. On a fresh site the archive mkdir is the first thing to create Assets/, so those mkdirs are EACCES. (On a ZFS dataset with aclmode=restricted the inherited ACL may mask this; on a dataset with a plain mode, or any site where the tree is new, it does not.)

**Failure scenario.** A second customer follows INSTALL.md on an empty tree: setup_tree.py creates Projects/..., then `install_dashboard_app.py` creates `<tree>/Assets/B-roll Archive` and `<tree>/Assets/Music` as 3000:3001 2770 but leaves `<tree>/Assets` root:root 0755. The dashboard's first provision cycle adds Syncthing folders assets-luts / assets-stills at /data/Assets/Luts and /data/Assets/Stills; Syncthing (uid 3000) cannot create them under a root-owned 0755 parent, both folders sit in "folder path missing" error on every cycle, the LUT library never reaches any editor, and an editor browsing P:\Assets over SMB cannot create anything beside the two pre-made leaves.

**Suggested fix.** Create and own the Assets parent explicitly in the same root script: `mkdir -p <tree>/Assets && chown 3000:3001 <tree>/Assets && chmod 2770 <tree>/Assets` (using APP_UID/APP_GID, see server-4), and create the shared-asset rels from common.SHARED_ASSET_FOLDERS the same way so the collector never depends on Syncthing's mkdir. Alternatively have setup_tree.py (or a documented install step) own Assets/ with the tree's posture. Add a test that the generated host-dir script chowns the Assets parent, not only the leaves.

**Verifier.** Confirmed in code: install_dashboard_app.py:3992 and :4028 `mkdir -p` the archive and Music leaves and chown/chmod only those leaves; grep finds no other creator of `<tree>/Assets` anywhere in server/*.py, docs/INSTALL.md, docs/SERVER.md or server/README.md (only common.py:321 names the constant); setup_tree's set_tree_acl (truenas.py:797-820) chowns only the project base and container dirs under Projects/. The collector (collector.py:405-418) adds Syncthing folders at <prefix>/Luts and <prefix>/Stills and relies on the Syncthing uid to mkdir them. So on a fresh tree Assets/ is created root:root with root's umask. Downgraded to PLAUSIBLE only because the real outcome depends on the dataset's ACL inheritance / aclmode (a TrueNAS dataset with an inheriting NFSv4 ACL would mask this), and on this fleet Assets pre-existed, so no live reproduction exists. Severity medium stands for a second customer following INSTALL.md.


### server-4: TrueNAS host-dir and tree-dir ownership is hardcoded 3000:3000 / 3000:3001 while the container runs as site.toml [stack] uid/gid

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `server/install_dashboard_app.py:3841`

APP_UID/APP_GID are `site_int("stack", "uid", 3000)` / `site_int("stack", "gid", 3001)` (lines 612-613, "Site-supplied since 2026-08-17") and compose_config puts `user: f"{uid}:{gid}"` on the container (line 1205). But the TrueNAS step-1 chain chowns with literals: `chown -R 3000:3000 <root>/data` and `<root>/venv` (3841-3843), `chown 3000:3000 <root>/music-data` (3875) and `<root>/ytdl-data` (3896), `chown 3000:3001` on the b-roll archive dirs (3992) and the music library (4028). publish_db's music identity is also the literal "3000:3000" (publish_db.py:153) and build_db_swap_script's default owner is "3000:3000". The Synology path does read the live ids (build_synology_host_dirs takes `uid`), so only TrueNAS is inconsistent. docs/SERVER.md shows [stack] uid/gid as site data.

**Failure scenario.** A second TrueNAS site whose service account was created as uid 4000 / editors gid 4001 sets `[stack] uid = "4000"`, `gid = "4001"` as SERVER.md invites. The container starts as 4000:4001; /data, /venv, /music-data, /ytdl-data, the b-roll archive and the music library are owned 3000:3000 / 3000:3001 mode 770/2770. run.sh cannot create the venv, the dashboard cannot open /data/dashboard.db, /broll answers "unable to open database file" -- the container crash-loops behind a green deploy that printed "host dirs ready".

**Suggested fix.** Render every chown from APP_UID/APP_GID (`f"{uid}:{uid}"` for the private dirs, `f"{uid}:{gid}"` for the tree-side dirs) and pass the same values into install_music_db/build_db_swap_script and publish_db.install_identity (which could use [stack] owner/group names it already reads for broll). Update the tests that pin the literal "chown -R 3000:3000" to pin the rendered value instead.

**Verifier.** APP_UID/APP_GID are site-supplied at install_dashboard_app.py:612-613 and the container runs as `user: f"{uid}:{gid}"` (:1205), yet the TrueNAS step-1 chain hardcodes `chown -R 3000:3000` for data/venv (:3841-3843), music-data (:3875), ytdl-data (:3896), and `chown 3000:3001` for the archive (:3992) and music library (:4028); publish_db.install_identity returns the literal "3000:3000" for music (:153). The Synology branch passes app_uid into build_synology_host_dirs, so only TrueNAS diverges. A TrueNAS site setting [stack] uid/gid to anything but 3000/3001 gets a container that cannot write /data. Medium: no shipped site hits it, but SERVER.md presents the keys as configurable.


### server-5: publish_db on a Synology site stages in /tmp, which its chrooted SFTP channel cannot reach, so every --apply fails at the transfer

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `server/publish_db.py:492`

publish_db calls `ida.make_staging_dir(args.dry_run, f"ccsync-{args.which}db-upload")` with the default `parent="/tmp"`, then `sftp.put(staged_local, backend.sftp_path(staged_remote))` (line 506). SynologyBackend.sftp_path only strips a leading `/volume<N>` (synology.py:197-202) and the module docstring records the measurement: "The SFTP channel is chrooted to the share view -- for the admin too ... /tmp does not exist there at all". install_dashboard_app.py handles this by staging under `<root>/staging` on DSM (code_staging_parent, line 3737) but publish_db never does. The mktemp over SSH succeeds, the SFTP put of `/tmp/ccsync-brolldb-upload.XXXX/broll.db` fails with a no-such-file error, and the script prints "FAILED: the transfer did not finish ... the live index is untouched". The Synology-specific `install_identity` branch (synoacltool -copy) and its test show publishing on DSM is intended to work, and docs/SERVER-SYNOLOGY.md points at publish_db/BACKUP_RESTORE for the index.

**Failure scenario.** On the Synology site (`[nas] kind = "synology"`, apps root /volume1/docker/ccsync), the operator runs `python server/publish_db.py --which broll --apply`. Local snapshot and quick_check pass, `mktemp -d /tmp/ccsync-brolldb-upload.XXXXXX` succeeds over SSH, then `sftp.put` into the chrooted share view fails (IOError: No such file) and the publish aborts every time. Nothing is damaged, but the b-roll index can never be published to a DSM box through the only supported path, and the staging dir is left in /tmp.

**Suggested fix.** Stage under the apps root on DSM exactly as the deploy does: pick `parent = f"{DEFAULT_APPS_ROOT}/{ida.SYNOLOGY_STAGING_DIRNAME}"` when `backend.kind == "synology"` (ensuring it exists/owned by the SSH user, as build_synology_host_dirs does), and keep /tmp for TrueNAS. Add a test that the Synology publish stages inside host_root_re's shape.

**Verifier.** publish_db.py:492 calls ida.make_staging_dir with the default parent="/tmp" (install_dashboard_app.py:2821-2823) and at :506 does `sftp.put(..., backend.sftp_path(staged_remote))`; SynologyBackend.sftp_path (synology.py:197-202) only strips a leading /volumeN, and the module docstring (finding 3) records the measurement that the admin's SFTP channel is chrooted to the share view where /tmp does not exist. The deploy itself special-cases this (code_staging_parent = <root>/staging at :3737) but publish_db never does, despite carrying a Synology-specific synoacltool branch (:154-161) and SERVER-SYNOLOGY.md:423 pointing at it for index publishes. Every --apply on DSM fails at the transfer with the live index untouched, so medium not high.


### server-6: setup_snapshots.py cannot schedule the apps target on this fleet (apps root is a directory in the pool root), yet BACKUP_RESTORE.md says the apps dataset is snapshotted and gives a restore path that does not exist

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `server/backends/truenas.py:972`

ensure_snapshot_schedule resolves the path with resolve_dataset and then refuses any dataset name without a slash (`DATASET_RE`, line 843/972: "that is a pool, not a dataset"). The repo records (truenas.py:880-886, test_a_directory_in_the_pool_root_snapshots_the_pool_root_dataset) that on this fleet `/mnt/tank/apps/ccsync-dashboard` is a plain directory in `tank`, so df answers `tank`. setup_snapshots.py therefore returns ("failed", ...) for the `app` target on every run, prints "Backups are NOT fully configured" and exits 1, and no scheduled snapshot ever covers dashboard.db, music.db or ytdl.db. docs/BACKUP_RESTORE.md §1 nevertheless states "dashboard.db ... Protected by: NAS snapshots of the **apps** dataset" and "Two datasets carry everything that matters ... Both are snapshotted on the same schedule", and §4b's restore recipe copies from `/mnt/tank/apps/.zfs/snapshot/<snap>/ccsync-dashboard/data/dashboard.db`, a `.zfs` directory that only exists if apps is its own dataset (on this box the snapshot of `tank` would be under `/mnt/tank/.zfs/snapshot/<snap>/apps/...`). Neither the script's failure text nor the doc tells the operator that the fix is to make `apps` (or `apps/ccsync-dashboard`) a dataset.

**Failure scenario.** Operator follows BACKUP_RESTORE.md on this fleet: `python server/setup_snapshots.py --apply` creates the two tree tasks and prints "FAILED -- app: refusing to schedule snapshots on 'tank': that is a pool, not a dataset. Name the dataset the tree lives in (site.toml [tree] pool_root)" -- advice about the wrong key -- and exits 1. Believing §1's table, they assume dashboard.db is covered. After a bad `--recreate` or a corrupted dashboard.db they follow §4b, find no `/mnt/tank/apps/.zfs`, and discover there is no hourly/daily snapshot of the fleet's provisioning state at all.

**Suggested fix.** Either let the apps target fall back to the pool-root dataset with a loud note (a recursive task on `tank` is what actually covers a directory in it), or make the refusal name the real remedy ("create a dataset at <apps root> -- `zfs create tank/apps` -- and move the directory into it") and reference the [apps] root key, not [tree] pool_root. Fix BACKUP_RESTORE.md §1/§4b to say the apps root must be its own dataset for the table and the `.zfs/snapshot` recipe to hold, and have check_health or setup_snapshots --list report when it is not.

**Verifier.** truenas.py:972-975 refuses any resolved dataset without a slash, and the code comment at :880-886 plus test_a_directory_in_the_pool_root_snapshots_the_pool_root_dataset record that on this fleet /mnt/tank/apps is a plain directory in `tank`, so df answers `tank`. setup_snapshots.py:104-106 always adds the app target when [apps] root is set, so on this box it returns failed, and the refusal text names `[tree] pool_root` for the app target. BACKUP_RESTORE.md:22-31 says the apps dataset is snapshotted on the same schedule and :209 restores from /mnt/tank/apps/.zfs/snapshot/, a directory that only exists if apps is its own dataset; :89-90 documents that a bare pool is refused but never says the apps root therefore has no scheduled snapshot here. Docs-vs-code confirmed; medium because the consequence is a missing backup floor for dashboard.db, not data loss by itself.


### server-7: The server scripts always SSH to port 22: [net] sftp_port reaches the container (DASH_NAS_SSH_PORT) but never common.ssh_client

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `server/common.py:1149`

ssh_client() calls `client.connect(host, username=user, password=pw, ...)` with paramiko's default port and there is no site key, env var or flag for an SSH port anywhere in server/ (grep: no `port=` in common.py or backends/synology.py). install_dashboard_app.py:4262 and compose_variables do pass `site_value("net", "sftp_port")` into the container as DASH_NAS_SSH_PORT, and setup_editor_account._rclone_stanza's comment (line 582) says "DSM often moves sshd off 22". The host-key record in ~/.ccsync/known_hosts is also written as bare `host`, which paramiko would not match for a non-22 port ([host]:port).

**Failure scenario.** A Synology site runs sshd on 2222 and sets `[net] sftp_port = "2222"` (so editors' rclone and the dashboard's authorized_keys writer work). Every server script -- install_dashboard_app.py, setup_tree.py, publish_db.py, check_health.py -- connects to port 22, gets connection refused, and fails with a paramiko error that names no port; there is no way to point them at 2222 short of editing common.py.

**Suggested fix.** Thread a port through: `[nas] ssh_port` (defaulting to [net] sftp_port, then 22) read in ssh_client/run_ssh/sftp_put_text, record and look up known_hosts entries with paramiko's `[host]:port` form when the port is not 22, and print the port in the refusal/connection-failure messages.

**Verifier.** common.ssh_client (:1099-1171) calls client.connect(host, username, password, ...) with no port, and grep finds no ssh_port/sftp_port read anywhere in server/common.py or server/backends/; [net] sftp_port is only threaded into the container (install_dashboard_app.py:1099, :1421, :1629, :4262) and into rclone stanzas (setup_editor_account.py:590). The known_hosts record is keyed on bare host, which paramiko would not match for a non-22 port. The scenario needs a site that moves sshd off 22, which DSM's default does not, so low is correct.


## Installer, onboarding, release tooling (3 medium / 3 low)

> **Coverage.** Read end to end (comments stripped): tools/ship.cmd + ship.ps1, tools/release.ps1, installer/build_editor_package.ps1, installer/windows_bootstrap.ps1, installer/windows_upgrade.ps1, installer/windows_uninstall.ps1, installer/macos_bootstrap.sh (shell half; the embedded Resolve mapping helper is covered by companion tests and was skimmed), installer/macos_uninstall.sh, onboarding/onboard.py, onboarding/steps.py, onboarding/build_onboard.spec + build_onboard_macos.spec, tools/release_key.py, sign_release.py, publish_package.py, publish_latest.py, check_deploy_drift.ps1, load_secrets.ps1, run_all_tests.ps1, check_licenses.py (first half), all five workflows; tools/release_macos.sh and build_onboard_macos.sh were grepped for version/sign/publish gates only. Cross-checked the site manifest consumers (companion config.py/site.py/canon.py, dashboard app.py _companion_token_ok and api.py package download route), KNOWN_BUGS.md headings through CR-45, docs/RELEASE.md code-signing section, and the onboarding test suite (test_release_gates, test_eula_step, test_site_manifest, test_cleanup_steps) to avoid re-reporting. Ran read-only: onboarding (295 passed), tools (117 passed), installer/tests/Test-DriveMapParser.ps1, Test-LicenceGate.ps1, test_macos_site_values.sh (all pass). Not covered: gen_notices.py internals, publish_feed.py, build_dashboard_bundle.py, make_product_repo.ps1, the GitHub-hosted runs themselves (reasoned from the YAML), and anything requiring a Mac or the NAS. Version-comparison paths in this area (windows_upgrade Test-VersionAtLeast, steps.eula_version_tuple) handle two-digit minors correctly; legacy com.creatorsclub cleanup is correct in both macOS installer/uninstaller and the wizard; the EULA flow (wizard write, companion read, upgrade-script re-ask) is consistent.


### installer-onboard-tools-1: onboard.exe is never Authenticode-signed, so the fresh-install SmartScreen case the signing gate exists for is never covered

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `installer/build_editor_package.ps1:732`. Related: COMMERCIAL_READINESS item 4 (code signing), SHIP-5

tools/release.ps1 is the only script that ever invokes signtool (lines 693-697), and it signs companion/dist/ccsync-companion.exe only. onboard.exe is built afterwards by build_editor_package.ps1 -RebuildOnboard (and by release-windows.yml's separate PyInstaller step) and is published as kind=onboard with `--signed-binary` decided purely by `Get-AuthenticodeSignature` on that file (line 732), which will always be NotSigned because nothing signs it. tools/ship.ps1's Authenticode gate (line 478 onwards) inspects only the companion exe, yet its own justification is 'every fresh install' and 'Windows protected your PC on first contact' - the binary a fresh install actually double-clicks is onboard.exe, served by the dashboard's [ INSTALLER ] link. docs/RELEASE.md 'Code signing' describes the Windows side as 'wired and tested' once a certificate is configured, which is not true for the installer artefact.

**Failure scenario.** Operator buys the OV/EV certificate, sets CCSYNC_SIGN_THUMBPRINT, runs tools\ship.cmd without -AllowUnsignedBinary. Step 2a signs ccsync-companion.exe; ship's gate passes; step 2b builds onboard.exe (unsigned, bundling the signed companion), publishes it as kind=onboard with signed_binary=0 and makes it current. Every new editor downloading the installer from the dashboard still gets 'Windows protected your PC', the exact outcome the certificate was bought to remove, and no script, gate or drift line says so (check_deploy_drift only reports the companion row's signed_binary).

**Suggested fix.** Factor release.ps1's Find-SignTool/signtool block into a shared step and run it on onboarding/dist/onboard.exe in build_editor_package.ps1 immediately after -RebuildOnboard (and in release-windows.yml after its onboard build). Make ship.ps1's Authenticode gate check both artefacts, and have check_deploy_drift.ps1 report the current kind=onboard row's signed_binary as well.

**Verifier.** Traced: the only signtool invocation in the repo is tools/release.ps1:660-699 and it signs companion/dist/ccsync-companion.exe only; build_editor_package.ps1 has no signing step, it merely reads Get-AuthenticodeSignature on onboard.exe (line 732) to decide --signed-binary, and release-windows.yml's onboard step (lines 162-178) only hashes the exe. ship.ps1's gate (line 478-492) inspects companion/dist/ccsync-companion.exe only. So once a certificate exists, onboard.exe (the artefact a fresh install double-clicks) still ships unsigned and nothing refuses or reports it. Severity stays medium: no certificate exists today, the manifest honestly records signed_binary=0, and the record is still offline-key signed; it is a gap in an unfinished feature rather than a live breakage.


### installer-onboard-tools-2: release-windows.yml can never materialise the PFX: a step-level env is not visible in that step's own `if`

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `.github/workflows/release-windows.yml:133`

The 'materialise the signing certificate' step defines `PFX_B64` in its own step-level `env:` and guards itself with `if: ${{ env.PFX_B64 != '' }}`. In GitHub Actions the `env` context inside a step's `if` contains only workflow- and job-level env; a step's own env block is applied after the condition is evaluated, so `env.PFX_B64` is always empty there and the step is always skipped. CCSYNC_SIGN_PFX therefore never reaches GITHUB_ENV and the build step only ever signs via CCSYNC_SIGN_THUMBPRINT, which cannot work on a hosted runner (no certificate store, no token). The secret name CCSYNC_SIGN_PFX_BASE64 is the documented CI signing route and it is dead on arrival.

**Failure scenario.** Operator adds CCSYNC_SIGN_PFX_BASE64 and CCSYNC_SIGN_PFX_PASSWORD as repository secrets and dispatches release-windows. The materialise step shows as skipped, release.ps1 prints 'UNSIGNED BUILD (no CCSYNC_SIGN_THUMBPRINT and no CCSYNC_SIGN_PFX in the environment)', the manifest records signed_binary=false, and tools/publish_latest.py happily publishes that build to the vendor feed with signed_binary=0 - the operator believes CI is signing and it silently is not.

**Suggested fix.** Move the secret into a job-level `env:` (e.g. `env: PFX_B64: ${{ secrets.CCSYNC_SIGN_PFX_BASE64 }}` under `jobs.build`) so the step `if` can see it, or drop the `if` and test `[string]::IsNullOrEmpty($env:PFX_B64)` inside the pwsh body. Add a summary line stating whether signing was attempted so a skipped step is visible in the run summary.

**Verifier.** release-windows.yml:130-140 defines PFX_B64 in the step's own env and tests `if: ${{ env.PFX_B64 != '' }}`. GitHub Actions evaluates a step's `if` with only workflow/job-level env in the env context; a step's own env block is not visible to its own condition (community discussion #26866 and others confirm). The step is therefore always skipped, CCSYNC_SIGN_PFX never reaches GITHUB_ENV, and the build step only has the thumbprint route, which is useless on a hosted runner with no cert store. The workflow's own comment shows the author believed this worked. Medium: dead CI signing path, discovered only once someone buys a cert; the resulting build is at least honestly labelled unsigned.


### installer-onboard-tools-3: The onboarding wizard still hardcodes the P: drive while both bootstraps and uninstallers are manifest-driven

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `onboarding/steps.py:1298`. Related: COMMERCIAL_READINESS item 11

CLAUDE.md (2026-08-17, item 11) says the drive letter comes from the site manifest's canonical_prefix and that 'both installers, both uninstallers and the companion read the same two keys'. windows_bootstrap.ps1 and windows_uninstall.ps1 do (CCSync-Subst<letter>, CCSync_<letter>, DriveRoot), and Test-DriveMapParser.ps1 even asserts no 'CCSyncSubstP' literal remains in the bootstrap. The wizard was missed: steps.py keeps `DEFAULT_BASE_LOCAL_ROOT = "P:\\"` (line 246), `SUBST_TASK_NAME = "CCSync-SubstP"` / `ALL_RUN_VALUES` with `CCSyncSubstP` (280/285), `OUR_LOOPBACK_SHARE = \\localhost\CCSync_P` (1452), `validate_local_root` refusing only drive 'P' for editors (1298), `build_cleanup_plan` scanning `Path("P:/")` (1609), `execute_cleanup` unmounting P: (1704 onwards), and every _RESOLVE_MAPPING_MESSAGES string naming P:\. The editor-role P: refusal exists because the bootstrap is about to unmap that letter and share the chosen folder through it; on a site whose canonical_prefix is another letter the refusal guards the wrong drive and the right one is unguarded.

**Failure scenario.** A second customer's site.toml sets canonical_prefix = "Q:\\". An editor re-runs onboard.exe on a machine where a previous install already mapped Q: -> \\localhost\CCSync_Q, and types Q:\CCSync as the local sync folder (it passes validate_local_root because Q: exists and is not 'P'). Clean-slate leaves Q: alone (it only knows P:), then windows_bootstrap.ps1 does Ensure-Dir Q:\CCSync (creating a folder inside the old loopback share), unmaps Q:, and tries New-SmbShare -Path Q:\CCSync on a path that no longer exists - the share fails, the subst fallback fails, and the machine ends with no tree drive. Separately, the base-role default root P:\ and the Finish-page 'P:\ Mapped Mount' wording are wrong on that site, and the old CCSync-SubstQ task / CCSyncSubstQ run entry survive a 'removes every trace' clean slate.

**Suggested fix.** Derive the letter in steps.py from the site manifest the same way the bootstrap does (site.get('canonical_prefix'), default P) and thread it through validate_local_root, build_cleanup_plan/execute_cleanup (task name, run value, loopback share, drive to unmount), default_base_local_root and the mapping messages; extend Test-DriveMapParser's 'no CCSyncSubstP literal' scan to onboarding/steps.py.

**Verifier.** Verified the literals: steps.py DEFAULT_BASE_LOCAL_ROOT="P:\\" (246), SUBST_TASK_NAME="CCSync-SubstP" (280), CCSyncSubstP in ALL_RUN_VALUES (285), OUR_LOOPBACK_SHARE \\localhost\CCSync_P (1452), validate_local_root refusing only drive P for non-base roles (1298), build_cleanup_plan scanning Path("P:/") (1609), execute_cleanup unmounting P: (1704-1867). Meanwhile the wizard itself forces canonical_prefix from the site manifest into config (2206/2213) and windows_bootstrap.ps1 derives DriveLetter/TaskName/ShareName from it (777-788, 1200-1213), and Test-DriveMapParser's literal scan (line 131) covers only the bootstrap. On a non-P site the wizard guards the wrong letter and cleans up the wrong task/share. Medium is right: no site in the field uses a non-P prefix today, so this is a second-customer edge rather than a live breakage.


### installer-onboard-tools-4: Wizard's default local sync folder is computed before the site manifest is fetched, so a first run ignores the site's tree_name

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `onboarding/onboard.py:131`

`self.local_root_var = tk.StringVar(value=steps.default_local_root())` runs in __init__, when site_mod.cached_site() is empty on a fresh machine, so the prefill is C:\CCSync. The manifest is only fetched in _on_verify (fetch_site caches it), and _on_role_changed re-derives the default only when the current value is already in root_defaults AND the effective role differs from the picked one; on the common path (picked editor, verified editor) nothing re-reads the now-cached tree_name, so show_install presents C:\CCSync rather than C:\<tree_name>. steps.default_local_root's docstring says the point is that 'a wizard install and a hand re-run of the bootstrap agree'.

**Failure scenario.** Site manifest says tree_name = "Creators_Club". A brand-new editor runs onboard.exe for the first time: the install page offers C:\CCSync and, left as is, the tree lands there. A colleague who runs windows_bootstrap.ps1 by hand on another fresh machine gets C:\Creators_Club. Second wizard runs (cache present) use the site name, so the fleet ends up with two default folder names for the same tree.

**Suggested fix.** After fetch_site succeeds in _on_verify, recompute the default via steps.default_local_root(site=self.site) and apply it when local_root_var still holds any value in root_defaults (include the neutral C:\CCSync / ~/CCSync in that set).

**Verifier.** onboard.py:131 seeds local_root_var from steps.default_local_root() at __init__ time; site_tree_name (steps.py:164-176) falls back to site_mod.cached_site(), which is empty on a first run, giving C:\CCSync. fetch_site in _on_verify (onboard.py:584) does save_site, but _on_role_changed (which would re-read the cache via default_local_root()) is only invoked in the `effective != picked` branch (line 604-608). On the ordinary editor-picks-editor path the prefill stays C:\CCSync while a hand-run windows_bootstrap.ps1 would use C:\<tree_name>. Low: editable prefill, second runs self-correct.


### installer-onboard-tools-5: build_editor_package.ps1's default package destination is a hardcoded P:\ path

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `installer/build_editor_package.ps1:102`

`[string]$Destination = "P:\Assets\Software\CC_Sync"` bakes the P: letter and the Assets\Software layout into the one script tools\ship.cmd calls in step 2b with no -Destination. Every other consumer of the tree root reads canonical_prefix from the site manifest or config.toml. On a release machine whose tree is mapped elsewhere (or with no P: at all, e.g. a second customer's operator rig) the package assembly creates or fails on a literal P: path.

**Failure scenario.** Operator of a site with canonical_prefix Q:\ runs tools\ship.cmd. Step 2b reaches the copy loop: New-Item on P:\Assets\Software\CC_Sync throws under $ErrorActionPreference = Stop (no P: drive), build_editor_package exits non-zero after the dashboard has already been deployed, and the companion is never published - or, if some unrelated P: exists, the package is written onto the wrong drive.

**Suggested fix.** Read canonical_prefix from ~/.ccsync/config.toml (or accept -Destination from ship.ps1, which already knows the dashboard URL and could read /api/v1/site) and build the default as <prefix>Assets\Software\CC_Sync; fail with a clear message when the drive is absent.

**Verifier.** build_editor_package.ps1:102 defaults $Destination to P:\Assets\Software\CC_Sync, $ErrorActionPreference='Stop' at line 116, and New-Item on it runs at line 388-390 before any copy/publish; ship.ps1:497 invokes the script without -Destination, after the step-1 dashboard deploy. With no P: drive New-Item throws and the companion is not published. Low: every operator rig today has P:, and the failure is loud and recoverable by re-running with -Destination.


### installer-onboard-tools-6: ship.ps1's 'already published' probe for the companion downloads the whole exe with no timeout

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `tools/ship.ps1:228`

The companion probe (lines 224-229) runs curl with `-o NUL -w %{http_code}` but, unlike the installer probe a few lines below and publish_package.py's preflight, without `-r 0-0` or `--max-time`. When the version is already published this pulls the full ccsync-companion.exe (tens of MB) over the tailnet just to learn '200', and a stalled dashboard blocks step 0 indefinitely because curl -s has no deadline.

**Failure scenario.** Dashboard container is mid-restart or the tailnet route is flapping when ship.cmd starts: the first probe hangs with no output for as long as the TCP connection lingers, before any gate has reported, and the operator cannot tell whether the secrets gate, the dirty-tree gate or the network is the problem.

**Suggested fix.** Add `"-r", "0-0", "--max-time", "20"` to the companion probe's ExtraArgs and treat 206 like 200, matching the installer probe directly beneath it.

**Verifier.** ship.ps1:224-228 calls Invoke-CurlWithToken (line 146-154, which adds only -s -K -) with -o NUL -w %{http_code} and no -r 0-0 / --max-time, while the installer probe directly below (line 257-260) has both and the comment there even explains why. A published version therefore downloads the entire companion exe and a stalled dashboard blocks step 0 indefinitely. Low: robustness/latency only, no wrong publish results.


## B-roll platform (1 high / 1 medium / 3 low)

> **Coverage.** Read end to end: broll/web/app/{routes_share,client_folders,routes_client_folders,main,media,routes_media,config,db,schemas,routes_api,search,routes_ingest,routes_fleet,routes_batches,ingest_batches,fleet_auth,identity,archive_names}.py, the share viewer (share.html, share.js, share_gone.html, clientfolders.js link builder), tests/test_client_folders.py + test_mounted_prefix.py, dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/broll.py (BrollGate/mount) and app.py login_gate carve-outs, server/publish_db.py, broll/indexer/broll_index/{claude_client,config(excerpt),pipeline(error path),storage/http_backend,storage/sqlite_backend}.py, docs/CLIENT_FOLDERS.md + BACKUP_RESTORE.md references, KNOWN_BUGS.md client-share/CR-4x entries. Ran broll/web suite read-only with the broll-platform venv: 493 passed, 0 skipped (node present so the JS parse test ran). Did NOT run the indexer suite (system python, not needed to substantiate findings) and did not read semantic.py/fuzzy.py beyond their outlines, build_archive.py beyond its DB writes, or the indexer's cli/pipeline stages other than the error-classification block. Path-traversal probes on /share (dot segments, %2F, /share/assets/..) were reasoned against Starlette routing and found non-exploitable; login_gate's /broll/share/ carve-out and the relative 303 redirect are consistent with the mount.


### broll-2: `--model fable` with the default `thinking: disabled` sends `{"type": "disabled"}`, which claude-fable-5 rejects with 400 -- and that 400 is classified per-video, so the run marks every clip 'error'

**HIGH, CONFIRMED.** `broll/indexer/broll_index/claude_client.py:528`

`MODEL_MAP` offers `fable -> claude-fable-5` (config.example.yaml:60, docs/indexing-api.md:57 advertise it). `_thinking_param` always emits an explicit `thinking` object: `{"type": "disabled"}` for the default `AnthropicConfig.thinking = 'disabled'` (config.py:209; test_anthropic_client.py:198 pins that it is sent). On Claude Fable 5 thinking is always on and an explicit `{"type": "disabled"}` is rejected with 400 invalid_request_error (the parameter must be omitted or be `adaptive`). `_call_with_retry` treats 400 as non-retryable and raises `ClaudeCallError('API error 400: invalid_request_error: ...')`; that text matches none of `_FATAL_MARKERS`/`_RATE_LIMIT_MARKERS`, so pipeline.py:681-698 takes the `else` branch and calls `storage.set_error(...)` for the clip and moves on -- i.e. exactly the 'march through the archive marking everything error' outcome the FatalRunError machinery exists to prevent, at one billed-nothing 400 per clip. (Opus 5 additionally only accepts `disabled` at effort <= high; the default effort is high so that one happens to work.)

**Failure scenario.** Operator sets `anthropic.model: fable` (or `--model fable`) in config.yaml and leaves `thinking` at its documented default. `broll-index run --stages claude` on a 2,000-clip queue: every call returns 400, every video's status becomes 'error' with `claude failed: API error 400: ...`, the queue is drained of resumable work, and the operator has to reset 2,000 statuses by hand before retrying with thinking: adaptive.

**Suggested fix.** In `_thinking_param`/`invoke_claude`, omit the `thinking` key entirely when mode is 'disabled' and the resolved model is `claude-fable-5` (or, simpler, never send an explicit `disabled` -- omitting it is equivalent on Sonnet 5/Opus 4.x and is the only accepted form on Fable 5). Additionally add `invalid_request_error` for a `thinking`/model mismatch to the fatal classification so a misconfiguration stops the run instead of poisoning the queue. Add a test for model='fable' asserting no `thinking` key (or `adaptive`) in the sent kwargs.

**Verifier.** Traced: MODEL_MAP has `fable -> claude-fable-5` (claude_client.py:54) and it is advertised in config.example.yaml:60 and docs/indexing-api.md:57. `_thinking_param` (513-519) returns `{type: 'disabled'}` for the default `thinking='disabled'` (config.py:209) and invoke_claude (603-605) always puts it in kwargs; test_anthropic_client.py:198 pins that it is sent. No effort/output_config is sent. Per the current API contract, claude-fable-5 rejects an explicit `{type: 'disabled'}` with 400 (thinking is always on; the param must be omitted or adaptive). `_call_with_retry` (480-494) raises ClaudeCallError('API error 400: invalid_request_error: ...') for non-retryable errors; that text matches none of _FATAL_MARKERS (108-115) or _RATE_LIMIT_MARKERS (122-), so pipeline.py's stage=='claude' branch falls to the else and calls storage.set_error per clip. So `anthropic.model: fable` with default config marks every queued clip 'error'. High stands: a documented model option is wholly broken and destroys queue resumability; it is opt-in, so not fleet-wide.


### broll-1: Public share media/detail routes trust a stale item.video_id without the (share, rel_path) identity check resolve_items applies

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `broll/web/app/routes_share.py:99`

`_member_id` returns `video_id` as soon as `cf.member_video_id(conn, folder_id, video_id)` finds a `client_folder_items` row with that integer. `resolve_items` (client_folders.py:490-497) deliberately does NOT trust that: it re-reads the index row and, if `(share, rel_path)` no longer matches the item's stored identity, re-resolves by name. The module docstring and `test_the_ledger_is_a_separate_database_that_survives_replacing_the_index` both say ids are only as stable as the next index build. So after a rebuild that renumbers `videos.id` (publish_db.py swaps the whole file), the page correctly shows clip X under its NEW id, but `/share/<tok>/api/videos/<old id>`, `/media/proxy/<old id>.mp4`, `/media/sprite/...` and `/media/poster/...` still answer 200 for whatever clip now occupies the OLD id -- a clip nobody curated into that folder. The docstring's promise ('a thumbnail the page shows is a thumbnail this will serve') holds, but the converse ('only those') does not. The test suite only covers the case where the old id no longer exists at all (DELETE then insert id=777), which 404s by accident.

**Failure scenario.** Folder item stored as (video_id=12, share='ff3', rel_path='day1/harbor.mov'). The base rig rebuilds broll.db from scratch and publishes it; harbor.mov is now id 4120 and id 12 is `Downloads/politics/protest.mp4`. A licensee holding the link (or anyone who has it) requests GET /broll/share/<tok>/api/videos/12 -> 200 with `name: 'protest'` and its segment descriptions; GET /broll/share/<tok>/media/proxy/12.mp4 streams the 540p preview of a clip that was never shared with them. Ids are small sequential integers, so every id that was ever in any folder is a cheap guess.

**Suggested fix.** In `_member_id`, after the direct membership hit, fetch the index row and require `(video['share'], video['rel_path']) == (item['share'], item['rel_path'])` (one extra indexed SELECT), falling through to `public_video_ids` otherwise; or simply compute membership via `public_video_ids` only. Add a test that re-inserts a DIFFERENT clip under the old id and asserts 404 on all four public routes.

**Verifier.** Traced: routes_share.py:99 `_member_id` returns on `cf.member_video_id` (client_folders.py:456-459, a bare `SELECT 1 ... WHERE folder_id=? AND video_id=?`) with no (share, rel_path) comparison, while `resolve_items` (client_folders.py:490-497) does compare identity and re-resolves. share_video (171), share_proxy (206-208), share_sprite, share_poster all use the returned stale id directly. The only test of the rebuild case (test_client_folders.py:239-253) deletes all videos and inserts id=777, so the old id does not exist and 404s by accident; a rebuild that reuses the old id for a different clip is not covered. The docs (CLIENT_FOLDERS.md:266) and the test explicitly model rebuilds with new ids, so the scenario is the project's own stated reality. Medium is right: information disclosure of one uncurated clip's preview/segments to a token holder after a renumbering rebuild, no write, no credential.


### broll-3: `/share/assets` publishes the ENTIRE editor static tree (app.js, ingest.js, clientfolders.js, index.html) past the tailnet, not just the viewer's assets

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `broll/web/app/main.py:296`

`app.mount('/share/assets', StaticFiles(directory=config.STATIC_DIR))` re-mounts the whole `broll/web/static` directory under the one prefix the operator funnels to the public internet. The viewer needs share.html's five assets (style.css, share.css, sprite.js, share.js, favicon, brand_mark.png) but the mount also serves app.js (the 1,700-line editor SPA, which share.js's own header says is 'a thing to hide, and to keep hidden'), ingest.js, clientfolders.js and index.html. These reveal the editor API surface (`api/client-folders/...`, `api/ingest-batches/...`, the 127.0.0.1:8899 loopback contract) to anyone without a token. No credential leaks and the login gate still protects the API, but CLIENT_FOLDERS.md §7 and the module docstring claim the funnel 'carries the whole experience and nothing else of the dashboard', which is not what the mount does.

**Failure scenario.** With Funnel on :8443 scoped to /broll/share, `curl https://<nas>.ts.net:8443/broll/share/assets/app.js` and `.../assets/index.html` return 200 to an anonymous internet client, handing over the internal SPA source and route map for reconnaissance.

**Suggested fix.** Serve an allow-listed subset: mount a small dedicated directory (e.g. static/share/ containing only the viewer's files plus symlinks/copies of sprite.js, style.css, favicon, brand mark) or wrap StaticFiles with a filename allow-list. Extend `test_the_viewer_and_everything_it_needs_answer_under_the_share_prefix` with an assertion that `/broll/share/assets/app.js` and `/broll/share/assets/index.html` are 404.

**Verifier.** main.py:66 mounts `/share/assets` on the whole `config.STATIC_DIR`, and that directory (ls) contains app.js, ingest.js, clientfolders.js, index.html alongside the six files share.html actually references (share.html:15-17,27,78-79). Under a Funnel scoped to /broll/share, anonymous clients can fetch the editor SPA source. No credential or data leaks and the API stays behind the login gate, so low is correct; the docstring's 'nothing else of the dashboard' claim is overstated.


### broll-4: The card popover's 'already in folder' ticks compare the raw stored video_id, so after an index rebuild they are wrong and a second add creates a duplicate entry

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `broll/web/app/routes_client_folders.py:77`

`list_folders(video_id=...)` computes `contains` from `SELECT folder_id FROM client_folder_items WHERE video_id = ?` on the CURRENT index id. Items are re-resolved by `(share, rel_path)` everywhere the folder is read (`resolve_items`), but not here and not in `add_items` (client_folders.py:382, `present` is a set of stored ids). After a rebuild renumbers ids, a clip that is in the folder shows as un-ticked, and pressing '+' inserts a second row (new id, same share/rel_path) -- `UNIQUE (folder_id, video_id)` does not catch it. The public page then lists the clip twice.

**Failure scenario.** Folder holds harbor.mov as id 12; archive rebuilt, harbor.mov is now id 4120. Editor opens the popover on harbor.mov: no tick. Presses '+': `add_items` sees 4120 not in {12}, inserts a second item. `resolve_items` now returns harbor twice (item 12 re-resolved to 4120, plus item 4120) and the client sees a duplicate card; removing via the panel only removes one of them per click.

**Suggested fix.** In `add_items` and the `contains` query, match on `(share, rel_path)` from `_video_identity` as well as on id (e.g. `WHERE folder_id = ? AND (video_id = ? OR (share = ? AND rel_path = ?))`), and have `resolve_items` dedupe by current id.

**Verifier.** Traced: routes_client_folders.py:77-78 computes `contains` from raw `client_folder_items.video_id`, and add_items (client_folders.py:382-403) builds `present` from stored ids only, with UNIQUE on (folder_id, video_id) not catching a second row with a new id for the same (share, rel_path). resolve_items then yields the clip twice (old item re-resolved by name plus the new item). Requires a renumbering rebuild, the same precondition as broll-1; low is fair since it is a duplicate card, not data loss.


### broll-5: Over the HTTP ingest backend, `set_error` and any update carrying fields outside VideoIn are silently dropped by the web app (status becomes 'error' with no message)

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `broll/indexer/broll_index/storage/http_backend.py:98`

`HttpBackend.set_error` -> `update_video(video_id, status='error', error=message)` POSTs `{share, rel_path, status, error}` to `/api/ingest/video`. `schemas.VideoIn` has no `error` field (nor `archive_path`, `original_path`, `duplicate_of`, `transcribed_at`), and pydantic's default `extra='ignore'` discards them without a 422, so `routes_ingest.ingest_video` writes `status='error'` and leaves `error` NULL. The indexer's local shadow keeps the message; the canonical web DB never sees why a clip failed, and nothing logs the mismatch. Real only for a deployment that runs the indexer in `api` mode (the base rig today uses the co-located sqlite backend plus publish_db, so this is dormant there).

**Failure scenario.** Indexer configured with `storage: api` against the NAS. A clip fails probe (`ffprobe failed ...`). The web DB row shows status 'error', error NULL; an operator looking at the archive (or a future UI surfacing `videos.error`) has no diagnosis, and the shadow DB that does hold it lives only on the indexing machine.

**Suggested fix.** Either add the missing optional columns (`error`, `archive_path`, `original_path`, ...) to `VideoIn` with COALESCE semantics on the upsert, or set `model_config = ConfigDict(extra='forbid')` on the ingest models so a field the contract does not carry is a loud 422 rather than a silent drop (the HttpBackend docstring already takes that stance for transcripts/embeddings).

**Verifier.** schemas.VideoIn (schemas.py:21-46) has no `error` field and no `model_config` with extra='forbid' anywhere in schemas.py (grep found none), so pydantic's default drops unknown keys silently. http_backend.py:98 sends `error=message` via update_video's payload. The ingest upsert updates status but never writes `error`. Dormant on the current deployment (co-located sqlite + publish_db), so low.


## Music platform (1 high / 3 medium / 2 low)

> **Coverage.** Read end to end: musicweb/{config,main,db,routes_api,routes_media,search,text_encoder,projection,drain,rescore,routes_ingest,routes_batches,routes_fleet,fleet_auth,identity,schemas,ingest_batches,vocab(hash)}.py, schema.sql, DEPLOY.md drain section; music/indexer/{index_music.py (drain/main/upsert/analyse_one), music_index/{config,ingest,audio,clap_model,proxies}.py, make_proxies.py, export_text_encoder.py (swap/check flow)}. Crossed the boundaries into dashboard/music.py + app.py login_gate carve-out + api.resolve_companion_credential, companion music_server.py / broll_server.py music dispatch / music_ingest.py (result, upload naming, token) / identity.preferred_report_token, broll_fetch MUSIC_REMOTE_REL, server/publish_db.py swap+post-swap message, and static/app.js send/reveal/status. Ran both suites read-only: music/web 456 passed, 2 skipped (torch-only checks); music/indexer 44 passed, 1 skipped (needs transformers). Verified the Range parser, the 200-for-ignored-Range streaming path, the (share, rel_path) body contract end to end (app.js -> /music/send -> contained_local_path -> Resolve worker), the mounted-prefix rule (all static URLs document-relative; the [ DASHBOARD ] link's "/" is intentional), the ONNX-on-CPU query path and its CLAP fallback, and require_db_path / require_tools refusals naming their keys: no defects found in those specifically-requested areas. Not covered: features.py, mel_numpy.py, export_audio_encoder.py internals, eval/, static/ingest.js beyond the duplicate-handling and URL checks, and anything that needs torch or a live Resolve. Known items checked and NOT re-reported: MUSIC-ING-1..6, MUSIC-10 (the reload route itself), CR-20 (pull-drain-push), CR-44/45.


### music-1: Music fleet ingest 403s for every editor holding a per-editor (cce1) report token

**HIGH, CONFIRMED.** `music/web/musicweb/fleet_auth.py:64`

`require_fleet_token` compares `X-CCSync-Token` against ONLY the shared `DASH_REPORT_TOKEN` (`config.fleet_token`, config.py:201). But the companion presents whatever `identity.preferred_report_token` picks, and since 2026-08-17 a per-editor `cce1.<id>.<secret>` token OUTRANKS the shared one and is published into `cfg["dashboard_token"]` (companion identity.py:139-164, 379-397) -- which is exactly what `IngestDeps.token` sends on every music fleet call (broll_ingest.py:270, 328). The dashboard's login_gate carve-out accepts that token (`_companion_credential` -> AUTH_EDITOR, app.py:378-397), so the request reaches musicweb, where it is refused with 403 'missing or invalid X-CCSync-Token'. `docs/BROLL_INGEST_PLAN.md:253` names per-editor acceptance as a 'follow-up for both apps', but the consequence is not in KNOWN_BUGS and the music docs (DEPLOY.md, MUSIC_INGEST_PLAN.md) do not warn that minting an editor a token switches their dashboard music ingest off. The same defect exists in broll/web/app/fleet_auth.py:62.

**Failure scenario.** Admin mints editor2 a per-editor token on Admin -> Users (the documented migration). editor2's companion adopts it (tray log 'using the report token from identity.json'). editor2 drops an album on /music: the page creates the batch, hands the uid to the companion, the companion POSTs /music/api/fleet/ingest/batches/<uid>/claim with X-CCSync-Token: cce1.… -> login_gate lets it through -> musicweb answers 403. The batch never leaves `queued`; editor2 sees nothing indexing and the companion log says the server refused its credentials. Once the admin sets DASH_SHARED_REPORT_TOKEN_ENABLED=0 (the documented end of the migration), this is every editor.

**Suggested fix.** musicweb cannot verify a cce1 token (it needs the dashboard db), so let the mount do it: in `MusicGate._identified_scope` (dashboard music.py) resolve `X-CCSync-Token` through `api.resolve_companion_credential`, strip any inbound `X-CCSync-Fleet-Auth`, and stamp `X-CCSync-Fleet-Auth: shared|editor:<name>` only when it verifies; have `require_fleet_token` accept that stamp when `config.login_gated()` is true (standalone keeps the shared-token compare). Alternatively give `config` an injectable `fleet_token_verifier` callable the mount sets. Add a test that a cce1-shaped token reaches `claim` as 200 when the gate stamped it, and apply the same to broll/web. Until then, document in DEPLOY.md that minting a per-editor token disables dashboard music/b-roll ingest for that editor.

**Verifier.** Traced end to end. companion identity.py:139-168 `preferred_report_token` puts a per-editor `editor_report_token` first and identity.py:375-397 publishes it into cfg['dashboard_token']; broll_ingest.py:270-273 `IngestDeps.token` reads exactly that key and :328 sends it as X-CCSync-Token; music_ingest.MusicIngestor subclasses BrollIngestor (music_ingest.py:70) so the same header is sent. dashboard app.py:564-573 `_companion_token_ok` -> `_companion_credential` (app.py:378-397) accepts a cce1 token (opens the db for it) and lets the `_music_fleet_re` path (app.py:614-617) through. music.py `_identified_scope` (149-172) only stamps the session identity/admin headers, leaves X-CCSync-Token untouched, and stamps nothing a sub-app could trust about the token. musicweb/fleet_auth.py:64-76 then compares the header against `config.fleet_token()` (config.py: DASH_REPORT_TOKEN) only, so a cce1 token 403s at `claim`. BROLL_INGEST_PLAN.md §9.3 records per-editor acceptance as a follow-up; grep of KNOWN_BUGS finds no entry for the consequence. Today the fleet is on the shared token so the blast radius is each editor an admin migrates; once DASH_SHARED_REPORT_TOKEN_ENABLED=0 it is every editor. High stands.


### music-2: A published or drained music index is not picked up: cached connections and the search matrices stay on the old data, and nothing in the runbooks triggers /api/reload

**MEDIUM (hunter said high), CONFIRMED.** `music/web/musicweb/search.py:198`

`search.index()` builds the numpy matrices once per process and only `refresh()` replaces them; the callers are the inline ingest route, the fleet `result` route, and `POST /api/reload` (routes_api.py:166). `db.con()` additionally caches one sqlite connection per thread for the thread's life (db.py:147-171), and anyio's worker pool reuses the most-recent idle thread (LIFO) and only retires a thread after 10 s idle -- on a dashboard polled every 2 s by htmx and every 60 s by each companion, the hot thread never dies.
