So after (a) `python -m musicweb.drain apply` on the NAS (DEPLOY.md:295-297) the new tracks are in the file but absent from text search and /api/similar until someone POSTs /music/api/reload; and after (b) `publish_db.py --which music --apply`, which REPLACES the file by rename, the hot thread's connection still reads the unlinked old inode, so browse, facets, audio lookups AND search all answer from the previous index. `publish_db.py:389-391` then prints 'the app reopens the database per connection, so a rename is normally enough' -- false for musicweb. No doc (DEPLOY.md, BACKUP_RESTORE.md §6, INDEXERS.md) mentions /api/reload or a restart for the music case, the SPA has no reload control, and /api/reload itself requires a browser session (it is under /music/api/ in login_gate). KNOWN_BUGS MUSIC-10 fixed the route's inode problem but noted the deploy's docker restart masked the rest; publish_db does no restart.

**Failure scenario.** Operator follows DEPLOY.md step 3a: drains 5 queued uploads, scp's drain.db, runs the sudo apply, sees 'applied 5 track(s)'. Editors searching /music get the same results as before; the 5 new cues are invisible to 'any moment'/'whole track' search and /api/similar, while the ingest panel's queue count shows them done. Same operator later runs `publish_db.py --which music --apply` after a full base-rig re-index; the tool prints the rename-is-enough message; /music keeps serving the old index (old counts on /api/stats, old tags) from a deleted inode for as long as the dashboard stays busy, i.e. indefinitely.

**Suggested fix.** (1) Make `db.con()` self-healing: record the live file's `os.stat(DB_PATH).st_ino` (and dev) at open, re-stat cheaply on each `con()` (or every N seconds) and call `invalidate()` plus `search.refresh()` when it changes; likewise compare `PRAGMA data_version` / the file mtime to detect an in-place drain apply and refresh the Index lazily on the next search. (2) Until then: have `drain.main` print 'now POST /music/api/reload (or restart the dashboard)', make `publish_db.py --which music` either restart the container or curl the reload route with an admin session, and put the step in DEPLOY.md 3a/4 and BACKUP_RESTORE.md §6. (3) Correct the publish_db message for music.

**Verifier.** Code traced: db.py:147-200 caches one connection per thread, reopened only when `_generation` changes (invalidate()); search.py:198-217 builds the matrices once and only refresh() swaps them; callers of refresh are routes_ingest.py:438, routes_fleet.py:195 and routes_api.py:166-179 (/api/reload, which sits under /music/api/ and is session-gated by login_gate app.py:673-681). `musicweb.drain apply` is stdlib sqlite writing directly to the file (drain.py:283-345) and touches neither; publish_db.py swaps by rename and its only docker use is the quick_check exec (publish_db.py:196), no restart; its rollback message (389-391) claims 'reopens per connection'. Grep of publish_db.py, drain.py, BACKUP_RESTORE.md, INDEXERS.md for 'reload'/'restart' finds nothing for the music case, and the SPA static dir has no reload control. Bug-hunt-2026-08-14 MUSIC-10 already notes the deploy's restart is what masks it. Scenario (a) drain-apply invisible to search until a reload, and (b) rename-publish served from the unlinked inode by long-lived threads, are both real. Downgraded to medium: the in-place drain-apply DOES show up in browse/facets/audio (same inode, no cache of rows) and only search/similar lag; the rename case self-heals on the next container restart, and the dashboard restarts on every redeploy; the fix is an ops step, not data loss.


### music-3: Drain failures never reach the live index: a queued upload that cannot be analysed stays `pending` on the NAS forever and is re-decoded by every drain

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `music/web/musicweb/drain.py:187`

`drain_queue` marks a row `failed` only in the PULLED COPY (index_music.py:264) and returns `drained` = the uids it closed as done (index_music.py:256). `export_bundle` iterates exactly those uids, so the bundle carries no record of failures, and `apply_bundle` only ever writes `state='done'` (drain.py:~300). The live `ingest_queue` row therefore stays `pending`: the ingest panel and `GET /api/ingest/queue` report it as waiting, `find_reencode`/`find_content_duplicate_by_digest` keep treating it as a held track (so re-dropping a fixed copy is refused as a duplicate), and the next pull-and-drain decodes it again because `queue_pending` selects pending rows. The journal's own design note ('a failed row is NEVER picked up again on its own... the reason never read', schema.sql) is defeated on the only index editors look at.

**Failure scenario.** An editor drops a corrupt .flac through the browser path; the container probes it fine (ffprobe reads the header) and queues it. Base rig drains: ffmpeg decode returns nothing, the row is parked failed in nas-index/music.db with 'no decodable audio', the bundle is exported without it and applied. On the NAS the row is still pending. The editor sees '1 pending' in the panel for weeks; re-dropping a good re-export of the same track answers 'already in the library as <rel_path> (same track)'; every subsequent drain re-decodes the corrupt file and fails again.

**Suggested fix.** Have `drain_queue` also return the (uid, error) pairs it parked, write them to a `bundle_failures(uid, content_hash, error, attempts)` table in `export_bundle`, and have `apply_bundle` mark the matching live row `failed` with that error (same uid + rel_path + content_hash agreement checks, never forcing a row that is already done). Bump BUNDLE_VERSION or make the table optional for older bundles. Add a test in test_drain_bundle.py that a failed drain row ends `failed` on the live index after apply.

**Verifier.** index_music.py:202-268: failures call db.queue_mark_failed on the PULLED copy and only successful uids go into `drained`; export_bundle (drain.py:170-200) iterates exactly those uids and the bundle schema has no failure table; apply_bundle (drain.py:283-330) writes only state=DONE. db.queue_pending (db.py:555-566) selects pending rows only, so on the live NAS journal the row stays pending, is re-pulled and re-decoded every drain, and stays visible as waiting. The schema note that a failed row is parked is honoured only on the base-rig copy. Medium is right: no data loss, but an editor-visible pending count that never clears and the duplicate defences still treating the file as held.


### music-4: Preview proxy is chosen by track id alone, so a reused rowid serves a different track's audio

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `music/web/musicweb/routes_media.py:93`

`audio_source` serves `PROXIES_DIR/<id>.mp3` whenever the file exists, with no check that the proxy belongs to the current row. `tracks.id` is `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY` without AUTOINCREMENT (schema.sql), so SQLite hands a new insert max(rowid)+1 -- i.e. the id of the most recently DELETED highest row is reused. `music_index/proxies.py:orphans` documents exactly this ('it inherits the old id -- and with it a proxy of completely different audio') but the only remedy is the manual base-rig `make_proxies.py --prune`, and the NAS's `/music-proxies` dir is a separately shipped directory nothing prunes. Since 2026-08-18 new rows are inserted on the NAS directly (fleet `result`, drain apply), so the reuse now happens on the host that holds the stale proxies.

**Failure scenario.** Base rig sweep prunes the highest-id cue (id 412, a renamed Epidemic track) and the operator publishes the db; 412.mp3 is still in /music-proxies. An editor then dashboard-ingests 'Slow Build.wav': `write_item_result` inserts it and SQLite gives it id 412. Every editor previewing 'Slow Build' in /music hears the old Epidemic cue (X-Audio-Source: proxy); the waveform and tags are for the new track. `?original=1` plays the right file, and Resolve gets the right file, so the bug looks like 'the preview is wrong' and is blamed on the library.

**Suggested fix.** Tie the proxy to the row, not the id: either name proxies `<id>-<first 8 hex of file_hash or content_hash>.mp3` (proxy_path takes the row), or have `audio_source` compare `proxy.stat().st_mtime >= analyzed_at` and fall back to the original when the proxy predates the row, or store the proxied file_hash in a sidecar/meta row the reader checks. Cheapest immediate guard: in `write_item_result`/`apply_bundle`/`upsert`, `unlink(proxy_path(new_id))` when a row is created at an id that did not previously exist, and make `prune_missing` delete the pruned ids' proxies.

**Verifier.** music/web/schema.sql:16-17 `id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY` without AUTOINCREMENT, so SQLite allocates max(rowid)+1 and reuses the id of a deleted highest row. routes_media.py:93-97 picks PROXIES_DIR/<id>.mp3 on existence only; the only proxy_path user in musicweb is that route, and neither ingest_batches.py:749 (fleet INSERT) nor drain.py:312 nor prune_missing (db.py:283+) unlinks a proxy. music_index/proxies.py:322-330 documents the exact hazard; the broom is a manual base-rig --prune and nothing prunes the NAS /music-proxies dir. There is also a second, simpler path: since fleet `result` inserts on the NAS, NAS ids and base-rig ids diverge after any base-rig sweep, and a later `--music-data proxies` push ships a proxy encoded for base-rig id N over a NAS row N of different audio. Reachable, preview-only (original and Resolve correct), medium.


### music-5: An embedding or window vector whose byte count is not a multiple of 4 turns into a 500 instead of a 422

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `music/web/musicweb/ingest_batches.py:680`

`_embedding_blob` base64-decodes the body and calls `np.frombuffer(raw, dtype='<f4')`; numpy raises `ValueError: buffer size must be a multiple of element size` for a payload of, say, 2049 bytes, and nothing catches it, so the fleet route answers a 500 with a traceback. `_write_windows` (line 831) has the same call inside the item transaction, where the exception also aborts the `with conn:` block after the track row's INSERT statement has run (rolled back, but the companion only sees a 500 and retries). The schemas module's stated rule (DAS H-5: attacker-shaped input on a token-reachable route must be a refusal, never a 500) covers exactly this case.

**Failure scenario.** A companion whose sidecar truncates the pooled vector (or any fleet-token holder sending `{"embedding": "<base64 of 2049 bytes>"}`) POSTs /items/<uid>/result. The server 500s; the companion treats it as transient and retries the same body until its attempt budget is exhausted, logging tracebacks on the dashboard each time.

**Suggested fix.** In `_embedding_blob` and `_write_windows`, check `len(raw) % 4 == 0` (and `len(raw) >= 4`) before `np.frombuffer`, raising `HTTPException(422, {'detail': 'the embedding is not a float32 vector', 'reason': 'embedding'})`; add a test with a 5-byte embedding.

**Verifier.** schemas.py:42-51 `_decode` validates base64 only; ingest_batches.py:680 and :831 call np.frombuffer(raw, '<f4') with no length check, and numpy raises ValueError for a byte count not divisible by 4. No exception handler in musicweb maps ValueError to 422, and no test covers it (grep for 'multiple of'/'% 4' in tests is empty). At :831 the call is inside `with conn:` after the tracks INSERT, so the transaction rolls back and the companion sees a 500. Fleet-token-gated route, so low.


### music-6: DEPLOY.md and the ledger docstring still promise the base-rig fallback uploads the audio and queues a journal row; neither happens

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `music/web/DEPLOY.md:144`. Related: MUSIC-ING-2

The comparison table says the queued path is 'still supported -- yes ... the item ends `queued_for_base_rig` and a `pending` journal row waits for the drain', and `ingest_batches.py:85-90` repeats that the companion 'uploads the file to the NAS and leaves an `ingest_queue` row'. In code there is no `queue_add` anywhere in ingest_batches.py or routes_fleet.py, and the companion cannot upload because `dest_name` is only allocated at `result` (music_ingest.py `_enqueue_uploads` fails the item with 'the library did not name this track'). KNOWN_BUGS MUSIC-ING-2 records the companion half of this but the server-side docs still describe the feature as working.

**Failure scenario.** An operator reading DEPLOY.md decides a companion without the CLAP artefact is fine ('the base rig drains it later'), lets editors drop albums on such machines, and later runs a drain that finds nothing: the items ended `queued_for_base_rig` with the audio still in the editors' staging dirs and no journal rows.

**Suggested fix.** Until the `POST .../items/{iuid}/queue` route from MUSIC-ING-2 exists, change DEPLOY.md:144 and the ingest_batches.py docstring to say the fallback leaves the file on the editor's machine and the page asks them to re-drop it through the browser upload; cross-reference MUSIC-ING-2.

**Verifier.** DEPLOY.md:144 says the fallback item ends queued_for_base_rig with a pending journal row waiting for the drain; ingest_batches.py:85-90 says the companion 'uploads the file to the NAS and leaves an ingest_queue row'. queue_add exists only in db.py:522 and is called only from routes_ingest.py:365 (the browser upload path), never from ingest_batches.py or routes_fleet.py. KNOWN_BUGS MUSIC-ING-2 (line 343-353) documents that the audio stays on the editor's machine and must be re-dropped, contradicting both docs. Docs-vs-code, low.


## Ytdl web service (1 high / 2 medium / 2 low)

> **Coverage.** Read end to end: db.py (schema runner, migrations 002-009 and their predicates, the claim/lease CAS family, reset_stale_jobs), routes_api.py, routes_fleet.py, worker.py, ai_backend.py, the health/ask_json/generate_terms/filter_relevance half of claude_cli.py, config.py, identity.py, session.py, attestation.py, projects.py, main.py, dashboard/ytdl.py (YtdlGate + mount), dashboard/ai_providers.py (resolver, probe cache, lookup_payload), the login_gate carve-outs in dashboard/app.py, the companion's ytdl_executor FleetClient (_call retry, _headers, _download_one) and the SPA's dispatchLocal/project picker. Ran the suite read-only with the dashboard venv: 631 passed (tests/conftest.py still builds the dashboard `selections` table in its pre-v24 shape, so the per-machine key is untested here). KNOWN_BUGS CR-29..CR-45, YTDL-WEB-1..8 and R18 were checked and are not re-reported; CR-37's fix (clear_mode_lock resetting all four columns) is in place. Not covered: vendor/ytsearch.py, vendor/downloader.py, ytdl_common.py, static/app.js beyond the dispatch/picker/cancel paths, the CLI setup wizard (cli_tools.py) beyond cli_env/cli_env_overlay, and the Anthropic SDK call shape (anthropic 0.122.0 accepts `output_config`, verified by signature only). Schema/migration ordering (predicate-driven, schema.sql re-run last, user_version stamped by ensure_schema) looked correct; the ensure_schema `_schema_ready` flag is not thread-safe but every path it races is idempotent.


### ytdl-web-1: Fleet routes accept only the shared DASH_REPORT_TOKEN, so the documented move to per-editor tokens silently kills requester-first downloads

**HIGH, CONFIRMED.** `ytdl/web/ytdlweb/routes_fleet.py:83`. Related: CR-18 (per-editor tokens), CR-29/CR-38 (requester-first invisible failures)

`require_fleet_token` compares `X-CCSync-Token` against `config.REPORT_TOKEN` (config.py:325, the shared `DASH_REPORT_TOKEN`) and nothing else. The dashboard's login_gate carve-out for `/ytdl/api/jobs/{id}/(claim|heartbeat|download-manifest|clips/*/status)` (app.py:581-655) goes through `_companion_credential`, which accepts BOTH the shared token and a per-editor `cce1.` token and honours `DASH_SHARED_REPORT_TOKEN_ENABLED` (api.py:128-150). The sub-app does not: a per-editor token that the gate lets through is 403'd one layer down, and the shared token the sub-app insists on is exactly the one the dashboard's own boot log tells the operator to retire (`_log_shared_token_migration`, app.py:178-183: 'mint per-editor tokens on Admin > Users, then set DASH_SHARED_REPORT_TOKEN_ENABLED=0'). The companion's ytdl client compounds it by always sending `cfg['dashboard_token']` (ytdl_executor.py:430-437) rather than `identity.preferred_report_token`, but even a fixed companion could not get past routes_fleet.

**Failure scenario.** Operator follows the boot warning: mints per-editor tokens, sets DASH_SHARED_REPORT_TOKEN_ENABLED=0, redeploys. Editor presses DOWNLOAD; the SPA dispatches to the loopback; the companion POSTs /ytdl/api/jobs/50/claim with the shared token -> login_gate answers 401 (shared disabled) -> companion logs 'could not be claimed' and stands down -> the NAS worker downloads every clip. Since 2026-08-16 lane B does not bring YouTube originals down, so every editor in the fleet stops receiving their own clips, with no error anywhere but a DEBUG-level companion line. If the companion were changed to send its cce1 token instead, the gate passes it and routes_fleet.py:83 answers 403 'missing or invalid X-CCSync-Token' for the same result.

**Suggested fix.** Make require_fleet_token accept either credential the dashboard accepts: have the dashboard mount pass a verifier callback (the way ai_backend.set_provider_lookup is installed) that runs api.resolve_companion_credential, and when AUTH_EDITOR is returned require that the bound editor equals the X-CCSync-Identity name; keep the env-only comparison as the standalone fallback. Have the companion's ytdl FleetClient use identity.preferred_report_token. Add a ytdl test that a cce1 token is accepted when the hook is installed and a shared token is refused when shared_report_token_enabled is false.

**Verifier.** Traced end to end. ytdl/web/ytdlweb/routes_fleet.py:83 compares X-CCSync-Token only against config.REPORT_TOKEN (config.py:325 = DASH_REPORT_TOKEN). The dashboard gate (app.py, _ytdl_fleet_re + _companion_token_ok -> api.resolve_companion_credential, api.py:128-150) accepts a cce1 per-editor token as AUTH_EDITOR, so such a request passes the gate and is then 403'd by the sub-app. One detail in the report is wrong but makes the defect WORSE, not weaker: the companion does not ignore preferred_report_token -- IdentityManager (identity.py:386-396) writes preferred_report_token() INTO cfg['dashboard_token'], and a per-editor token outranks the shared one there. So the moment an admin mints a cce1 token for an editor (KNOWN_BUGS CR-18, the documented migration path) and it lands in identity.json/config.toml, every ytdl claim/heartbeat/status POST from that editor carries the cce1 token and routes_fleet answers 403 -- even with DASH_SHARED_REPORT_TOKEN_ENABLED still 1. Flipping the shared token off then kills requester-first for the rest of the fleet at the gate (401) as described. No ytdl test covers a cce1 token (grep of ytdl/web/tests finds none). Same pattern exists in broll/web/app/fleet_auth.py:62-74 (and the music twin), so the CR-18 migration silently disables every companion-executed fleet feature, not just ytdl. Kept at high: a real editor path (requester-first downloads, the only way YouTube originals reach an editor since 2026-08-16) breaks with only a DEBUG/stand-down line; not critical because the server worker still downloads to the NAS.


### ytdl-web-2: Project picker lists each project once per machine since the dashboard's v24 selections key

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `ytdl/web/ytdlweb/projects.py:436`. Related: docs/MULTI_MACHINE_PLAN.md (per-machine plans, dashboard 0.7.0)

`_SQL` is `SELECT s.project_slug, p.label FROM selections s JOIN projects p ... WHERE s.editor_username=? ORDER BY s.position` with no DISTINCT and no machine predicate. Since dashboard schema v24 (db.py:703-726, shipped as dashboard 0.7.0 on 2026-08-19) `selections` is keyed `(editor_username, machine, project_slug)`, and the migration itself fans every existing row out to one row per machine the editor owns. The SPA renders `r.projects.forEach(... appendChild(option))` with no dedupe (app.js:428-432). CLAUDE.md defines a request without `?machine=` as 'the PERSON: the union to read' -- a union, not a multiset. ytdl/web/tests/conftest.py:113 still creates the pre-v24 two-key table, so the suite cannot see this.

**Failure scenario.** An editor with a laptop and a desktop both registered in `machines` (or any editor who existed before the v24 migration and had two machine rows) opens /ytdl: the project <select> shows '2026/FF5/Energy Transition' twice (three times with a base rig row), once per machine. `resolve_project` still works, `loadProject` selects the first match, so searches go through -- but every project in the picker is duplicated for every multi-machine editor, which reads as a broken page.

**Suggested fix.** Use `SELECT DISTINCT s.project_slug AS slug, p.label AS label ... ORDER BY MIN(s.position)` (GROUP BY slug, label) so the picker is the person's union; update tests/conftest.py's copied schema to the v24 shape and add a test with two machine rows for one editor expecting one entry.

**Verifier.** projects.py:35-38 _SQL selects from selections with no DISTINCT and no machine predicate. dashboard db.py SCHEMA_V24 (lines 703-726) rekeys selections to (editor_username, machine, project_slug) and the migration fans each pre-existing row out to one row per row in `machines` for that editor. ytdl reads the dashboard's live DB (the one on v24+ since dashboard 0.7.0 shipped 2026-08-19), so an editor with two machine rows gets each project twice; app.js:428-432 appends an option per row with no dedupe. ytdl/web/tests/conftest.py:113-119 still creates the old two-column PK table, which is why the suite is blind to it. Functionally the first duplicate is selected and searches work, so medium is right (visible breakage on a page every multi-machine editor opens, no data impact).


### ytdl-web-3: clip status posts are not idempotent, so the companion's CR-31 transport retry double-counts dl_done/dl_failed

**MEDIUM, CONFIRMED.** `ytdl/web/ytdlweb/routes_fleet.py:538`. Related: CR-31 (companion transport retry), YTDL-WEB-4

`clip_status` records `done` (`_record_done` -> set_video + ledger_add + `bump(dl_done)`) and `failed` (set_video + `bump(dl_failed)`) without looking at the row's current `dl_state`. The worker's own path is guarded by the `begin_download` CAS (db.py:1085) but this route has no equivalent. Since companion 0.9.4 (CR-31) `FleetClient._call` retries any RAISED transport failure for up to 60 s (ytdl_executor.py:766-792), and a client-side timeout (`self.timeout`, 15 s) on a POST the server already processed is exactly such a failure -- the retry re-POSTs the same terminal state.

**Failure scenario.** Job of 22 clips on the editor's machine. Clip 7's `done` POST is processed by the server but the response is lost to a dashboard restart / slow NAS; the companion re-sends it. `dl_done` is bumped twice, so the poll shows '23 of 22'. Worse for `failed`: two bumps put dl_failed at 2 for one clip; at hand-back `requeue_failed` returns 1 and `bump(dl_failed, -1)` leaves dl_failed=1 permanently, so a job whose server retry succeeded still reports one failed clip in the SPA and in Recent searches.

**Suggested fix.** Make the two terminal writes compare-and-set on the row state: `UPDATE job_videos SET dl_state='done',... WHERE job_id=? AND video_id=? AND dl_state IN ('pending','downloading')` (a helper beside begin_download), bump the counter only when rowcount is 1, and answer `{ok:true, state:'done', duplicate:true}` otherwise. Add a test posting `done` twice and `failed` twice and asserting the counters.

**Verifier.** routes_fleet.py clip_status (lines 524-560): `done` -> _record_done -> set_video + ledger_add (upsert, harmless) + db.bump(dl_done); `failed` -> set_video + bump(dl_failed); neither reads the row's current dl_state, and db.bump (db.py:522) is an unconditional += . The companion's FleetClient._call (ytdl_executor.py:748-795) re-issues the same POST on ANY raised transport failure (including a client timeout after the server already committed) for up to CALL_RETRY_BUDGET_SECONDS, so a duplicate terminal POST is reachable exactly as described. _after_terminal's hand-back uses unfinished_downloads (state-based) so job completion is unaffected, but the counters drift: dl_done over-counts, and a duplicated `failed` leaves dl_failed permanently +1 after _hand_back_to_the_server's requeue_failed/bump(-n) (which uses the row count, not the counter). Cosmetic-to-misleading job status in the SPA and history; medium.


### ytdl-web-4: AI health is probed once at worker boot and never again, so a transient boot-time failure pins the red pip until a job happens to succeed

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `ytdl/web/ytdlweb/worker.py:171`. Related: YTDL-5

`claude_cli.refresh_health(force=True)` is the only call site of `refresh_health` in the tree; `_MIN_PROBE_INTERVAL` (claude_cli.py:897) guards a re-probe that nothing ever requests. The cache is afterwards written only by `_note_ok`/`note_failure` from live calls. The worker thread starts at dashboard mount time (ytdl.py:383), i.e. during create_app before the lifespan, which on a NAS boot is routinely before WAN egress is up.

**Failure scenario.** Container starts at NAS boot before the uplink; the boot probe's Messages call fails with APIConnectionError -> health = 'missing' with 'could not reach the Anthropic API from this container'. Every editor's /ytdl page shows the red 'AI missing' pip for hours/days; nothing re-checks until someone ignores the warning and submits a search (which then succeeds and flips it green).

**Suggested fix.** Re-run `refresh_health()` (non-forced, so _MIN_PROBE_INTERVAL applies) from the worker's idle loop when the cached state is not 'ok' and checked_at is older than the interval, or have GET /api/health nudge the worker to re-probe when the cache is stale and red.

**Verifier.** grep confirms claude_cli.refresh_health is called exactly once, worker.py:171 with force=True at thread start; thereafter the cache changes only via _note_ok/note_failure from live calls (claude_cli.py:946-960). The worker starts at dashboard mount time. routes_api.py:107 /api/health deliberately never probes. The SPA (app.js:357-371) only shows a banner for 'missing'/'unauthenticated'; it does not disable submission, so recovery via a live call is possible and the module's own docstring treats that as the designed recovery (YTDL-5). Net effect: a transient boot-time failure leaves a stale red pip until somebody submits a job anyway. Real but minor; low is correct.


### ytdl-web-5: Resolving the provider per AI call probes every enabled CLI with a live billed call, even when an API provider is pinned

**LOW, CONFIRMED.** `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/ai_providers.py:733`

`lookup_payload` -> `resolved()` -> `provider_states(probe=True)` probes each CLI provider (`probe_cli`: `--version` plus a real one-token completion, up to 10 s + 60 s) whenever `cli_enabled` is true, before `resolve_provider` looks at the pin. The probe cache TTL is 600 s, and ytdl calls `current_provider()` on every AI call (refresh at boot, generate_terms, each relevance batch) from the worker thread.

**Failure scenario.** Site with ai_cli_providers on, Claude Code installed and signed in via the wizard, and `ai_provider_preference=anthropic_api` pinned. Every ~10 minutes of ytdl activity the worker spends a Claude Code subscription call (the thing the admin pinned away from) and blocks up to 70 s inside the job just to learn an availability it then ignores; a relevance phase with five batches straddling a TTL boundary pays it mid-job.

**Suggested fix.** Short-circuit in `lookup_payload`/`resolved`: when a pin is set, probe only the pinned provider; otherwise probe CLIs lazily in chain order and stop at the first available. Keep the full probe for the Settings page snapshot only.

**Verifier.** lookup_payload (ai_providers.py:740) -> resolved (733) -> provider_states(probe=True) (631-726), which for every CLI provider calls probe_cli when cli_enabled, regardless of the pin; resolve_provider applies the preference only afterwards. probe_cli (498-560) runs `--version` plus a real one-token completion (LOGIN_PROBE_TIMEOUT) and caches for PROBE_TTL_SECONDS = 600 (line 435). ai_backend.current_provider (ytdl ai_backend.py:212-243) calls the lookup fresh on every AI call, and refresh_health calls it too. So with ai_cli_providers on and an API provider pinned, each 10-minute TTL expiry during ytdl activity spends a billed/subscription CLI call and can block the worker for the probe duration. Efficiency/cost only, no correctness impact, and it requires the off-by-default feature flag; low.


---

# Part 2: design review (macro fleet)

Six reviewers, one lens each, asked for logical inconsistencies, inefficiencies, fragility and gaps in the APPROACH, with evidence, and for strengths that must not be broken in fixing them.


## Lens: Sync safety and data-loss model across lanes A, B and C: coherence of the latches (breaker, halt, ignoreDelete, removal gate, base rig holds no tick), cross-lane gaps, and where the design relies on humans.

The latch set is coherent about its core promise and the code honours it: the only thing that deletes on an editor's disk is lane B's `rclone sync`, and it is boxed three ways (--backup-dir into a per-run trash, rclone's per-pass caps, and a persisted breaker that bounds the sequence and is cleared only by a human, now reachable from the dashboard). The NAS side has no automated deleter at all: lane A is `copy --ignore-existing`, Syncthing runs `ignoreDelete` on every folder on both sides, and the collector's drift repair keeps that asserted. The halt is persisted, refuses local release of a fleet halt, and absence of the `commands.halt` key is treated as no information. The removal gate fails closed. Those properties are well commented, tested (test_lane_guard.py, test_sync_halt.py), and documented in SYNC_SAFETY.md.

The gaps are between lanes rather than inside any one of them. (1) Lane A has no memory of what it uploaded, so any reorganisation done on the NAS (the path SPEC.md §"Flaws" item 2 tells operators to use) is silently undone by every editor who still holds the originals at the old path: CR-44's 148-original move would have been re-uploaded, proxied by BPG, and fanned back out by lane B; the breaker fix made the trip go away without noticing that lane A rebuilds the folder. (2) The halt pauses only the selection's folders; the shared asset libraries keep syncing through a "stop everything", and the doc and test both say "every lane C folder". (3) "Remove from this machine" unticks the person everywhere, which stops lane A on the editor's other computer. (4) The halt release and the marker check each bypass a guard that exists elsewhere in the same codebase. (5) The one unguarded deletion surface (a human on the NAS) is covered only by ZFS snapshots that KNOWN_BUGS CR-10 still records as "code, not protection", with nothing on the dashboard saying so. Human reliance is signposted well for the breaker and the halt, less well for "who moves folders, and where".

**Strengths (do not break):**

- One deleter on the editor side: only lane B's `rclone sync` removes local files, and it is boxed three ways (per-run --backup-dir trash, rclone --max-delete/--max-delete-size, and the persisted breaker bounding the sequence). Every other local mutation is copy/move/repoint (fixer, repath, proxy_relink), and the removal gate fails closed.
- No automated deleter on the NAS side: lane A is copy --ignore-existing, every Syncthing folder carries ignoreDelete on both ends, and the collector's FOLDER_TUNING_KEYS drift repair re-asserts it each cycle, so a delete-protection regression heals itself.
- Latches are on disk, not in memory, and the asymmetry is right: absence of `commands.halt` holds a live halt, a fleet halt refuses local release, the dashboard's resume request is idempotent against `tripped` and cleared only when the machine reports clear.
- The breaker parks lane B `paused`, never `error`, and lanes A and C keep running, so a safety stop never costs an editor their uploads; the relocation probe is lazy, clamped to the pass's own deletions, and every failure in it falls back to 'treat as deletions'.
- Trash retention refuses to prune while tripped and never prunes the newest batch; the policy reversal from AUDIT_2 C-7 is stated with its reason.
- The removal gate asks both outbound lanes with their own tools (lane A dry-run with the real filter file, Syncthing completion) and makes an override cost typing the project name, a log line and a report field.
- The base rig / wired machine cannot hold a tick (db.base_only_editors, 409 on tick) and remove_project refuses when effective_mode() is base, so the machine whose local_root IS the NAS tree can never rmtree it.
- The enforce cycle's blast-radius brake counts share removals, not devices, and the B16 lesson (an empty selections read must never unshare the fleet) is encoded in three places with the history attached.
- Syncthing supervision respects every latch (halt, pause, sync_enabled=false) and lane C can no longer report green while the engine is down.
- SYNC_SAFETY.md and KNOWN_BUGS name the human step at each latch (who clears what, from where, and what to check first) rather than pretending the software decides.


### sync-safety-1: Lane A resurrects any folder reorganisation done on the NAS (the reorganisation path SPEC recommends)

**HIGH, gap, effort large.**

**Evidence.** companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/rclone_lane.py:1333-1343 (`copy --ignore-existing` per project subpath, no upload ledger; the only guards are --min-age/--min-size); SPEC.md:332 ("folder reorganizations must happen on the server side"); KNOWN_BUGS.md:1401-1436 (CR-44: 148 originals + 214 proxies moved on the NAS; the fix only teaches lane B's breaker to tolerate it); SPEC.md:103 (repath handles project-level moves only, "so lane A can't resurrect the old NAS path" - there is no equivalent for moves inside a project); docs/delete-protection-ignoredelete.md:75-84 (editor-side ignoreDelete keeps the old small files too).

**Consequence.** Admin drags `Interviewees/Creator_Interviews` into `B-roll/...` on the NAS. On the next pass of any editor who shot or previously held those originals, lane A finds them absent at the old remote path and re-uploads all of them over the WAN (hundreds of GB); lane C's ignoreDelete keeps the old sidecars on every editor; BPG/proxy_gen on a wired machine then generates proxies for the resurrected originals and lane B distributes them to the fleet. The move is undone piecemeal with every lane green, and the NAS now holds two copies of the shoot. (The same mechanism is, incidentally, the only thing that brings back an original a human deletes on the NAS.)

**Recommendation.** Give lane A a notion of "this file has reached the NAS somewhere": cheapest is a per-project uploaded-ledger (rel path + size + mtime, written from rclone's completion log, stored under ~/.ccsync/state) consulted before the periodic pass, so a file that WAS uploaded and is now missing remotely is reported as "moved/removed on the server" instead of re-sent; or extend repath.py to within-project moves by matching basename+size against a recursive remote listing (the CR-44 probe already produces that listing) and moving the local originals to follow. Until then, SPEC §Flaws item 2 and SYNC_SAFETY's runbook must say that a NAS-side move re-uploads from every editor holding originals, and that the editors' local copies must be moved (or removed) first.


### sync-safety-2: The halt (local and fleet) does not pause the shared asset folders, though the doc and its test say "every lane C folder"

**MEDIUM, inconsistency, effort small.**

**Evidence.** companion/src/ccsync_companion/app.py:4157-4170 (`_pause_lane_c_folders` iterates `sequencer.expected_folder_slugs()`); sync/sequencer.py:453-462 (that list is `_slug_to_item`, the project selection only); sync/shared_folders.py:13 ("never paused by the rotation, because it is not part of it") and :156-158 (reconcile RELEASES a paused shared folder whenever it runs); sequencer.py:549 (reconcile runs from the sequencer pass, which the halt stops, so the folders are simply left running); docs/SYNC_SAFETY.md §4 ("pauses every lane C folder"); companion/tests/test_sync_halt.py:67,80 (the fake sequencer's expected_folder_slugs is the whole universe the test checks).

**Consequence.** An admin presses FLEET SYNC HALT because a bad music ingest or a mass rename in `Assets/B-roll Archive` is spreading. Every editor's tray says "Nothing is uploading, downloading or sharing", but the B-roll Archive, Music and LUT folders keep syncing additions and modifications (ignoreDelete only blocks deletes) on every machine. On release, the reconcile would also have unpaused any shared folder an admin had paused by hand as soon as the sequencer resumes.

**Recommendation.** Have the halt set include the SharedFolderManager's folder ids (expose `shared_folders.folder_ids()` and union it in `_pause_lane_c_folders`), make `_reconcile_one` refuse to unpause while `HaltState.active`, and extend test_sync_halt so the fake sequencer exposes an asset folder that must be paused too. Correct the sentence in SYNC_SAFETY.md either way.


### sync-safety-3: "Remove from this machine" unticks the PERSON on every machine, which silently stops lane A uploads on the editor's other computer

**MEDIUM, inconsistency, effort small.**

**Evidence.** companion/src/ccsync_companion/selection.py:341-346 ("NO ?machine= here, deliberately ... Removals go wide"); dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/db.py:1936-1954 (`remove_selection(machine=None)` deletes every row for the editor+slug, including the other machine's own plan rows); app.py:3342-3356 (step 1 "untick it on the dashboard ... lanes stop scheduling it"); CLAUDE.md "A sync plan belongs to a COMPUTER" and "under-sharing is the safe direction for a removal".

**Consequence.** An editor with a desktop and a laptop (two plans, per MULTI_MACHINE_PLAN) removes project X from the laptop to free its SSD. The desktop's tick is deleted too: its sequencer drops X on the next poll, the server unshares the desktop's Syncthing device, and lane A stops uploading the desktop's new originals for X. Footage shot that week on the desktop sits un-backed-up with no warning, because the desktop shows X as simply "not selected". "Under-sharing is safe" is true for lane C and false for lane A.

**Recommendation.** When the requesting machine has rows of its own (`selections_for_machine` returned machine-scoped rows), untick with `?machine=<this>`; only fall back to the person-wide DELETE when this machine is on the unassigned bucket (the case the comment worries about). The dashboard already distinguishes the two. At minimum, the tray confirm should say it also unticks the project on the editor's other machines, naming them.


### sync-safety-4: Halt release unpauses every selected folder directly, bypassing the sequencer's "ignores never landed, stay paused" latch

**MEDIUM, fragility, effort small.**

**Evidence.** companion/src/ccsync_companion/app.py:4127-4135 (`release_halt` -> `_pause_lane_c_folders(False)`) and :4383 (fleet release does the same); app.py:4167 calls `syncthing_admin.set_folder_paused` (sync/syncthing_admin.py:368-371, a bare PATCH); sync/sequencer.py:869-894 (`_unpause_all` skips folders where `_ignores_unconfirmed_for(slug)`); syncthing_admin.py accept_folder docstring ("Created paused ... Only unpaused after set_ignores() succeeds ... the 'it stays paused' promise has to survive past this call").

**Consequence.** A folder accepted just before a halt whose set_ignores POST failed is left paused by design. The halt pauses it again (no-op); the release unpauses it unconditionally. A sendreceive folder with no .stignore goes online and indexes and offers every original and every Proxy/ file under the project; only the NAS's and other editors' own ignore lists prevent the transfer, and any peer whose ignores also failed pulls them. The sequencer's subsequent `_startup_unpause` never re-pauses it.

**Recommendation.** Route both release paths through the sequencer (a `release_for_halt()` that reuses `_unpause_all`'s filter), or have `_pause_lane_c_folders(False)` consult `syncthing_admin.ignores_confirmed(slug)` before each PATCH, matching the per-turn re-assert that eventually releases such a folder.


### sync-safety-6: The one unguarded deletion surface (a human on the NAS) rests on snapshots that are still 'code, not protection', and nothing in the product says so

**MEDIUM, gap, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** KNOWN_BUGS.md:612-629 (CR-10: "until then this entry is code, not protection"; setup_snapshots.py --apply still owed on both NASes as of the ledger); docs/BACKUP_RESTORE.md:20-21 (snapshots named as the first line for the tree and the ONLY line for video in Assets); docs/SYNC_SAFETY.md has no mention of snapshots; companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/syncthing_admin.py:163-164 (editor versioning maxAge 30 d) vs dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/provision.py:247-248 and server/setup_syncthing_folder.py:459-462 (365 d), flagged "deliberately untouched" in KNOWN_BUGS R5; rclone_lane.py build_down_command (lane B mirrors NAS deletions down, bounded by --max-delete 100 and the breaker's 50/pass).

**Consequence.** An admin deletes a Proxy folder or a shoot on the NAS by hand. Nothing on the NAS side objects; lane B mirrors up to 50 proxies per pass per editor into 14-day trashes before tripping, and video originals have no Syncthing version at all. If `setup_snapshots.py --apply` was never run (the ledger's current state), the only recovery is whichever editor still holds the originals locally, via the very lane A behaviour sync-safety-1 describes. The dashboard, which holds TRUENAS_API_KEY and renders every other safety chip, shows nothing about the missing snapshot schedule.

**Recommendation.** Have the collector (or health.py) query the snapshot task via the existing truenas_client and raise a fleet banner when no task matches the schedule `setup_snapshots.py` would create; list snapshots in SYNC_SAFETY.md as the latch for NAS-side deletion; reconcile the maxAge disagreement to one value.


### sync-safety-5: The breaker's remote_root marker check never runs in managed mode; the doc credits it with catching a wrong remote_root

**LOW, inconsistency, effort small.**

**Evidence.** companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/lane_guard.py `check_remote`: `if not key and self.marker_dirs:` (whole-tree scope only); sync/sequencer.py:1144-1145 and :1230 (managed mode always passes a project subpath, so `key` is never empty); docs/SYNC_SAFETY.md §1 table row 1 and KNOWN_BUGS CR-11 ("trips BEFORE a pass on a marker-less ... remote"); companion/tests/test_lane_guard.py:45 tests the unit in isolation only.

**Consequence.** Today harmless for a plain typo (rclone fails the pass on a missing source dir and deletes nothing). But a remote_root pointing at a stale copy of the tree that still holds `Projects/<rel>` (a backup dataset, a snapshot clone mounted for a restore drill) passes the only pre-flight probe in managed mode, and the pass then trashes every proxy newer than the stale copy up to the per-pass caps, trigger 2/3 permitting. Operators reading SYNC_SAFETY believe the typo guard is live on their fleet.

**Recommendation.** Probe `remote_root` itself for the marker dirs once per sequencer pass (one `rclone lsf`, cached for the process) independent of scope, and say in the doc which triggers apply per scope.


### sync-safety-7: A NAS-side move costs every editor a trash + full re-download + a recursive remote listing; rclone can rename locally instead

**LOW, inefficiency, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/rclone_lane.py:1553-1575 (lane B argv: sync + --backup-dir + caps, no --track-renames) and :2963-2995 (`_count_relocations` re-lists the scope with `lsf -R` after the pass has already trashed the files); KNOWN_BUGS CR-44 (214 proxies trashed and re-fetched for a move that changed no bytes).

**Consequence.** Every reorganisation on the NAS is paid for by each editor as a full re-download of the moved proxies over the HiNet upstream, plus a 10-minute-capped recursive listing inside `_run_lock` that delays every other project's turn, plus trash growth counted against the 50 GB cap.

**Recommendation.** Evaluate `--track-renames --track-renames-strategy modtime,leaf` (or `hash`) on lane B against the bundled rclone: the destination is local so renames are supported; verify interaction with --backup-dir and the filter file in test_rclone_filters.py before adopting, and keep the relocation probe as the fallback for anything track-renames cannot match.


### sync-safety-8: Breaker and halt state files are written non-atomically and a torn write fails OPEN, also losing the baseline trigger 1 depends on

**LOW, fragility, effort small.**

**Evidence.** companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/lane_guard.py `_write_json` ("Not atomic on purpose ... a torn write degrades to {} -- which for the breaker means 'not tripped'"); `LaneBBreaker.__init__` reads `remote_counts`, `deletes` from the same file; SYNC_SAFETY.md §1 ("persisted ... so a trip survives the tray restart").

**Consequence.** Power loss or a self-upgrade kill mid-write leaves an empty/partial lane_b_breaker.json. On restart the breaker is not tripped, the cumulative counter is zero, AND remote_counts is empty, so the empty-listing and shrink triggers have no `last` to compare against; the next pass against the still-bad remote runs with only rclone's per-pass cap until 50 more files go. The comment's "re-derived from the next pass's evidence" does not hold for trigger 1. Same shape for sync_halt.json: a torn write releases a fleet halt on one machine.

**Recommendation.** Write to `<path>.tmp` and `os.replace` (atomic on NTFS and APFS); it is a three-line change with no behavioural cost, and it is the direction every other latch comment in the file argues for.


## Lens: Security and trust model end to end (dashboard auth/sessions/OIDC, per-editor and identity tokens, 8899 loopback allow-list, signed upgrade channel, secrets handling, client share links via Funnel, SSH/TLS peer verification, AI provider keys)

The trust model is unusually well thought through for a product this young: the upgrade channel is anchored on an offline Ed25519 key that signs the whole package record (not just a hash), with a monotonic downgrade floor, same-origin pinning and a transport check (companion/upgrade.py, release_pubkey.py, dashboard/release_trust.py); browser sessions became server-side revocable rows with a session-bound CSRF token (auth.py, sessions.py); per-editor fleet tokens are hashed at rest and bound to an identity (db.py 984-1100, api.py 4163-4250); the 8899 loopback applies Host + exact-Origin + on-disk token + JSON-only rules (loopback_guard.py, broll_server.py 1150-1230); the public client-folder viewer is a token-in-path read-only surface with an explicit column allow-list and one uniform 404; and the operator's NAS SSH hop refuses an unpinned host key. The docs (SECRETS.md, LOOPBACK_API.md, CLIENT_FOLDERS.md, CONFIG.md) are honest about what is and is not closed.

The weak links are all at the seams between those strong parts. The companion's machine identity is still a stateless 30-day HMAC bearer: nothing can revoke it before expiry (disable, delete, NAS password change, per-editor token revocation all leave it working, and /api/v1/verify keeps handing every authenticated editor the shared fleet token), yet when it does expire the companion stops syncing until a human clicks the tray - wrong in both directions. Disabling a local account revokes nothing at all. The default DASH_TRUSTED_PROXIES=loopback does not match how Tailscale Serve actually reaches the container on the shipped TrueNAS layout (bridge gateway), so every editor shares one IP throttle bucket and five bad passwords from anyone lock the whole fleet's login and companion sign-in. The editors' own SFTP lanes - the path that moves every byte of footage - do no host-key verification at all, while the operator scripts refuse an unpinned key; a compose comment even claims the opposite. OIDC sign-in skips the fleet-membership check that password sign-in enforces. And one secret (DASH_SESSION_SECRET) underwrites cookies, identity tokens, CSRF, the OIDC flow cookie, session ids and the mounted b-roll app's fleet routes, so rotating it is a fleet-wide outage the docs describe only as "every session is invalidated".

**Strengths (do not break):**

- Upgrade channel: offline Ed25519 key signs the whole record (kind/platform/version/filename/sha256/size/min_version/published_at/signed_binary), the dashboard verifies on publish but cannot sign, the companion re-verifies before any byte hits disk, origin-pins the URL, refuses plain http to public hosts, and keeps a monotonic downgrade floor (companion/upgrade.py:244-320, 640-760; release_pubkey.py; release_trust.py). Do not weaken the 'record not hash' rule or the two-copies-pinned-by-test arrangement.
- Sessions: server-side rows keyed by HMAC(secret, cookie), revocable per session/user, idle + absolute lifetimes, per-login nonce, CSRF token derived from the session id with Origin/Referer cross-check, logout revokes before deleting the cookie (auth.py:437-535, sessions.py, app.py:494-517).
- Fleet credentials: cce1 tokens stored as sha256, shown once, shape-discriminated before any DB work, bound to an editor at /report and on selection routes; shared-token users are named at boot so the migration is observable (db.py:984-1100, api.py:109-153, 4163-4250).
- Loopback guard: Host check against DNS rebinding, exact-match Origin allow-list with no CORS headers on refusal, on-disk 0600/icacls token for non-browser callers, JSON-only POST, octet-stream only with a custom header on the one PUT, share/path containment, bundle-never-opened rule (loopback_guard.py, broll_server.py:1150-1230).
- Client share links: 128-bit token is the only credential, every request re-checks liveness and clip membership, PUBLIC_VIDEO_COLUMNS allow-list, one 404 for revoked/expired/unknown, noindex + no-referrer, a separate DB so publish_db cannot take the links, and Funnel scoped to one path on its own port (routes_share.py, client_folders.py, CLIENT_FOLDERS.md S3-S4).
- Boot-time refusal of weak secrets (>= 24 chars, no placeholder, entropy floor) for DASH_SESSION_SECRET, DASH_REPORT_TOKEN and BROLL_INGEST_TOKEN, with DASH_DEV_INSECURE=1 as the single documented bypass (auth.py:548-600, broll.py:108-126).
- OIDC implementation details: PKCE S256 always, state + nonce in an HMAC-signed 10-minute cookie, RS/ES algorithm allow-list, iss/aud/exp/nonce verified, https-only issuer except loopback, break-glass password login limited to DASH_ADMIN_USERS (oidc.py, auth.check_oidc_settings, ui.py:301).
- Operator secrets: DPAPI-at-rest + session-scoped load instead of setx, sudo password over stdin never argv, fleet token passed to the bootstrap via environment and cleared, identity.json/config.toml hardened with icacls on Windows (docs/SECRETS.md, server/common.py, installer/windows_bootstrap.ps1:211-214, companion/secretfile.py).
- SSH host-key pinning is the rule for every operator script: pin from site.toml/env, else the recorded key, else refuse unless --trust-host-key-on-first-use is given once; a changed key is always a refusal (server/common.py:915-1070).
- AI provider keys: files under <data>/secrets/ai via the same 0600 writer as the boot secrets, env always wins, masked in every response, never in /api/v1/site or logs; CLI adapters dark unless a site flag is on and run with the API keys stripped from their environment (ai_providers.py:204-320, 473-489).
- Defensive request handling that protects auth itself: credential check before body buffering on /report, SMB probe concurrency cap so an unauthenticated /login cannot starve the worker pool, guest/null SMB sessions rejected, no-redirect openers on every credentialed client, generic error bodies (app.py:519-547, auth.py:63-115).


### trust-model-1: Companion identity tokens are unrevocable 30-day bearers with no refresh: too long to revoke, too short to live with

**HIGH, gap, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/auth.py:58 IDENTITY_TTL_SECONDS = 30 days; auth.py:232-284 make_identity_token/_read_token are pure HMAC over (user, expiry) with no server-side row (sessions got rows on 2026-08-17, identity tokens did not); api.py:1339-1385 /api/v1/verify returns a fresh token AND `report_token: settings.report_token` (the shared fleet token) to any editor with a valid NAS password; api.py:4209-4232 /report accepts shared token + any valid identity for that editor; api.py:2393-2420 _purge_user_credentials revokes sessions and cce1 tokens but cannot touch identity tokens; settings.py:62 shared_report_token_enabled defaults True; companion/src/ccsync_companion/app.py:5719-5752 _identity_watch_loop stops all sync lanes the moment the token expires and waits for a tray click; identity.py has no refresh path (grep 'refresh' -> none). KNOWN_BUGS CR-18 claims tokens are 'revocable one person at a time'.

**Consequence.** An editor who leaves (NAS password changed, local account deleted/disabled, cce1 token revoked) keeps a working fleet credential for up to 30 days: their companion still posts reports, overwrites machine_state/presence rows, reads its selection and downloads packages, because the shared token it was handed by /verify is still accepted and the identity HMAC still verifies. Conversely every editor in every fleet has their sync silently STOP once a month (memory note: 'EULA gate parks self-upgraded editors' is the same shape) until someone right-clicks the tray - on an unattended render rig that is a lost weekend.

**Recommendation.** Give identity tokens the same treatment sessions got: a server-side row keyed by HMAC(secret, token) created at /verify, validated on every companion call, revoked by disable/delete/admin button and by NAS password change where detectable; have /report return a refreshed token (sliding expiry) so a healthy companion never expires; stop returning `report_token` from /verify once a per-editor token exists for that user, and put a date on retiring DASH_SHARED_REPORT_TOKEN_ENABLED.


### trust-model-2: Disabling a local account revokes nothing: sessions, report tokens and identity keep working

**HIGH, gap, effort small.**

**Evidence.** dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:2374-2392 api_admin_disable_user only flips users.disabled; compare api_admin_delete_user 2421-2453 which calls _purge_user_credentials. sessions.py:184-215 SessionStore.validate reads only auth_sessions (never users.disabled), so auth._resolve_session (auth.py:450-475) keeps answering the username. local_users.py:229-240 verify_password and 173-178 is_local_admin do check disabled, but only on a NEW login.

**Consequence.** DASH_AUTH_METHOD=local appliance: admin disables a departed editor on Settings > Users; that person's open browser tab keeps a 7-day session (12h idle, refreshed by the SPA's own polling) and their companion keeps reporting; a disabled ADMIN keeps admin rights for the rest of their session because is_admin is only consulted via is_local_admin for non-DASH_ADMIN_USERS names, and that check happens per request on the users row - wait, it does check disabled per request, but the session itself is still accepted, so the disabled admin becomes a live non-admin editor rather than a logged-out one, and a disabled editor stays fully live.

**Recommendation.** Route disable through the same _purge_user_credentials as delete (sessions + cce1 tokens; identity once trust-model-1 lands), or have _resolve_session consult users.disabled in local mode. Add a test that a disabled user's existing cookie 401s on the next request.


### trust-model-3: Default trusted-proxy list does not match the shipped TrueNAS network shape, so the whole fleet shares one login-throttle IP bucket

**HIGH, fragility, effort small.**

**Evidence.** settings.py:89/541 trusted_proxies defaults to '127.0.0.1,::1'; auth.py:176-186 client_ip returns the raw peer unless the peer is trusted; sessions.py:59-64 LOGIN_FAILURE_LIMIT=5 per (user) AND per (ip), 308-340 throttled() blocks on whichever bucket is exhausted; docs/CONFIG.md:241 itself says 'A proxy on the docker host arrives from the bridge gateway (e.g. 172.17.0.1)'; server/install_dashboard_app.py never emits DASH_TRUSTED_PROXIES (grep: no hits) and publishes the port via compose `ports:` (1589-1602), so Tailscale Serve's connections from the host are SNATed to the bridge gateway; sessions.summarize_client (sessions.py:106-113) records that same IP on every session row.

**Consequence.** On every TrueNAS deployment browsed through Tailscale Serve (the decided-only publish path), every request's peer is 172.17.0.1. Five wrong passwords by anyone - one editor with caps lock on, a companion retrying /api/v1/verify with a stale password, or anyone on the tailnet - puts the fleet-wide IP bucket into exponential backoff (60s doubling to 1h): /login AND /api/v1/verify answer 429 for every editor and every companion sign-in. The admin sessions page also shows the same IP for everyone, so 'revoke the session from the unknown address' is impossible. Secure-flag auto-detection is also off behind Serve for the same reason (mitigated by DASH_COOKIE_SECURE=1 guidance).

**Recommendation.** Have install_dashboard_app.py (and the appliance compose) set DASH_TRUSTED_PROXIES to the docker bridge CIDR plus the tailnet node address it actually proxies from, and log at boot when X-Forwarded-For arrives from an untrusted peer on every request (the signature of this misconfiguration). Consider exempting the IP bucket when the peer is a known shared gateway, and make the IP budget larger than the per-user one.


### trust-model-4: Editor SFTP lanes verify no NAS host key while the operator scripts refuse an unpinned one; a compose comment claims the opposite

**MEDIUM, inconsistency, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** installer/windows_bootstrap.ps1:1856-1864 and installer/macos_bootstrap.sh:2068-2076 write the rclone stanza with host/user/port/key_file/shell_type and no `known_hosts_file` (rclone's sftp backend accepts any host key unless that option is set); companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/rclone_lane.py adds no --sftp-known-hosts-file flag (grep known_hosts in sync/ -> none); api.py:952-1010 /api/v1/site publishes no ssh_hostkey even though site.toml [nas] ssh_hostkey exists and server/common.py:921-940 pins it for the base rig; dashboard/deploy/compose.appliance.yaml ~277-281 says 'a changed host key makes every editor's rclone refuse the NAS on its next connection', which is not what happens.

**Consequence.** Lanes A/B (every original upload and every proxy download) authenticate the editor TO the NAS (ed25519 key) but never the NAS to the editor. Inside the tailnet WireGuard already authenticates the peer, which is why this has not bitten; but a LAN-addressed `sftp_host` (a site that sets [net] to the LAN IP, or a laptop on the studio Wi-Fi) or a compromised tailnet node impersonating the NAS address receives uploads and can feed arbitrary files into lane B's download scope - the exact 'NAS stops looking like the tree' shape lane_guard exists to park. The appliance doc promising a refusal on key change will mislead whoever debugs a real key rotation.

**Recommendation.** Publish the sftp host key(s) in /api/v1/site (they are public keys, same class as the Syncthing device id), have both bootstraps write a ~/.ccsync/known_hosts and `known_hosts_file = ...` into the stanza, and have the companion refuse lane start when the manifest key and the recorded key disagree (surface it as a tray line, not an rclone error). Fix the compose comment until then.


### trust-model-5: OIDC sign-in skips the fleet-membership check that password sign-in enforces, and maps the IdP username claim straight onto an editor identity

**MEDIUM, gap, effort small.**

**Evidence.** dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/oidc.py:330-383 oidc_callback -> username_from_claims (258-272) -> auth.start_session with no membership, editors-group or known_editors check; compare api.py:1294-1337 _require_fleet_member on /verify, and ui.py:301 which restricts only the break-glass password path. oidc.py:1-17 docstring acknowledges 'there is no way for the code to check it'.

**Consequence.** A customer points DASH_OIDC_ISSUER at their company IdP: every account in the directory (accounting, interns, a contractor tenant) can sign in to the dashboard and lands as an editor whose username is whatever preferred_username says; if that string equals an existing NAS editor's name (jsmith) they inherit that editor's selections, project ticks, Syncthing device approvals and client-folder panel. Nothing in the fleet distinguishes the two.

**Recommendation.** Require the resolved username to be a known fleet member (known_editors / editors group / local users table, same predicate as /verify) or an explicit DASH_OIDC_ALLOWED_GROUP claim before start_session; log and 403 otherwise. Optionally persist the IdP `sub` per username and refuse a second sub claiming the same username.


### trust-model-6: One secret (DASH_SESSION_SECRET) underwrites six unrelated trust relationships, so rotating it is a fleet-wide outage the docs understate

**MEDIUM, fragility, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** auth.py:215-243 _sign/_make_token (session cookies AND identity tokens), auth.py:437-444 session_id_for, 525-535 csrf_token, oidc.py:92-100 pack_flow (flow cookie), docs/CONFIG.md:209 'Also read by the MOUNTED b-roll app (broll/web config.get_session_secret), which verifies the same identity tokens on its ingest fleet routes'; docs/SECRETS.md table: rotate by 're-deploy; every session is invalidated' (no mention of companions); companion app.py:5719-5752 stops sync on identity invalidation.

**Consequence.** A suspected leak of the session secret (the value sits in plaintext compose env per SECRETS.md 'Still owed') cannot be rotated without simultaneously signing out every browser, invalidating every companion's identity (every machine stops syncing until a tray click - trust-model-1), killing in-flight OIDC logins and breaking the mounted b-roll app's ingest routes if its copy lags. Operators will therefore not rotate it, which is the worst outcome for the one secret whose compromise is 'a forgeable admin cookie'.

**Recommendation.** Derive per-purpose keys with HKDF(secret, purpose) so the wire formats stay but purposes are separable; support DASH_SESSION_SECRET_PREVIOUS for a verify-old/sign-new overlap window; once identity tokens are server-side rows (trust-model-1) they no longer depend on the secret at all. Document the real blast radius in SECRETS.md until then.


### trust-model-7: AI CLI SET UP wizard installs Codex with no publisher checksum while docs call the fetch checksum-verified

**LOW, inconsistency, effort small.**

**Evidence.** dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/cli_tools.py:797-832: when the release has no codex-package_SHA256SUMS asset, `checksum_source = 'downloaded_bytes'`, _download runs with expected_sha='' and the install completes with `checksum_verified: False` (line 847) and only a detail string; CLAUDE.md 'AI provider' bullet and cli_tools.py:18 describe the fetch as 'verified against the PUBLISHER'S OWN checksum'. For Claude Code the checksum manifest and the binary come from the same origin (CLAUDE_BASE, line 159), so TLS to that host is the real anchor either way.

**Consequence.** An admin clicking SET UP gets a binary that the dashboard will then execute with fleet ytdl prompts, with nothing but GitHub TLS behind it, while every doc and the page's own wording imply a second check. Not exploitable on its own; it is the 'strong word, weak guarantee' pattern this lens is about.

**Recommendation.** Refuse the Codex install when no checksum is published (the page already has the 'type its path' fallback), or move the claim in CLAUDE.md/cli_tools docstring to 'TLS from the publisher, plus the publisher's checksum where one exists'. Consider pinning an allow-list of expected sha256 per version in the vendor feed so the dashboard, not the publisher, is the anchor.


### trust-model-8: Dashboard-to-TrueNAS REST disables TLS verification while the SSH hop pins host keys

**LOW, inconsistency, effort small.**

**Evidence.** dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/nas/truenas.py:10 ('self-signed cert so verify=False') and :123 `verify=self.verify_ssl`; the credential on that hop is DASH_NAS_API_KEY or the admin password (settings.py:145-164), sent as HTTP Basic; server/common.py:921-940 documents 'PINNING IS THE RULE' for the same NAS over SSH.

**Consequence.** The container's NAS admin credential is offered to whoever answers the NAS address on :443. On the shipped layout the container runs on the NAS itself, so the hop never leaves the box; on a split deployment (dashboard container on one host, NAS on another, which the Synology/appliance work explicitly contemplates) an on-path party on the LAN gets full pool control.

**Recommendation.** Pin the NAS certificate fingerprint the way ssh_hostkey is pinned (a `[nas] tls_fingerprint` site value, verified by a custom requests adapter), or at least fail closed when the NAS host is not loopback/the container's own host and no pin is set.


### trust-model-9: The loopback allow-list makes any XSS in the dashboard-hosted SPAs equal to code execution on every editor's desk, and keeps the plain-http origin trusted forever

**LOW, fragility, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** companion/src/ccsync_companion/loopback_guard.py:207-232 origins_for_url returns BOTH http:// and https:// forms of dashboard_url unconditionally; broll_server.py:1185-1200 _post_authorised accepts any allowed Origin with no further proof; routes reachable: /insert, /music/reveal, /ytdl/reveal (spawns Explorer/open), /broll/ingest/run, on-demand rclone fetches. docs/LOOPBACK_API.md 'What this does not fix' names the MITM case but not the XSS-amplification case; no CSP header is set on the broll/music/ytdl SPAs (grep Content-Security-Policy in dashboard/src, broll/web, music/web -> none).

**Consequence.** The dashboard serves three third-party-data-rendering SPAs (clip transcripts, YouTube titles, music tags, client-folder captions typed by any editor) from the one origin the companion trusts. A single stored XSS there is not 'a dashboard bug' - it is a POST to 127.0.0.1:8899 on every editor who opens the page: files revealed, clips inserted, rclone fetches started. Separately, a site that has moved to https keeps accepting an http origin that no browser will ever legitimately present again.

**Recommendation.** Add a strict CSP (no inline scripts, self-only connect-src plus 127.0.0.1:8899) to the dashboard and the mounted SPAs, and have each suite's scan test assert it; once site.dashboard_url is https, drop the http twin (or require a second signal such as the loopback token for the spawner routes /reveal and /ingest/run).


## Lens: Data model and identity consistency: editor vs hostname vs machine_id vs Syncthing device id, per-machine selections and the unassigned bucket, site-manifest keys, tree-layout assumptions

The fleet model has been moved, deliberately and mostly well, from "the person is the unit" to "the computer is the unit": `machines` (v23) carries a companion-minted `machine_id` and the Syncthing device id as attributes of a hostname-keyed row, `selections`/`editor_prefs` are keyed `(editor, machine, slug)` (v24), the unassigned bucket `machine=''` is resolved in exactly one Python function (`db.selections_for_machine`, E:\Projects\resolve-remote-sync\dashboard\src\ccsync_dashboard\db.py:1957) and one SQL spelling (`fetch_sync_backlog`, db.py:3195), and every back-compat direction (union for a companion that cannot name itself, person-wide removal, B16 "unmapped devices are never touched", the share-removal brake) errs toward under-sharing. That is the right shape and it is tested (`dashboard/tests/test_multi_machine.py`). What has NOT moved with it is the ROLE. `base`/`editor` still exists in four places with three owners: `config.toml mode` (machine, hand-edited), `identity.role` minted from `DASH_ADMIN_USERS` (person, api.py:1379), `machine_state.mode` (machine, reported) and `editor_media_project.mode`; the dashboard collapses them back to a person-level predicate (`db.base_only_editors`, db.py:1175) that gates the tick, the copy-plan, the sidebar, the assignments grid and two of the three queue sources, while the third (`fetch_sync_backlog`) is already per machine. The moment one account owns a wired machine and a remote one, which is the owner's stated target for the next customer (docs/MULTI_BASE_RIG_PLAN.md §0), the per-machine plan and the per-person role contradict each other and CR-28's permanent GETTING READY chip comes back one machine at a time.

The second family of problems is "same concept, two authorities". A Syncthing device is tied to an editor both by its label (the `name == username` convention, `resolve_editor_username`) and by the authenticated report (`machines.syncthing_device_id`); the enforce cycle adds shares through the second and removes them only through the first. One device id may legitimately sit on two registry rows (no unique index, COALESCE-only upserts, and the CR-42 refusal path produces exactly that), and three joins assume it sits on one. The tray's "Remove this project from this machine" is machine-scoped in its label and person-scoped on the wire. The ytdl lease is keyed on the editor name and explicitly treats a second claim by the same name as a refresh. And the site manifest's `canonical_prefix`/`tree_name` are live-editable on the dashboard but install-time constants in every companion and in `musicweb`. None of these is a line-level bug; each is a place where a second customer with two computers per person, or a non-`P:` drive, will exercise a path the first customer never did. The tree-layout half of the lens is already audited honestly in docs/TREE_LAYOUT_AGNOSTICISM.md (the `Projects` literal, the `Proxy/` predicate, the unread `template_folders`/`shared_asset_folders` manifest keys); I found nothing to add there beyond confirming that `projects_dir` is still half-wired (server/common.py:197 vs settings.py:363) and that the companion still pulls only three manifest keys into its config (config.py:1322).

**Strengths (do not break):**

- The unassigned bucket (`machine=''`) has exactly one resolution rule, written once in Python (`db.selections_for_machine`, db.py:1957) and mirrored once in SQL (`fetch_sync_backlog`, db.py:3195), with a test that pins 'applies only where there is no plan'. Keep that single-spelling discipline.
- Every back-compat direction errs toward under-sharing: a companion that cannot name itself gets the UNION of the person's plans (api.py:1441-1451), a removal with no machine goes wide, unmapped devices are never touched (B16), and the share-removal brake counts (folder, device) pairs (collector.py:903-921). These are the reasons a bad migration has not yet unshared a fleet; do not 'simplify' them away.
- `machine_id` is an ATTRIBUTE, not a credential or a hardware fingerprint (machine.py docstring), minted once, never derived, and only ever used to answer 'is this the computer I already know' for an ALREADY-authenticated editor; rename adoption refuses to destroy a plan (CR-42). The threat-model reasoning in that docstring is exactly right.
- Per-editor `cce1.` report tokens BIND to an identity and the report handler refuses an editor_name that disagrees with either the token or the signed identity header (api.py:4183-4231); `known_editors` is evidence-based and append-only. The username is therefore a verified identity at the one boundary that matters.
- A project's identity is the marker slug travelling with the directory, not `slugify(rel)` (provision.py:85-103), so a moved project is a retarget and not a delete-plus-create, and two clients' `<project>` leaves cannot collide because the slug is the whole rel path.
- The site manifest is published from one store with one validator and one documented key set (`site_store.KEYS`), served unauthenticated with no secrets, cached on the client for offline start, and the feature block is a fail-closed whitelist on BOTH ends (`FEATURE_KEYS` in site_store and companion site.py:120, CR-40). Adding a flag requires touching both lists on purpose.
- `canon.py` / `paths.py` are genuinely prefix-agnostic (a Windows prefix on a Mac, a POSIX prefix, and the base-rig identity case are all handled), so the drive letter really is site data for every Resolve write even though a few user-visible literals remain.
- docs/TREE_LAYOUT_AGNOSTICISM.md and MULTI_BASE_RIG_PLAN.md are honest about what is and is not per-machine or per-site, including the half-migrated role model; the code comments cite the bug ids and dates. That honesty is what made this review possible to verify rather than guess.


### data-model-1: Wired/remote is per machine in the data but per person in every gate: a mixed-role account re-creates CR-28 and shares folders with a wired machine

**HIGH, inconsistency, effort small.**

**Evidence.** db.py:1175 `base_only_editors` returns usernames whose EVERY machine is base (the per-machine map `machine_modes` at db.py:1153 exists but is only consumed through this rollup). Consumers: api.py:423 (queue, lane C block `who in base_editors`), api.py:520-523 (GETTING READY block, `r['editor'] in base_editors`), api.py:1573 (tick 409), api.py:2767 (copy-plan 409), assignments.py:96, ui.py:769/859. Meanwhile `add_selection_for_person` (db.py:1916-1934) fans a tick out to `machines_of(editor)` with no mode filter, and `fetch_sync_backlog` (db.py:3199) already excludes `emp.mode != 'base'` PER MACHINE. The companion now reports `base` when config.toml says so (app.py:3037 `effective_mode`, MULTI_BASE_RIG_PLAN WP0 done), so a mixed account is reachable today. MULTI_BASE_RIG_PLAN.md §1.7 calls the model 'half-migrated' and nothing is built for it.

**Consequence.** Billy: office desktop with `mode="base"` (reports base) + remote laptop (editor). He is not base-only, so the sidebar tick is allowed and writes a row for BOTH machines. The desktop row can never gain a completion row, so the transfers page shows `billy · <project> [ GETTING READY ]` for ever (the exact CR-28 symptom); if the desktop reported a Syncthing device id, `_run_enforce` (collector.py:877-880) shares the project folder with the wired machine's device, whose local_root IS the NAS share. The queue's lane C block and the pending block disagree with the backlog about the same machine.

**Recommendation.** Make `machine_modes` the predicate everywhere: `add_selection_for_person` skips machines whose mode is base, the two queue blocks test `(editor, machine)` not `editor`, the tick endpoint 409s only when the named machine (or every target machine) is base, and the assignments grid greys the COLUMN not the account. Keep `base_only_editors` only for the no-`?machine=` legacy path. ~30 lines plus a mirror of the CR-28 tests with one base and one editor machine under a single user.


### data-model-2: The machine's role is still minted from the PERSON (DASH_ADMIN_USERS) and obeyed by the companion and the wizard

**HIGH, gap, effort large.**

**Evidence.** api.py:1379 `"role": "base" if auth.is_admin(settings, username) else "editor"`; companion app.py:2967 `_apply_identity_role` (role may disable sync; a non-admin owner of a wired machine gets `editor` and must hand-edit config.toml, installer/windows_upgrade.ps1:296-306); onboarding/steps.py:1146 `effective_install_role`: 'the verified role wins whenever the dashboard sent a recognised one'. `auth.is_admin` (auth.py:714) also means dashboard permissions, so the two concepts are one boolean. docs/MULTI_BASE_RIG_PLAN.md §1.3-1.6 and §2 document this; WP1-WP5 (detection, per-machine toggle, precedence) are unbuilt.

**Consequence.** (a) An admin who onboards a remote laptop is told role=base at sign-in and the laptop syncs nothing, silently, for ever (plan §2, 'not yet observed only because no admin has onboarded a second machine'). (b) A non-admin office editor who picks 'physically connected' in the wizard gets an EDITOR install that tears down the real NAS `P:` mapping and replaces it with a loopback share of a local folder (the B20 shape). (c) Nothing supported can put a machine into base mode: the second customer's office of wired machines needs a hand-edited TOML per machine.

**Recommendation.** Treat this as the next structural work item, not a polish: `machine_state.mode` (already per machine, already reported) becomes the authority the dashboard hands back in the report reply/verify for THAT machine; `/verify` stops deriving role from `is_admin` (send `role` only as a hint, or per `machine_id`); the wizard asks the machine question and only refuses the dangerous direction (editor install onto a path that resolves to the NAS share). Ship the plan's R3/R5 refusal (never run a deleting lane B onto a tree that is the NAS) as the one mechanical guard.


### data-model-3: "Remove this project from this machine" is machine-scoped in the tray and person-scoped on the wire

**MEDIUM, inconsistency, effort small.**

**Evidence.** companion selection.py:341-346: `untick` deliberately sends DELETE /api/v1/selection/{editor}/{slug} with NO `?machine=`; api.py:1616-1627 maps that to `db.remove_selection(machine=None)` which DELETEs every row for the person including other machines' OWN rows (db.py:1936-1955). The justification in the comment only holds for bucket rows ('if it is in the unassigned bucket'). Pinned by tests/test_multi_machine.py:214 `test_an_untick_with_no_machine_removes_it_everywhere`. app.py:3407 then removes the local Syncthing folder and deletes the local copy on the machine that asked.

**Consequence.** Ruskin has the project ticked on his desktop (own row) and on his laptop (own row). On the laptop he picks 'Remove this project from this machine' to free disk. The desktop's row is deleted too: on the next enforce cycle the desktop's device is unshared from the folder, its sequencer drops the project, and nobody is told. The desktop keeps its files but stops receiving proxies and stops uploading originals for that project until someone notices the tick is gone.

**Recommendation.** Make the companion's untick name its machine (`?machine=`), and make the machine-scoped DELETE correct for the bucket case by MATERIALISING: before deleting, for every sibling machine that currently has no own rows, copy the bucket rows into own rows, then delete the bucket rows and this machine's row. Person-wide deletion stays only for a DELETE with no machine from a companion that cannot name itself (detect by absence of machine_id in the registry).


### data-model-4: Two authorities bind a Syncthing device to an editor (its label vs the registry), and the enforce cycle adds through one and removes through the other

**MEDIUM, fragility, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** Label path: `devices.editor_username` = `resolve_editor_username(name, known_editors)` (db.py:924, db.py:1287 upsert_device); admin approve sets name=username (api.py:2791-2800). Registry path: `machines.syncthing_device_id` from the authenticated report (api.py:3516-3546).
