In `_run_enforce`: additions come from `machine_devices[(editor, machine)]` regardless of label (collector.py:877-880); but a device whose label does not resolve is kept in `desired` unconditionally (collector.py:866 `desired |= {d ... id_to_editor[d] is None}`) so it is never removed. The lane C queue (api.py:436-449) joins `d.editor_username` (label) to `m.machine` (registry) and silently `continue`s when the pair is not in the plan.

**Consequence.** A device approved directly in the Syncthing GUI (label = hostname, e.g. `DESKTOP-LQQ41TC`) is logged once as UNMAPPED, yet the registry knows its device id from the companion's report: every tick adds a share to it and no untick ever removes one, so the unshare half of the plan is dead for that machine. Conversely a label that resolves to a different username than the reporting account (admin typo at approve time) makes the queue drop that machine's lane C backlog and attributes its history (collector.py:1196) to the wrong person.

**Recommendation.** Declare the registry the single authority: device -> `machines` row -> editor; a device the registry knows is MAPPED whatever its label says, and the label convention survives only as the fallback for devices no companion has ever reported. Pre-fill the pending-device approval from the reported `syncthing_device_id` (the plan's §3.1 payoff) so the label stops being typed by hand. One collector change plus the two queue joins.


### data-model-5: One Syncthing device id can sit on two registry rows, and three joins assume it sits on one

**MEDIUM, fragility, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** db.py:661-673: `machines` PK is (editor, machine); `syncthing_device_id` has a plain index, no uniqueness. db.py:1678-1690 `upsert_machine` COALESCEs attributes and never clears another row's. The CR-42 refusal branch (db.py:1847-1895, api.py:3519-3540) records the report under the new name and LEAVES the old row with its plan and its device id, so the two rows now share one id. Readers assuming uniqueness: `machine_by_device_id` (db.py:1697, `fetchone`), `machine_devices` in `_run_enforce` (collector.py:854-858, both rows map to the same device), `lane_c_q` LEFT JOIN (api.py:436) and the GETTING READY completion probe (api.py:525-531). The same multiplication happens when a Mac's Bonjour hostname churns (`platform.node()` gains a '-2' suffix on a name clash) since the plan is keyed on the hostname (machine.py:4-9) and stale `machine_state` rows are evicted only past 20 (db.py:583).

**Consequence.** After a refused rename (or a restored image carrying another box's machine.json), the live machine's device is handed the UNION of both rows' plans by enforce, while its own `GET /selection?machine=<new>` returns only its own rows: Syncthing offers it folders the companion never configures (the 'folder not configured' shape from memory), the lane C queue shows the same need twice under two hostnames, and the stale hostname shows a permanent GETTING READY chip because nothing ever reports under it again.

**Recommendation.** Enforce one-row-per-device: in `_register_machine`, when the reported device id is already on another row of the same editor, NULL it there (the report is the fresher evidence) and log; add a partial unique index on `syncthing_device_id`. Longer term, finish the plan's original §3.1: key `selections`/`editor_prefs`/`machine_state` on `machine_id` with the hostname as a label, as the 'separate, later, mechanical change' WP1 promised.


### data-model-7: The ytdl download lease is keyed on the editor NAME, so one account's two machines both hold it

**MEDIUM, gap, effort small.**

**Evidence.** ytdl/web/ytdlweb/db.py:676-700 `claim_download`: the compare-and-set treats 'leased to THIS editor' as a refresh ('the companion re-announcing itself after a restart is not a second holder'); `is_leaseholder` (db.py:657) compares `claimed_by` to the name only. routes_fleet.py:261-262 promises `409 somebody else holds a live lease -- ... one editor on two machines`, which the WHERE clause cannot deliver. No machine identifier is in the claim even though every companion already mints and reports one (machine.py, reporter.py:378). MULTI_BASE_RIG_PLAN §1.10 names it and defers to 'decision 4', which is about proxy/index producers, not this path.

**Consequence.** Billy requests a 30-clip job from his laptop; both his laptop and his home PC run companions signed in as `billy`. Both poll, both claim (the second claim is a 'refresh'), both download and both upload via lane A into the same `Youtube/<term>/` folder: doubled bandwidth, two writers of the same filenames, and whichever finishes last wins the sidecar.

**Recommendation.** Lease holder = `(editor, machine_id)`; the claim body carries `machine_id`, the refresh rule requires the same id, and a different id from the same editor gets the documented 409. The routes already verify the editor identity; the machine id is self-asserted but only needs to discriminate, not authorise.


### data-model-8: `canonical_prefix` / `tree_name` are live-editable site values on the dashboard but install-time constants in every companion and in the music service

**MEDIUM, fragility, effort small.**

**Evidence.** setup_routes.py:206/238 `site_store.set_many(payload.values)` writes any manifest key; `_validate_canonical_prefix` (site_store.py:127-140) checks shape only, with no 'machines already installed' guard. On the client the wizard writes the value into config.toml once (onboarding/steps.py:2206-2213; windows_bootstrap.ps1:513-519) and `_SITE_CONFIG_KEYS` (companion config.py:1322-1337) refreshes only `smb_unc`, `sftp_chunk_size`, `sftp_concurrency` from the cached manifest; `paths.py:288` then classifies anything not under the configured prefix as OUT_OF_TREE. Independent defaults persist: companion config.py:125/690 `P:\`, site_store.py:358 `or "P:\\"`, music/web/musicweb/config.py:39 (`CCSYNC_CANONICAL_PREFIX`, which server/install_dashboard_app.py never emits), drive_swap.py:43 `P_DRIVE` and installer/macos_uninstall.sh:90 (already listed in TREE_LAYOUT_AGNOSTICISM.md §6).

**Consequence.** A second customer starts on `P:`, installs three editors, then an admin changes the site's prefix to `Q:` on the setup page because `P:` clashes with something in their office. New installs map `Q:`, the three existing companions keep `P:` silently, the manifest and every new Resolve path say `Q:\Projects\...`, and the old machines' popup flags every new clip as outside the tree. Nothing on the dashboard or the tray says the fleet is split.

**Recommendation.** Make `canonical_prefix` and `tree_name` write-once after the first machine registers (refuse in `set_many` with a message naming the machines, or require an explicit 'I will reinstall N machines' flag), and have the companion compare its config to the cached manifest on each refresh and surface a tray line when they differ. Feed `CCSYNC_CANONICAL_PREFIX` from `DASH_SITE_CANONICAL_PREFIX` in the deploy so the music service stops carrying its own default.


### data-model-6: The per-machine `editor_prefs` migration (v24 WP7) re-keyed a table that nothing reads or writes

**LOW, complexity, effort small.**

**Evidence.** db.py:728-749 (SCHEMA_V24 rebuilds `editor_prefs` with a machine column), db.py:2074-2117 (`set_project_root_override` / `get_project_root_override`). A grep of dashboard/src (py, html, js) and companion/src finds no caller of either function and no other reference to `editor_prefs` or `project_root_override` outside db.py; the report reply's destination root comes from `machine_state.detected_project_root` + `project_roots` (api.py:1228-1234). `known_editor_usernames` (db.py:1192) still treats a row there as evidence of an editor.

**Consequence.** A PK-changing migration and a two-level fallback rule (machine -> bucket -> any) exist for a feature with no UI, no API and no consumer; the next person to touch it will reasonably assume it is live and either wire a second store beside `project_roots` or 'fix' the fallback.

**Recommendation.** Decide: either wire it (an admin cell per machine that the report reply honours) or drop the table in a v27 migration and remove the three functions. If kept, add a test that fails when nothing outside db.py references it.


## Lens: Release, upgrade and deployment pipeline coherence (ship.cmd, release.ps1, CI workflows, GitHub Releases feed, package_store, OTA push, auto_update, dashboard self-update, versioning, rollback, Mac path)

The pipeline has strong primitives: one offline Ed25519 key signs every record, the dashboard's package_store is the single writer for both the human PUT and the feed, the companion verifies offers, keeps a monotonic downgrade floor and compares versions numerically (0.10 > 0.9 works), select_code_root.py gives the dashboard a verified, watchdog-protected code-root switch, and ship.ps1 front-loads its refusals. But the system has grown THREE distribution paths that do not reconcile: (1) ship.cmd builds locally and PUTs into the studio dashboard, (2) publish_latest.py/publish_feed.py sign CI builds into a GitHub-hosted channel that customer dashboards pull, and (3) the dashboard's own OTA bundle plus the `:1` container image. The local `feed/` directory (gitignored) is the only memory of what the feed contains; the live channel shows windows 0.9.3 missing while macos 0.9.3 is present, 0.9.4/0.9.42 absent, no `onboard` records at all, every min_version 0.0.0 and `dashboard_image` blank. ship.cmd still assumes bind-mount mode while the studio runs in image mode, so it restarts the production dashboard for nothing and cannot pass its own health gate once the repo's dashboard VERSION is bumped.

The highest-risk defect is that publish_feed.py never reads the published channel: it loads `feed/channel.json` from the local disk, upserts, re-signs and uploads with `--clobber`. A new rig, a wiped checkout or the Mac would publish a validly signed channel containing one record, erasing the macOS companion, every dashboard bundle and the CLAP model artefacts for every customer, and nothing downstream can tell because the signature is good. Second tier: the feed has no notion of "current" and no retraction, so `stage`/`current` policies replay the whole history in append order and a bad build cannot be withdrawn; the installer channel is unpublishable through the CI path; same-version-different-bytes is permitted on the feed but 409s on every dashboard that already holds the version; publish_latest trusts whatever CI run is newest regardless of branch. Most of these are half-applied-release shapes the repo's own docs warn about (OPS-4, R13) reappearing one layer up.

**Strengths (do not break):**

- One offline Ed25519 key signs the whole record (kind, platform, version, filename, sha256, size, min_version, published_at, signed_binary[, runtime_id]); dashboard/package_store.py is the single writer for both the human PUT and the feed, so 'published' means one thing; release_trust/release_pubkey parity is pinned by dashboard/tests/test_packages.py.
- The companion side is robust: upgrade.py verifies the offer against baked keys before it is even shown, keeps a monotonic downgrade floor on disk, compares versions as int tuples (0.10 > 0.9 is safe), refuses non-same-origin URLs and redirects, and auto_update never takes an older build while an admin push is explicit and retried (CR-41).
- select_code_root.py is a well-designed trust boundary: image python judges the volume tree, refuses unverifiable/foreign-runtime/older trees, and a boot-attempt watchdog reverts automatically; apply() stages, import-checks against DB copies, backs up and snapshots before swapping.
- ship.ps1 front-loads its refusals (secrets, dirty tree, already-published companion AND installer, Authenticode identity) and reads the drift doctor rather than just running it; publish_feed signs before uploading and refuses a channel whose URLs point away from the asset host.
- The feed client is paranoid in the right places: https-only, bounded redirects with no credentials, byte caps, channel signature plus per-record signatures, and a compromised host can at worst serve stale or nothing.
- Provenance travels with the artefact (ccsync-release.json sha-matched by every consumer), dirty/untested manifests are refused by both publish paths (OPS-1), and vendored-file byte parity is gated in release.ps1 and server tests.


### release-pipeline-1: publish_feed.py rebuilds the channel from the local, gitignored feed/ dir and clobbers the live one

**CRITICAL, fragility, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** tools/publish_feed.py:202-210 load_channel() reads only <feed-dir>/channel.json and returns an EMPTY package list when it is absent; :213-220 upsert_record; :486 `gh release upload ... --clobber`; nothing in the file fetches the published channel first. .gitignore:50-53 ignores feed/. tools/publish_latest.py:84-98 published_versions() likewise reads the local dir. Local feed today (feed/channel.json, generated 2026-08-20) carries 18 package records + 2 CLAP artefacts that exist nowhere else in the repo.

**Consequence.** Run publish_feed/publish_latest from any machine without this rig's feed/ directory (new base rig, Mac, fresh clone, a --feed-dir typo) and the upload replaces the live channel with a correctly signed document containing only the new record. Every customer dashboard then sees no macOS companion, no dashboard bundles and no music-clap-audio artefacts (RELEASE.md:114-120: 'a feed with no copy of the version the shipped companion pins means no editor can ingest music at all'). The signature verifies, so nothing logs an error; the loss is discovered by a customer.

**Recommendation.** Before upserting, download the published channel.json(+.sig) from the GitHub release, verify it against the baked pubkeys, and merge into it; refuse to upload a channel whose package/artefact set is a strict subset of the published one unless --allow-shrink is passed. Also stop treating feed/ as state: derive 'already published' from the fetched channel.


### release-pipeline-2: Two publish paths for one fleet (ship.cmd PUT vs CI+feed) with no reconciliation; the studio and the feed already disagree

**HIGH, inconsistency, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** tools/ship.ps1:497-498 publishes via build_editor_package.ps1 PUT and never touches publish_feed; docs/RELEASE.md:90-110 says a full release is four separate commands. Live evidence in feed/channel.json: companion macos 0.9.3 present, companion windows 0.9.3 ABSENT (shipped by ship.cmd PUT only); 0.9.4 and 0.9.42 on neither platform. dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/release_feed.py:525-527 409s 'already published' without comparing sha256, so a dashboard holding a locally built 0.9.41 keeps those bytes even if the feed's 0.9.41 is a different CI build.

**Consequence.** The studio fleet runs builds feed customers can never receive, and one version number can denote two different signed binaries (local vs CI) across dashboards. check_deploy_drift and the Packages page compare versions only, so the divergence is invisible.

**Recommendation.** Make the feed the single channel: ship.cmd (or a wrapper) publishes the signed record to the feed and the studio dashboard pulls it like any customer (policy stage/current), or at minimum ship.cmd runs publish_feed in the same invocation and publish_from_feed compares sha256 on 409 and reports a mismatch loudly.


### release-pipeline-3: ship.cmd is unaware of image mode: it restarts the live dashboard for nothing and cannot pass its health gate once the dashboard VERSION is bumped

**HIGH, inconsistency, effort small.**

**Evidence.** tools/ship.ps1:396-441 step 1 runs install_dashboard_app.py then polls /api/v1/health until version == repo $DashVersion (dashboard/__init__.py VERSION=0.7.4) and exits 1 otherwise; ship.ps1 contains no 'image'/'stack' handling (grep). server/install_dashboard_app.py:3471-3474 `push_code = not image_mode`; :4190-4235 in image mode still runs pull_container_image and `docker restart` of the live container and prints 'a restart keeps the container's CURRENT image'. tools/check_deploy_drift.ps1:451-459 already knows re-shipping trees 'would change nothing' in image mode. Memory/KNOWN_BUGS: the studio has run image mode since 2026-08-18.

**Consequence.** Every companion ship bounces the production dashboard (the thing that 'tells everyone whether their footage is syncing') without changing its code. If dashboard/ changed and VERSION was bumped, ship waits 90 s, fails 'investigate before continuing' and never builds or publishes the companion; the operator must first run build_dashboard_bundle + publish_feed + click APPLY, which no ship output tells them.

**Recommendation.** ship.ps1 reads [stack] mode from site.toml: in image mode skip the install_dashboard_app step entirely (or run bundle+publish_feed and wait for the OTA apply), never restart the container, and gate on 'live >= repo' with a clear 'dashboard is updated over the air' message.


### release-pipeline-4: The installer (kind=onboard) channel is never published through the CI/feed path, so feed-only customers have no installer

**HIGH, gap, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** tools/publish_latest.py:53-56 SOURCES lists companion only and --kind choices=['companion'] (:165); .github/workflows/release-windows.yml uploads onboarding/dist/onboard.exe with no ccsync-release.json for it; feed/channel.json has zero onboard records. dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/ui.py:1546-1560 /download/<platform> answers 404 'Publish one from the base rig: build_editor_package.ps1 -Publish' when no onboard package exists.

**Consequence.** A customer dashboard fed only from the vendor channel shows an empty INSTALLER page and its admin is told to run a vendor-internal PowerShell script. A new Mac or Windows editor cannot be onboarded at that site. Even on the studio, the installer only moves when ship.cmd runs locally (memory: 'onboard stuck at 1.0.34' while companion advanced via CI).

**Recommendation.** Have release-windows/macos emit a manifest for the onboard artefact, add onboard to publish_latest SOURCES (signed as kind=onboard with the installer version), and make the 404 text customer-facing.


### release-pipeline-5: The feed has no 'current' pointer and no retraction; stage/current policies replay the whole history in append order

**HIGH, fragility, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/release_feed.py:483-498 _apply_policy publishes EVERY verified record not already held and, under 'current', makes each one current in channel order; tools/publish_feed.py:213-220 upsert_record only ever appends or replaces in place, no remove/retract option in parse_args (:524-560); feed/channel.json now holds 18 records across 0.9.0..0.9.41 / 0.6.2..0.7.4.

**Consequence.** A fresh customer dashboard on policy 'current' downloads ~1 GB of historical artefacts on its first check and flips current several times; whichever record is LAST in the list wins, which is append order, not version order, so a --force republish of an older build or a late publish of an older macOS CI run appends after a newer one and offers the whole fleet a rollback. A bad build (e.g. the 0.6.1 proxy-generator shape) cannot be withdrawn from feed customers at all.

**Recommendation.** Add per (kind, platform) `current` to the channel and make _apply_policy publish/make-current only that record (plus max-version selection as a fallback); add `publish_feed.py --retract kind/platform/version`; cap what a new dashboard auto-publishes.


### release-pipeline-6: Same version, different bytes is accepted by the feed but refused by every dashboard that already holds the version

**MEDIUM, inconsistency, effort small.**

**Evidence.** tools/publish_feed.py:213-220 upsert_record replaces an existing (kind, platform, version) record unconditionally; tools/publish_latest.py --force (:169-172) exists for exactly that; dashboard release_feed.py:525-527 and api.py:3111-3122 both 409 on an existing version without comparing sha256 (R13 taught build_editor_package.ps1 to compare, KNOWN_BUGS.md:2004-2025).

**Consequence.** A CI re-run of the same version published with --force gives new customers one binary and existing customers another under the same version number; drift tooling, the Packages page and companion reports all speak in versions, so the split is invisible.

**Recommendation.** publish_feed refuses to replace a record whose sha256 differs unless --allow-replace, and prints the bump hint; publish_from_feed compares sha on 409 and surfaces 'same version, different bytes' in the feed view.


### release-pipeline-7: publish_latest.py signs whatever CI run is newest, with no tie to main, to HEAD, or to version monotonicity

**MEDIUM, fragility, effort small.**

**Evidence.** tools/publish_latest.py:107-115 latest_green_run uses `gh run list --workflow X --status success --limit 1` with no --branch filter and the headSha is only printed (:199-201); no comparison of the artifact's version against the newest version already in the channel; .github/workflows/release-windows.yml is workflow_dispatch on any ref.

**Consequence.** Anyone who dispatches release-windows on a feature branch (or an old commit) makes that artifact the next thing the rig signs and publishes to every customer; an older version re-published lands after the newer one in the channel (see release-pipeline-5).

**Recommendation.** Filter runs with --branch main, verify headSha is an ancestor of origin/main and matches the repo's companion VERSION, and refuse a version lower than the channel's newest for that platform.


### release-pipeline-8: The downgrade floor is never raised: every signed record in the feed carries min_version 0.0.0

**MEDIUM, gap, effort small.**

**Evidence.** feed/channel.json: all 18 records min_version 0.0.0. installer/build_editor_package.ps1:629 `$env:CCSYNC_MIN_VERSION` default empty; tools/release_macos.sh:764 `${CCSYNC_MIN_VERSION:-0.0.0}`; tools/publish_feed.py:531 default '0.0.0'; publish_latest passes --min-version only if typed. docs/RELEASE.md:681-683: 'Raise it whenever a release fixes something a downgrade would reintroduce' (CR-6/7/22/27/40/41 have shipped since).

**Consequence.** Any party able to answer as a dashboard (a customer's own container) can replay the genuinely signed 0.9.0 record and every companion accepts the rollback past CR-22/27/40/41, because the floor on each machine is still 0.0.0. The mechanism is built but has never had an input.

**Recommendation.** Move the floor into the repo as a tracked constant (e.g. config.py MIN_VERSION or a release.toml) read by all three signers so it is bumped with a commit, and make publish_feed refuse a record whose min_version is lower than the last published one for that kind/platform.


### release-pipeline-9: Dashboard OTA hinges on a mutable `:1` image tag the vendor has only tagged once, and a lock change silently disables OTA for every customer

**MEDIUM, fragility, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/dashboard_update.py:486-492 preflight 409s a bundle whose runtime_id differs from /venv/.runtime-id; runtime_id covers dashboard/deploy/requirements.lock + Dockerfile + BASE_IMAGE, which changed in 4 commits over 2 days (git log a39e295, 8158403, feb235d, b2d348a); image.yml pushes `:1` only on a `v*` tag and the only tag is v0.6.1 while code is 0.7.4; feed/channel.json dashboard_image is blank; publish_feed.py has no check that a dashboard bundle's runtime_id matches the image currently published as `:1`.

**Consequence.** The next dependency addition turns that dashboard release into a 'runtime update' for every customer: the APPLY button disappears with a 'click in your NAS UI' hint, but no v* tag was pushed so `:1` still resolves to the 0.6.1 image; the NAS re-pull changes nothing and customers stay on the old code until someone remembers to tag. RELEASE.md:121-127 documents the split rule but nothing enforces it.

**Recommendation.** publish_feed --kind dashboard warns/refuses when the bundle's runtime_id differs from the runtime_id of the image at dashboard_image.digest (or the `:1` manifest); make `git tag v<version>` + --set-image part of the dashboard release command; surface 'image behind bundle' on the vendor side, not only in the customer's UI.


### release-pipeline-10: publish_latest.py, the command actually used to ship, is documented nowhere

**LOW, gap, effort small.**

**Evidence.** grep for 'publish_latest' across docs/, CLAUDE.md and tools/*.ps1 returns nothing; docs/RELEASE.md:435-456 still describes the manual `gh run download` + publish_package.py path and :84-88 calls ship.cmd the one command; memory (release-tooling-publish-latest) records it as the real flow since 2026-08-19.

**Consequence.** A second operator following RELEASE.md reproduces the manual path that release-pipeline-2 shows already diverged, and misses the go/no-go checks publish_latest performs (platform, dirty, hash).

**Recommendation.** Add a RELEASE.md section and CLAUDE.md line: 'CI builds, publish_latest signs and publishes; ship.cmd is for the studio rig only' (or retire one of them).


### release-pipeline-11: Authenticode .pfx is designed to live in GitHub secrets while the release-key policy is 'CI builds, this rig signs'

**LOW, inconsistency, effort small.**

**Evidence.** .github/workflows/release-windows.yml 'materialise the signing certificate' decodes secrets.CCSYNC_SIGN_PFX_BASE64 and passes CCSYNC_SIGN_PFX_PASSWORD to release.ps1; docs/CI.md:275; tools/publish_latest.py docstring: 'A runner that could sign would mean anyone who compromised the repo ... could push code'.

**Consequence.** Once a certificate is bought, a repo or third-party-action compromise yields SmartScreen-trusted binaries carrying the product's identity (companions would still refuse them for lack of a release signature, but every fresh install and AV allowlist would not). The two signing decisions were made separately and contradict each other.

**Recommendation.** Decide deliberately before buying the cert: sign Authenticode on the rig (or via Azure Trusted Signing with OIDC and branch protection) and drop the PFX secret path from the workflow, or document why CI-held Authenticode is acceptable.


## Lens: Operational efficiency and failure modes: polling cadences, SQLite/WAL writers, subprocess spawning, editor-machine CPU/memory, container boot resilience, logging volume, timeouts and retries

This system has already absorbed several efficiency audits (AUDIT_2/AUDIT_3, COMP-CORE, SYNC-n) and it shows: the companion's background loops all block on events rather than spinning, the collector polls before it writes and backs off without ever speeding up, the Resolve watcher fingerprints the timeline before walking it, lane A has an event-driven express path so uploads do not wait for a pass, and the dashboard's SQLite contract (WAL, one uvicorn worker, the collector as sole bulk writer, a connection per thread) is written down and respected. The remaining problems are not hot loops; they are steady-state work that is repeated when nothing has changed, and a few places where one slow dependency serialises everything behind it.

The two biggest are (1) the heavy report: every machine re-sends its full local_manifest and media_tree every 60 s even though those caches refresh only every 300 s / 120 s, and the server answers by DELETE+INSERTing up to 4000 + 2000xN rows per machine per minute into a WAL database on a ZFS pool that is now being snapshotted hourly - while the report handler already has an "absent field means untouched" contract that would make change-only sending trivial; and (2) the sequencer pass: in steady state each ticked project costs three rclone processes (each with a 16-connection SSH pool), two recursive SFTP listings and three local walks every ~60 s, for a lane (B) that has no NAS-side change signal, then blocks up to 600 s in a lane C wait that no longer paces anything now that the pause scheme is off. Offline editors get the worst of it: no reachability probe means a laptop off the tailnet spends a whole pass spawning rclone processes that each sit in a 60 s contimeout x 3 retries. On the container side the single-threaded collector has no per-cycle deadline (a hung Syncthing parks enforce, connections and the health signal behind ~120 sequential 10 s-timeout calls), the healthcheck the compose file relies on to recover a dead collector cannot actually restart anything under plain Docker, and the access log plus htmx 2 s partial polls go to an unbounded json-file log. None of these is an outage today at one site with a handful of editors; all of them scale linearly with machines x projects, which is precisely the axis a second customer moves along.

**Strengths (do not break):**

- The collector's three structural rules are exactly right and documented where they matter: poll every Syncthing endpoint BEFORE the first write so no SQLite transaction spans network calls (collector.py:1052-1066), per-kind failure isolation with a backoff that only ever delays a kind (collector.py:186-196), and a DB-open retry inside the loop so a lock at boot cannot kill the only thread that runs retention (collector.py:163-177).
- The SQLite concurrency model is written down at the top of db.py (WAL, busy_timeout, one uvicorn worker, the collector as sole bulk writer, one connection per thread) and the code actually honours it; lane_report_history and completion_history only insert on state change / the anti-bloat rules (db.py:1464, :1320-1339), so the hot per-report path is upserts, not appends.
- The companion never busy-waits: every loop blocks on an Event for its whole timeout (sequencer._interruptible_wait/_wait_or_wake after AUDIT_2 L-18; reporter, manifest, media-tree, LUT, identity loops), and the reporter's heavy/light split plus the 7 MB payload budget with logged shedding keep the 5 s active cadence cheap.
- Lane A's express path (watchdog -> debounced per-file rclone with its own lock and partial suffix) decouples upload latency from the pass cadence, which is what makes ops-efficiency-2's longer idle safe to adopt.
- Syncthing pacing was moved from N(N-1) pause/unpause config commits per pass to maxFolderConcurrency (sequencer.py:593-609) - one of the best efficiency decisions in the tree; do not bring the rotate scheme back as the default.
- The Resolve watcher's poll cache (fingerprint by timeline id + per-track counts, full walk every 10th poll, resolve_bridge.py:636-684) and the per-path MISSING log dedupe (watcher.py:134-147) keep a 3 s poll from being a fusionscript and log-volume problem.
- Expensive remote listings are already amortised: structure clone every 10 passes, orphan/size-mismatch scans every 20 (sequencer.py:1147-1196), rclone probe cached (rclone_lane.py:540-545), selection fetch TTL'd on success AND failure (selection.py:171-206), trash prune at most every 6 h and never while the breaker is tripped.
- GPU/CPU arbitration on editor machines is explicit and one-directional: ingest blocks proxy generation, never the reverse; llama-server is stopped the moment the editor returns (broll_ingest.py:1551-1553, :1646); ffmpeg runs BELOW_NORMAL / niced; every idle probe treats 'cannot tell' as 'not idle'.
- The Syncthing supervisor rides lane C's existing 15 s poll (no extra thread), with a 30 s grace, capped exponential backoff and persisted strike counts, so a machine where the engine cannot start costs about six log lines an hour.
- Boot resilience on the container is careful: migrations commit per step so a restart mid-upgrade resumes, optional mounts (/broll, /music, /ytdl) fail absent rather than fatal, the release feed is the one documented redirect-following call and is justified, and the healthcheck reads the body so the 200-on-every-path contract used by ship.ps1 and the Mac wizard is preserved.


### ops-efficiency-1: Heavy report re-sends unchanged local_manifest/media_tree every 60 s and the server rewrites thousands of rows per machine per minute

**MEDIUM, inefficiency, effort small.**

**Evidence.** companion/src/ccsync_companion/reporter.py:729-742 (heavy payload every report_interval=60 s; only light ticks omit the sections); reporter.py:497-508 always attaches get_local_manifest()/get_media_tree() when not light; companion/src/ccsync_companion/manifest.py:211 (ManifestCache refresh 300 s) and app.py:878 (media_tree_refresh_interval 120 s) - so 4 of every 5 manifest sends and every other media_tree send are byte-identical to the previous one. dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:4379-4408 hands them to db.replace_editor_media (db.py:2925-2941: DELETE + INSERT up to MAX_PER_FILE_ENTRIES=2000 rows per selected project) and db.replace_media_tree (db.py:2943-2960: DELETE + INSERT up to MEDIA_TREE_CAP=4000 rows) unconditionally; the handler's own comment at api.py:4368 already states 'absent field => table untouched'. server/setup_snapshots.py keeps hourly snapshots of the apps dataset holding dashboard.db.

**Consequence.** For a fleet of M machines with P ticked projects each, the dashboard performs roughly M x (4000 + 2000P) deleted+inserted rows per minute with zero information gain - tens of MB/min of WAL on the NAS data pool, retained by every hourly ZFS snapshot, and a sustained write load that competes with the collector's write cycles under a 5 s busy_timeout (the documented failure shape is a 500 'database is locked' on an editor's report). On a slow tailnet the 30 s heavy-report timeout (reporter.py:229-231, payload budget 7 MB at line 114) is spent re-transmitting the same bytes.

**Recommendation.** Make the companion send local_manifest and media_tree only when the cached section's content hash changed since the last successful heavy post (plus a forced full resend every ~30 min so MEDIA_REPORT_MAX_AGE_DAYS pruning and a restarted dashboard still converge) - the server contract already treats an absent key as 'leave the table alone'. Optionally have the server compare a per-section hash stored on editor_media_project/machine_state before issuing the DELETE+INSERT. Both halves are additive and version-tolerant.


### ops-efficiency-2: Steady-state sequencer pass costs 3 rclone processes, 2 recursive SFTP listings and 3 local walks per project every ~60 s with nothing to transfer

**MEDIUM, inefficiency, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/sequencer.py:183-184 (sequencer_idle_seconds default 60) and :536; per project (:1079-1093) lanes A and B each spawn rclone (rclone_lane.py:2529-2560 run_once) - lane A `copy --ignore-existing` must list the remote project tree, lane B `sync` lists both sides; lane B additionally runs the breaker pre-flight `rclone lsf` (rclone_lane.py:2598-2604, list_remote_top at :1000-1030) and count_local_proxies (lane_guard.py:136-168, a full os.walk of the project) on EVERY pass. Each rclone process opens its own SSH pool: DEFAULT_SFTP_CONNECTIONS=16 (rclone_lane.py:166) with the comment that this exists to avoid tripping sshd MaxStartups 10:30:100. Lane A already has an event-driven express path (rclone_lane.py:3344-3365), so the pass cadence only matters for lane B, which has no NAS-side change signal. The dashboard already computes a per-project directory signature (collector.py:1010 _dir_signature -> nas_inventory_state.tree_sig) that is never handed to companions.

**Consequence.** Ten editors with ten ticked projects each generate ~300 rclone spawns and SSH key exchanges per pass (roughly every 100 s) against the NAS sshd, plus ~200 recursive SFTP directory listings, purely to discover nothing changed - and the proxy-tree walks on the editor SSD repeat every minute alongside the manifest (300 s) and proxy_gen (900 s) walks of the same directories. The NAS CPU, sshd MaxStartups budget and editor-side disk/CPU cost all scale with machines x projects, which is the axis a second customer grows along; a slow NAS makes each pass longer but not rarer.

**Recommendation.** Two complementary changes: (a) adaptive idle - when a full pass transferred nothing, back the idle interval off (60 -> 120 -> 300 -> 600 s) and reset to 60 s on any express event, selection change or tray action; (b) give lane B a change signal so it can skip a project outright: publish a per-project 'proxy tree version' (the collector's tree_sig or an mtime of Proxy/ directories) in the selection response, and have the sequencer run lane B + its lsf pre-flight only when that version differs from the last completed pass. Longer-term, a persistent `rclone rcd` with a reused SFTP session would remove the per-process handshake entirely.


### ops-efficiency-3: Lane C turn still blocks the whole sequencer for up to 600 s per project although the pause scheme that justified the wait is off

**MEDIUM, inconsistency, effort small.**

**Evidence.** sequencer.py:1338-1340 calls _wait_for_folder_sync unconditionally; :1568-1604 loops until needTotalItems==0 AND every remote device's needBytes==0 or project_rotation_seconds (600, :180-181) elapse. sequencer.py:593-609 and the comment at :1302-1316: with lane_c_pause_scheme='none' (the default) nothing is paused and Syncthing paces all folders itself via maxFolderConcurrency, so the wait no longer gates anything. _uploads_drained (:1546-1566) returns False while any remote device still needs bytes, i.e. while the NAS peer is slow or relayed.

**Consequence.** Tick a project with a large lane C backlog (first sync of an audio/GFX-heavy project, or a NAS peer riding a DERP relay at 1-5 MB/s as app.py:3966-3977 notes is the case today) and every pass stalls 10 minutes at that project; with N such projects, lane B proxies for the projects behind it arrive up to N x 600 s later than the 60 s idle cadence promises, and the tray/dashboard show the machine 'on' one project the entire time. Lane A is covered by express, lane B is not. A folder with a permanently non-zero need (a conflict, a deleted-but-still-ticked remote) turns this into a fixed 10-minute tax per pass forever.

**Recommendation.** When lane_c_pause_scheme == 'none', skip _wait_for_folder_sync (or cap it at a short settle such as 15-30 s so the dashboard's current_project still reads sensibly) and let Syncthing own lane C pacing; keep the full wait only for PAUSE_SCHEME_ROTATE, where it is load-bearing. Update the config.example.toml text for project_rotation_seconds (line 275-277) to say which scheme it applies to.


### ops-efficiency-4: No NAS-reachability short-circuit: an offline editor spends each pass spawning rclone processes that sit in contimeout x retries

**MEDIUM, fragility, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** rclone_lane.py:1284-1288 (_transport_flags: --timeout 5m, --contimeout 60s, --retries-sleep 10s; rclone's default --retries 3 is not overridden); the lane B pre-flight lsf has its own 60 s timeout (rclone_lane.py:1009); sequencer.py:1079-1093 runs lsf + lane A + lane B per project and :536 restarts the pass 60 s after it ends. Grep for any reachability gate (nas_reachable, socket.create_connection, backoff on consecutive failures) in sync/*.py and app.py finds none; transport_health (app.py:3966) is report-only diagnostics. The express path (rclone_lane.py:3344-3365) spawns on every debounce window regardless.

**Consequence.** A laptop off the tailnet (plane, hotel wifi blocking UDP, NAS powered down) with 10 ticked projects runs ~30 rclone processes per pass, each blackholed for up to 60 s x 3 attempts (+10 s sleeps), i.e. roughly 45 minutes of serial failing subprocesses painting lanes A/B red, then 60 s later starts again - and every file the editor saves meanwhile adds an express spawn. The tray's last_error is 'rclone exited 1' rather than 'NAS unreachable', so the editor cannot tell a broken lane from a missing network. Battery and CPU are spent for nothing; when the link returns the machine is mid-pass rather than reacting promptly.

**Recommendation.** Add a cheap pre-pass probe (TCP connect to the SFTP host:port with a 3 s timeout, or Tailscale's local status API) in the sequencer: on failure mark lanes A/B 'offline: cannot reach <host>' (a distinct state from error), skip the pass, and back off 1 -> 2 -> 5 -> 10 min with an immediate retry on a network-change/tray event; gate express on the same flag. Pass --retries 1 on the lsf/check helpers, which are probes rather than transfers.


### ops-efficiency-5: Collector is one thread with sequential Syncthing calls and no per-cycle deadline, so a slow Syncthing parks enforce, connections and the health signal

**MEDIUM, fragility, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** collector.py:157-212 (single loop runs every due kind in series); _run_completion :1052-1118 issues one db_status per folder plus one completion per (folder, device), each through SyncthingClient with timeout 10 s (syncthing_client.py:78-84); the method's own docstring counts '~120 sequential 10s-timeout requests'. nudge() (:128-136) only sets an event the loop cannot see while blocked. db.fetch_collector_status (db.py:3305-3316) flips syncthing_reachable off when the newest non-prune poll_run is older than COLLECTOR_STALE_SECONDS=180, and api_health uses that for `ok`.

**Consequence.** A Syncthing that hangs rather than refuses (pool import, a stuck folder scan, a GUI under load - all seen on NAS appliances) turns one completion cycle into up to 20 minutes; during it no tick is enforced (the 60 s promise the UI makes), connections stop refreshing (editors flip to offline/red per health.py's 15-minute rule), provision and inventory do not run, and /api/v1/health reports ok=false although the dashboard itself is fine. Lane A/B reports keep landing, so the grid half-updates, which is harder to diagnose than a clean outage.

**Recommendation.** Give each cycle a wall-clock budget (e.g. completion stops issuing new calls after 30 s and records a partial run), use a shorter timeout (2-3 s) for the per-pair completion calls, and run completion/remoteneed on their own thread and DB connection so enforce/connections/provision never queue behind them - the poll-then-write rule already makes the cycles independent at the DB level. Record 'partial' in poll_runs so the health panel can say why.


### ops-efficiency-6: The container healthcheck cannot restart anything under plain Docker, so the dead-collector case it was added for is still not self-healing

**LOW, gap, effort small.**

**Evidence.** dashboard/deploy/compose.yaml:479-500 - `restart: unless-stopped` plus a healthcheck, with the comment 'Without this, restart: unless-stopped only covers a process that EXITS. A collector thread that died ... leaves a container that answers nothing useful forever'. Docker's restart policies act only on process exit; health status is informational unless Swarm or a sidecar (autoheal) acts on it, and grep for autoheal/unhealthy in server/install_dashboard_app.py, the three compose files and docs finds nothing. run.sh:250-276 already has a relaunch loop keyed on exit code 75. The same `ok` also goes false whenever Syncthing is unreachable (api.py:874-878), so if an orchestrator DID restart on health it would restart-loop the dashboard through every Syncthing upgrade.

**Consequence.** DASH-2's failure (collector thread dead, container answering 200 forever) is now visible as 'unhealthy' in `docker ps` but is not recovered; an operator has to notice. Conversely, any future move to an orchestrator that honours health would take the dashboard - 'what tells everyone whether their footage is syncing' - down for the duration of every Syncthing outage.

**Recommendation.** Self-heal in-process: a tiny lifespan watchdog that, when the collector thread is not alive or poll_runs is stale beyond a few minutes, restarts the collector thread (or exits 75 so run.sh relaunches). Make `ok` mean 'this process is serving and its collector is alive', and expose syncthing_reachable as a separate field the UI renders. Then verify on the TrueNAS/Synology hosts what, if anything, acts on unhealthy, and document it in docs/SERVER.md.


### ops-efficiency-7: Dashboard container stdout is unbounded: access log on, 2 s htmx partial polls, no json-file size cap

**LOW, gap, effort small.**

**Evidence.** dashboard/deploy/run.sh:266 and :280 start uvicorn with no --no-access-log and app.py configures no log_config; no `logging:` block in compose.yaml / compose.image.yaml / compose.appliance.yaml and none emitted by server/install_dashboard_app.py compose_config (grep empty); DASH_LOG_DIR rotation is opt-in and off by default (crash_report.py:27). Request volume: every companion posts /api/v1/report every 5 s while any lane syncs (reporter.py:299-301), and each open dashboard tab polls /partials/transfers every 2 s (ui.py:192) and /partials/sidebar every 30 s (ui.py:161).

**Consequence.** Several access-log lines per second per active site accumulate in Docker's default json-file driver with no cap; over months on an appliance that nobody logs into, this eats the apps dataset (which is also snapshotted hourly) and makes `docker logs` useless for the one time someone needs it. Whether the host's daemon.json caps it depends on the NAS vendor, i.e. on exactly the thing the product no longer wants to assume.

**Recommendation.** Run uvicorn with --no-access-log (or an access-log filter that drops /api/v1/report, /partials/* and /api/v1/health), and add `logging: {driver: json-file, options: {max-size: '20m', max-file: '5'}}` to compose_config() and the template (the server tests already diff the two). Consider turning DASH_LOG_DIR rotation on by default in the appliance profile.


### ops-efficiency-8: Base rig media_tree refresh stats every pool clip over SMB every 120 s

**LOW, inefficiency, effort small.**

**Evidence.** app.py:2632-2649 (_refresh_media_tree_once: `present = self._exists_fn(file_path)` for every item returned by get_media_pool_items, then _relink_proxies_once and _classify_pool_once over the same list), driven by _media_tree_loop at app.py:2955-2966 on media_tree_refresh_interval=120 s. On a base rig local_root is the NAS share itself (SPEC.md 'Path canon': P: -> \\nas\...), so every exists() is an SMB round trip; MEDIA_TREE_CAP is 4000 (db.py:27).

**Consequence.** A 4000-clip project open on the base rig generates ~4000 SMB stat calls every two minutes for the life of the session, on the machine that also runs the indexers, and the resulting rows are then re-sent and re-written per ops-efficiency-1. On an editor SSD the cost is negligible; on SMB it is a steady background load and a latency spike each cycle.

**Recommendation.** Cache present/absent per file_path with the clip count as the invalidation key (re-probe only paths that were absent last time plus any new paths), or lengthen the interval when effective_mode() is 'base' - the base rig's presence data changes only when files land on the NAS, which the collector's inventory already tracks.


### ops-efficiency-9: Companion log window is 20 MB total, which a DEBUG-level incident can rotate through in under half an hour

**LOW, fragility, effort small.**

**Evidence.** app.py:87-88 RotatingFileHandler(maxBytes=5_000_000, backupCount=3). watcher.py:134-147 records the 2026-08-13 incident where per-clip DEBUG lines rotated 5 MB every ~25 minutes and 'rotated away the upgrade-history lines we were trying to read hours later' - that one flood was deduplicated, but the window itself was left as is.

**Consequence.** The next chatty code path (there are dozens of log.debug sites across lanes, sidecars and ingest) reproduces the same evidence loss: by the time an editor reports a problem the lines that explain it are gone, and remote diagnosis (the only kind available for Mac editors) fails.

**Recommendation.** Raise backupCount to ~10 (50 MB total is trivial on an editing SSD) and emit one INFO line on each rotation so a reader can see evidence was lost; optionally keep WARNING+ in a second, slower-rotating file so upgrade/breaker/halt history always survives.


## Lens: Product surface and maintainability: docs vs code drift, feature-flag plumbing, site-manifest/brand abstraction for a second customer, UI copy rules and scan tests, test-suite honesty, duplication and dead code

The product surface is in better shape than most repos its age: the em-dash rule is enforced by six scan tests and a byte-scan of installers, onboarding, legal text and resources finds zero stray U+2014 outside the tests' scope; brand literals have been pushed into the site manifest with explicit, warned-about legacy fallbacks; ~70 cross-component parity tests in server/tests/test_cross_component.py pin every vendored copy (identity verifiers, stignore builders, VLM/CLAP modules) byte-for-byte; and the test conftests document every bypass they install. Where the design has drifted is in the site manifest itself. Since WP D (2026-08-17/18) the manifest has TWO sources: `DASH_SITE_*` environment (written by server/install_dashboard_app.py from site.toml) and the `site_settings` table (written by the Setup wizard, the Settings page and TOML import). `site_store.resolved_manifest` merges them DB-first and publishes that to every companion via /api/v1/site, but the dashboard's OWN internals still read only the environment: the /ytdl mount gate (`ytdl.py:257`), the topbar brand (`ui.py:119-122`), the ASGI title (`app.py:353`), the create-project template (`api.py:1932` via `provision.TEMPLATE_FOLDERS`, read once at import from env), the enforce cycle's shared-asset list (`collector.py:407`) and the storage checks in setup_engine. In image/appliance mode, where compose.appliance.yaml sets no `DASH_SITE_*` at all, everything the wizard's "Your studio" step asks for is stored, published to the fleet, and ignored by the dashboard that asked for it.

The second theme is docs that describe a state the code has left behind: APPLIANCE_INSTALL.md (the customer install doc) says the first-run wizard "does not exist yet" on the day it landed; CONFIG.md still says the drive letter is "hardcoded by decision"; TREE_LAYOUT_AGNOSTICISM.md claims `template_folders` reaches the create flow "from one place" (true only on the env path); LOOPBACK_API.md's route table omits `POST /ytdl/fetch`; docs/README.md's index misses three docs. First-customer data still lives in the deploy defaults (`BROLL_CREATORS_SHARES` = "mofa-disaster" in install_dashboard_app.py and both compose templates; `BROLL_ARCHIVE_CREATORS_DIR` documented in CONFIG.md but plumbed from nowhere). Test-suite honesty is good and explicit: the dashboard suite runs under DASH_DEV_INSECURE=1 with three files that deliberately turn it off; the companion suite runs every test with the EULA accepted and YouTube features ON via autouse fixtures, with the gate tests opting out; a handful of tests are pinned to this base rig (E:\Projects\music-tagger venv, P:\Assets\Music) and skip honestly elsewhere. Duplication is real (identity.py x3, ytdl_common, routes_*, ed25519.py, crash_report.py across components) but the vendored copies are gated; only crash_report.py is a same-named, different-content pair with no gate, which is by design but easy to mistake.

**Strengths (do not break):**

- Em-dash rule is genuinely enforced: six AST/template scan tests (companion, dashboard, broll/web, music/web, ytdl/web, onboarding) each with a self-check that the scan covers something and would catch a regression; an independent byte-scan of installer/*.ps1, *.sh, onboarding, docs/legal and companion resources found zero U+2014 outside the declared exemption (broll_vlm/compact_format.py _NONE_WORDS).
- Cross-component parity is tested rather than hoped for: server/tests/test_cross_component.py pins ~70 invariants (identity verifiers x3 agree, stignore builders agree, vendored VLM/CLAP modules byte-identical, release gates pin every vendored pair, version rankers agree, ledger rows written the same way by both executors).
- Conftest bypasses are explicit, documented and opt-out-able: dashboard's DASH_DEV_INSECURE=1 is set once at import with test_sessions/test_oidc/test_local_auth_mode deliberately deleting it; the companion's autouse fixtures (EULA accepted, YouTube feature on, unbranded, no live Resolve, no real Tk windows) each say why and name the tests that opt out; AI provider keys are scrubbed from the environment so a developer's shell cannot make a test pass.
- Brand abstraction is mostly complete with honest legacy fallbacks: `HOME_PARENT`, `get_archive_creators_dir`, `LEGACY_COLLECTION_CREATORS`, the com.creatorsclub.* launchd retirement, and the broll collection slug all keep the first fleet working while logging when the fallback is relied on.
- Feature flags fail closed end to end on the companion side: FEATURE_KEYS whitelist in site.normalise, `feature_enabled` returns False for anything unknown or unreadable, and the dashboard publishes youtube_unblock only when youtube_download is also on (api.py:1064).
- Optional mounts are tri-state and best-effort (broll/music/ytdl: MOUNTED/ABSENT/DEGRADED), only advertised in the nav when fully working, and cannot stop the dashboard booting.
- Dead-code hygiene is good: pystray is gone from the dependency set and guarded by tools/check_licenses.py; the CCSYNC_TRAY_BACKEND hatch is inert when frozen (tray.py:58-69); no orphan modules found in dashboard/src.
- docs/TREE_LAYOUT_AGNOSTICISM.md §4.4 is candid that the published layout keys have no companion consumer yet; the repo's habit of dating decisions and citing bug ids in comments makes drift findable.


### product-surface-1: Feature flag `youtube_download` has two sources of truth: Settings/DB publishes it to companions, but the /ytdl mount reads only the environment

**HIGH, inconsistency, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** dashboard/templates/admin_settings.html:59-62 offers a 'YouTube downloader' checkbox that writes `features.youtube_download` into site_settings via site_store.set_many; dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:1058-1065 publishes `site["features"]["youtube_download"]` from site_store.resolved_manifest (DB-first, site_store.py:372-376); but dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/ytdl.py:257 gates the mount on `settings.site_feature_youtube_download`, which is only `DASH_SITE_YOUTUBE_DOWNLOAD == "1"` (settings.py:587), evaluated once at create_app (app.py:800). ai_providers.cli_enabled (ai_providers.py:386-395) shows the intended DB-then-env precedence that ytdl.py does not follow. dashboard/deploy/compose.appliance.yaml sets no DASH_SITE_* at all. dashboard/tests/test_ytdl_mount.py:672-702 pins the env-only behaviour, so the suite enshrines the split.

**Consequence.** An admin ticks 'YouTube downloader' on Settings (or imports a site.toml with `youtube_download = true` via /api/v1/admin/site/import, the only path in an appliance). /api/v1/site now says `youtube_download: true`; every companion un-hides its YouTube tray items and starts accepting /ytdl/* loopback calls (companion ytdl_server.py:267, ytdlp_manager.py:263); the dashboard nav never shows /ytdl and every /ytdl and fleet route 404s, even after a container restart. Conversely an env-on / DB-off site (DB row written '0' after seed) mounts /ytdl while telling the fleet the feature is off. No UI text or doc says the checkbox needs an env change plus a redeploy.

**Recommendation.** Make one predicate (site_store.resolved_manifest or a `site_store.feature_enabled(conn, settings, name)` with cli_enabled's precedence) the only reader of a feature flag, and have mount_ytdl take it. Because the mount happens at boot, either mount /ytdl unconditionally behind a per-request gate that checks the DB (cheap; the table is tiny) or mark the flag as 'restart required' in the Settings UI and add a boot-time read of the DB. Add a test: set the DB row to 1 with env unset, build the app, assert /ytdl is mounted.


### product-surface-2: The wizard/Settings site manifest is stored and published but the dashboard's own brand, create-project template and asset-sharing still read the environment snapshot

**HIGH, inconsistency, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** setup_engine.py:436-450 makes `org_name, tree_name, canonical_prefix, template_folders` REQUIRED wizard answers, read from site_store. Yet: ui.py:119-122 sets `brand_org`/`brand_product` from `settings.site_org_short/site_org_name/site_product_name` (env only); app.py:353 builds the ASGI title from the same; api.py:1932 creates project subfolders from `provision.TEMPLATE_FOLDERS`, which provision.py:72 computes ONCE at import from `DASH_SITE_TEMPLATE_FOLDERS` falling back to the first customer's documentary-shop list (provision.py:62-71: AE, Interviewees, Render in Place, Youtube...); ui.py:600 previews that same env list on /project-setup; collector.py:407 and setup_engine.py:485,528 iterate `provision.SHARED_ASSET_FOLDERS` (provision.py:200, env at import) for what the enforce cycle shares fleet-wide. site_store.seed_from_env_once (site_store.py:249-306) explicitly logs 'the database is now authoritative ... a later env change will not be picked up', and nothing ever copies the DB back into provision/settings (no reassignment of provision.TEMPLATE_FOLDERS anywhere in dashboard/src). compose.appliance.yaml carries no DASH_SITE_* variables.

**Consequence.** Appliance/second-customer path: the admin completes 'Your studio' with org_name='Northlight', template folders 'Footage, Audio, Graphics', and adds `Assets/Fonts` to shared asset folders. Companions receive all of that from /api/v1/site. The dashboard itself keeps a 'CC SYNC' topbar, creates every new project with AE/Interviewees/Render in Place/Subs/Youtube, and the enforce cycle never shares Assets/Fonts with anyone while Assets/Luts (the default) is shared. The Settings page shows the DB values, so the admin sees their edits 'saved' with no effect. CLAUDE.md ('Brand strings come from the site manifest ... the dashboard's through ui._render's brand_org') and TREE_LAYOUT_AGNOSTICISM.md:97 ('`[tree] template_folders` reaches ... the dashboard create flow and /api/v1/site from one place') are only true on the site.toml->env deploy path.

**Recommendation.** Decide one direction and finish it: either (a) at boot, after seed_from_env_once, overlay the resolved manifest onto `Settings` and recompute provision.TEMPLATE_FOLDERS / SHARED_ASSET_FOLDERS from it (and mark Settings fields that need a restart), or (b) make provision's lists and _render's brand read `site_store.resolved_manifest(conn, settings)` per request/cycle. Add a cross-check test: write org_name/template_folders to the DB with no env, render `/` and create a project, assert both reflect the DB. Until then, have the Settings page say which fields the dashboard itself does not yet honour.


### product-surface-3: First-customer data survives in vendor deploy defaults: BROLL_CREATORS_SHARES defaults to 'mofa-disaster'; BROLL_ARCHIVE_CREATORS_DIR is documented but plumbed from nowhere

**MEDIUM, gap, effort small.**

**Evidence.** server/install_dashboard_app.py:4257-4258 `broll_creators_shares=os.environ.get("BROLL_CREATORS_SHARES", "mofa-disaster")`; dashboard/deploy/compose.yaml:145 and compose.image.yaml:125 hardcode `BROLL_CREATORS_SHARES: "mofa-disaster"`; site.example.toml:216-232 `[broll]` has only default_collection/default_collection_label, no shares key, so a second site cannot set it in the manifest at all. docs/CONFIG.md:443 documents `BROLL_ARCHIVE_CREATORS_DIR` (default `Creators_Club`) but the only reader is broll/web/app/config.py:186-194; no compose template, install_dashboard_app.py branch or site.toml key sets it (grep over server/, dashboard/deploy/, site.example.toml finds nothing). CLAUDE.md: 'No customer's name in code.'

**Consequence.** A second customer's deploy ships with an own-footage share list naming the first customer's shoot, and every clip their editors ingest through the b-roll drag-and-drop lands under `<archive>/Creators_Club/<shoot>/` (ingest_batches.py:209) with no supported way to change it short of editing a rendered compose file that the next deploy re-renders. Harmless for search (no share named mofa-disaster), wrong for the folder tree editors browse and the SMB paths they see.

**Recommendation.** Add `[broll] creators_shares` and `[broll] archive_creators_dir` to site.example.toml, project both into the compose env in install_dashboard_app.py (and the appliance compose via the wizard), default `creators_shares` to empty, and move 'mofa-disaster' into the studio's git-ignored site.toml. A test in server/tests that renders the compose with an empty site and asserts no customer literal appears would keep it out.


### product-surface-4: docs/APPLIANCE_INSTALL.md tells the customer the first-run wizard does not exist; it shipped the same day

**MEDIUM, inconsistency, effort small.**

**Evidence.** docs/APPLIANCE_INSTALL.md:65-70 'NOT YET TRUE: there is no first-run wizard at this URL today ... the SetupEngine wizard (ZERO_TOUCH_PLAN.md 3.5, WP D) does not exist yet' and :145-147 'the wizard that would create them (WP D) does not exist yet'; file last touched 2026-08-17 22:56. Commit 751b86c 'WP D: SetupEngine + wizard, site manifest moves into the DB' and a9c3116 (2026-08-18) landed dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/setup_engine.py (12 registered tasks), setup_routes.py and dashboard/templates/setup.html, served at /setup (ui.py:1772-1788). ZERO_TOUCH_PLAN.md:324-337 records WP D as landed.

**Consequence.** The one document a customer follows to install the appliance instructs them to skip the setup step and pre-create the tree by hand, and says the storage step that creates Projects/Assets does not exist, when the wizard's storage task now does exactly that. Support calls and a half-configured site.

**Recommendation.** Rewrite the 'NOT YET TRUE' blocks to describe /setup as it is (first-run anonymous window, the studio/storage/admin tasks, what still needs the NAS shell), and add APPLIANCE_INSTALL.md, MULTI_BASE_RIG_PLAN.md and BACKCATALOGUE_INGEST.md to the docs/README.md index, which currently omits all three.


### product-surface-5: Settings UI exposes only two of the four feature flags; auto_update and ai_cli_providers can only be set by TOML import or env

**LOW, gap, effort small.**

**Evidence.** dashboard/templates/admin_settings.html:59-62 renders checkboxes for features.youtube_download and features.youtube_unblock only; site_store.KEYS (site_store.py:79-92) accepts all four; docs/CONFIG.md:97-99 describes auto_update as a site feature an admin turns on; the only non-env write path for it is the 'paste a site.toml' textarea (admin_settings.html:137-142 -> setup_routes.py:229-240). ai_cli_providers is set by the CLI wizard (cli_tools.py), which is intentional, but auto_update has no control at all.

**Consequence.** An appliance admin who wants unattended companion updates has to know to paste `[features]\nauto_update = true` into the migration textarea; nothing on the page suggests it. An admin who wants to turn it OFF again has the same problem in reverse.

**Recommendation.** Add the auto_update checkbox beside the YouTube ones (with its one-line consequence), and render ai_cli_providers read-only with a link to the AI providers page so the page shows every flag the manifest carries.


### product-surface-6: Doc statements that are now false or stale

**LOW, inconsistency, effort small.**

**Evidence.** docs/CONFIG.md:91 `canonical_prefix` ... 'Hardcoded by decision (2026-07-26)' vs CLAUDE.md (2026-08-17: 'The editor tree root is site data, not code') and site_store.py:127 validator. SPEC.md:79 describes the dashboard as 'tailnet-only, no login' in the same section that later describes editors logging in (SPEC.md ~line 100+) and app.py's login_gate; SPEC.md:17-18 'Current state (verified)' still says Tailscale is logged out and Syncthing not installed. docs/LOOPBACK_API.md:13 route table lists the ytdl group as reveal/capabilities/download/progress but companion/src/ccsync_companion/broll_server.py:36-37 also serves `POST /ytdl/fetch` (CR-32, 2026-08-19), which the doc never names. docs/TREE_LAYOUT_AGNOSTICISM.md:97 (see product-surface-2).

**Consequence.** A second operator reading CONFIG.md believes the drive letter cannot be changed; an integrator reading LOOPBACK_API.md cannot discover the fetch endpoint the ytdl page depends on; SPEC.md's in-place historical text reads as current state.

**Recommendation.** Fix the CONFIG.md cell, add `/ytdl/fetch` to the LOOPBACK_API.md table, and either date-stamp SPEC.md's 'Current state'/'no login' paragraphs as history or delete them.


### product-surface-7: Tests pinned to this base rig's filesystem skip honestly but leave the gate they guard un-run anywhere else

**LOW, fragility, effort medium.**

**Evidence.** companion/tests/test_music_clap_sidecar.py:548-556 `TORCH_VENV = Path(r"E:\Projects\music-tagger\.venv\...")`, `LIBRARY = Path(r"P:\Assets\Music")`, skipif unless both exist: the cosine>=0.999 numeric gate for the exported CLAP artefact only ever runs on one machine. tools/run_all_tests.ps1:74 hardcodes `E:\Projects\broll-platform\web\.venv` for the broll/web suite (fallback documented in CLAUDE.md). companion/tests/test_canon.py:141-218 skip the drive-rooted local_root cases off Windows (CI does run windows-latest, ci.yml:57, so these are covered).

**Consequence.** A regression in the exported CLAP artefact or its loader passes CI and every other developer's machine; the first failure is an editor's music search returning nonsense.

**Recommendation.** Either check a tiny fixture (a few seconds of audio plus the reference embedding) into the repo so the numeric gate runs everywhere with the in-repo ONNX/torch-free path, or make the skip loud in run_all_tests' summary ('SKIPPED: artefact gate') rather than a silent pytest skip.


### product-surface-8: Same-named modules with different contents across components (crash_report.py) sit beside byte-identical vendored copies, with no marker distinguishing the two kinds

**LOW, complexity, effort small.**

**Evidence.** Duplicate basenames across component src dirs: identity.py, ytdl_common.py, ingest_batches.py, routes_api/batches/fleet/ingest/media.py, fleet_auth.py, search.py, config.py, ed25519.py, crash_report.py. The vendored pairs are gated (server/tests/test_cross_component.py:437,486,527,604,618,664,678). dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/crash_report.py and companion/src/ccsync_companion/crash_report.py differ from line 1 (`diff` shows two different designs) and are not under any parity test; ed25519.py is identical in dashboard and companion but only implicitly pinned via test_upgrade/test_packages.

**Consequence.** A maintainer who learns 'same filename across components means vendored copy, the gate will catch drift' will edit dashboard's crash_report.py expecting the companion's to follow, or vice versa; nothing says which pairs are intentionally separate.

**Recommendation.** Add a one-line header convention ('VENDORED COPY OF <path>: edit the source' vs 'SAME NAME, DIFFERENT MODULE: not a copy') and a cross_component test that asserts every duplicate basename is either byte-identical-and-gated or carries the not-a-copy header. Add ed25519.py to the explicit byte-identity gate.


---

## Plausible (not fully traced)

- **comp-lane-c-5** (low) Supervisor's 20 s API wait runs on whatever thread calls check_once, including the tray's diagnostics callback, with no lock around the launch decision (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/syncthing_supervisor.py:643`): Half refuted. The tray does not run diagnostics on its UI thread: tray.py:2474 uses _spawn, which starts a daemon worker thread (tray.py:1696-1700), so the menu is never blocked; the 20 s _wait_for_api only parks that worker (Copy diagnostics takes up to 20 s to reach the clipboard, a minor annoyance). The double-launch race is technically present -- _note_unreachable (supervisor.py:526-592) reads since/attempts/last_attempt under the lock, releases it, and _attempt_start re-takes it to bump attempts/last_attempt -- but the unguarded window is the few instructions between releasing the lock and re-acquiring it, so a poll-thread tick and a diagnostics click would have to land within microseconds of each other; and even then the second `syncthing serve` on the same home exits on the DB/port lock, costing one spurious 'start attempt N' log line and an attempts counter off by one (which the next _note_reachable resets). Not practically reachable by an editor; kept PLAUSIBLE rather than REFUTED only because the lock-gap genuinely exists.
- **server-3** (medium) Fresh TrueNAS install leaves <tree>/Assets root:root 0755, so Syncthing (uid 3000) cannot create the LUT/Stills folders and editors cannot write under Assets (`server/install_dashboard_app.py:3992`): Confirmed in code: install_dashboard_app.py:3992 and :4028 `mkdir -p` the archive and Music leaves and chown/chmod only those leaves; grep finds no other creator of `<tree>/Assets` anywhere in server/*.py, docs/INSTALL.md, docs/SERVER.md or server/README.md (only common.py:321 names the constant); setup_tree's set_tree_acl (truenas.py:797-820) chowns only the project base and container dirs under Projects/. The collector (collector.py:405-418) adds Syncthing folders at <prefix>/Luts and <prefix>/Stills and relies on the Syncthing uid to mkdir them. So on a fresh tree Assets/ is created root:root with root's umask. Downgraded to PLAUSIBLE only because the real outcome depends on the dataset's ACL inheritance / aclmode (a TrueNAS dataset with an inheriting NFSv4 ACL would mask this), and on this fleet Assets pre-existed, so no live reproduction exists. Severity medium stands for a second customer following INSTALL.md.

No finding was refuted in this hunt. Refuted items from earlier hunts stay archived in their own documents.
